| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Goneril]] |
| LEAR | Who stocked my servant? [[Regan]], I have good [[Hope\|hope]]<br>Thou didst not know on’t. Who comes here? O heavens,<br>If you do [[Love\|love]] old men, if your sweet sway<br>Allow obedience, if you yourselves are old,<br>Make it your cause! Send down and take my part.<br>(To [[Goneril]]) Art not ashamed to look upon this beard?<br>O [[Regan]], will you take her by the hand? |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why not by th’ hand, sir? How have I offended?<br>All’s not offence that indiscretion finds<br>And dotage terms so. |
| LEAR | O sides, you are too tough!<br>Will you yet hold?—How came my man i’th’ stocks? |
| CORNWALL | I set him there, sir; but his own disorders<br>Deserved much less advancement. |
| LEAR | You? Did you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, father, being weak, seem so.<br>If till the expiration of your month<br>You will return and sojourn with my sister,<br>Dismissing half your train, come then to me.<br>I am now from [[Home\|home]], and out of that provision<br>Which shall be needful for your entertainment. |
| LEAR | Return to her, and fifty men dismissed?<br>No, rather I abjure all roofs, and choose<br>To be a comrade with the wolf and owl,<br>To wage against the enmity o’th’ air<br>Necessity’s sharp pinch. Return with her?<br>Why, the hot-blooded France, that dowerless took<br>Our youngest born—I could as [[Well\|well]] be brought<br>To knee his throne and, squire-like, pension beg<br>To keep base life afoot. Return with her?<br>Persuade me rather to be slave and sumpter<br>To this detested groom. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | At your choice, sir. |
| LEAR | I prithee, daughter, do not make me mad.<br>I will not trouble thee, my [[Child\|child]]. Farewell.<br>We’ll no more meet, no more see one another.<br>But yet thou art my flesh, my [[Blood\|blood]], my daughter—<br>Or rather a disease that’s in my flesh,<br>Which I must needs call mine. Thou art a boil,<br>A plague-sore or embossed carbuncle<br>In my corrupted [[Blood\|blood]]. But I’ll not chide thee.<br>Let shame come when it will, I do not call it.<br>I do not bid the thunder-bearer shoot,<br>Nor tell tales of thee to high-judging Jove.<br>Mend when thou canst; be better at thy leisure.<br>I can be patient, I can stay with [[Regan]],<br>I and my hundred knights. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Not altogether so.<br>I looked not for you yet, nor am provided<br>For your fit welcome. Give ear, sir, to my sister;<br>For those that mingle reason with your passion<br>Must be content to think you old, and so—<br>But she knows what she does. |
| LEAR | Is this [[Well\|well]] spoken? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I dare avouch it, sir. What, fifty followers?<br>Is it not [[Well\|well]]? What should you need of more,<br>Yea, or so many, sith that both charge and danger<br>Speak ‘gainst so great a number? How in one [[house]]<br>Should many people under two commands<br>Hold amity? ’Tis hard, almost impossible. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why might not you, my lord, receive attendance<br>From those that she calls servants, or from mine? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why not, my lord? If then they chanced to slack ye,<br>We could control them. If you will come to me—<br>For now I spy a danger—I entreat you<br>To bring but five-and-twenty; to no more<br>Will I give place or notice. |
| LEAR | I gave you all. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And in good [[Time\|time]] you gave it. |
| LEAR | Made you my guardians, my depositaries,<br>But kept a reservation to be followed<br>With such a number. What, must I come to you<br>With five-and-twenty? [[Regan]], said you so? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And speak’t again, my lord. No more with me. |
| LEAR | Those wicked creatures yet do look [[Well\|well]] favoured<br>When others are more wicked. Not being the worst<br>Stands in some rank of praise. (To [[Goneril]]) I’ll go with<br>thee.<br>Thy fifty yet doth [[Double\|double]] five-and-twenty,<br>And thou art twice her [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Hear me, my lord.<br>What need you five-and-twenty, ten, or five,<br>To follow in a [[house]] where twice so many<br>Have a command to tend you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What need one? |
| LEAR | O, reason not the need! Our basest beggars<br>Are in the poorest thing superfluous.<br>Allow not [[Nature\|nature]] more than [[Nature\|nature]] needs,<br>Man’s life is cheap as beast’s. Thou art a lady.<br>If only to go warm were gorgeous,<br>Why, [[Nature\|nature]] needs not what thou, gorgeous, wear’st,<br>Which scarcely keeps thee warm. But for true need—<br>You heavens, give me that patience, patience I need.<br>You see me here, you gods, a poor old man,<br>As full of grief as age, wretched in both.<br>If it be you that stirs these daughters’ hearts<br>Against their father, [[Fool\|fool]] me not so much<br>To bear it tamely. Touch me with noble anger,<br>And let not women’s weapons, [[Water\|water]]-drops,<br>Stain my man’s cheeks. No, you unnatural hags,<br>I will have such revenges on you both<br>That all the world shall—I will do such things—<br>What they are, yet I know not; but they shall be<br>The terrors of the earth. You think I’ll weep.<br>No, I’ll not weep. I have full cause of weeping, |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] and tempest |
| LEAR | But this heart shall break into a hundred thousand<br>flaws<br>Or ere I’ll weep.—O [[Fool]], I shall go mad! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear, [[Fool]], Gentleman, and [[Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | Let us withdraw. ’Twill be a [[Storm\|storm]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | This [[house]] is little. The old man and ’s people<br>Cannot be [[Well\|well]] bestowed. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | ’Tis his own blame;<br>Hath put himself from rest, and must needs taste his folly. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | For his particular I’ll receive him gladly,<br>But not one follower. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | So am I purposed.<br>Where is my lord of [[Gloucester]]? |
| CORNWALL | Followed the old man forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | He is returned. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is in high rage. |
| CORNWALL | Whither is he going? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He calls to [[Horse\|horse]], but will I know not whither. |
| CORNWALL | ’Tis best to give him way. He leads himself. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>My lord, entreat him by no means to stay. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, the [[Night\|night]] comes on, and the high winds<br>Do sorely ruffle. For many miles about<br>There’s scarce a bush. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, to wilful men<br>The injuries that they themselves procure<br>Must be their schoolmasters. Shut up your doors.<br>He is attended with a desperate train,<br>And what they may incense him to, being apt<br>To have his ear abused, wisdom bids [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| CORNWALL | Shut up your doors, my lord. ‘Tis a wild [[Night\|night]].<br>My [[Regan]] counsels [[Well\|well]]. Come out o’th’ [[Storm\|storm]]. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still. Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised and the First Gentleman, severally |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Who’s there, besides foul weather? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | One minded like the weather,<br>Most unquietly. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I know you. Where’s the [[King]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Contending with the fretful elements;<br>Bids the [[Wind\|wind]] blow the earth into the sea<br>Or swell the curled waters ’bove the main,<br>That things might change or cease. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | But who is with him? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None but the [[Fool]], who labours to outjest<br>His heart-struck injuries. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, I do know you,<br>And dare upon the warrant of my note<br>Commend a dear thing to you. There is division,<br>Although as yet the face of it is covered<br>With mutual cunning, ’twixt Albany and Cornwall,<br>Who have—as who have not that their great [[Stars\|stars]]<br>Throned and set high—servants, who seem no less,<br>Which are to France the spies and speculations<br>Intelligent of our state. What hath been seen,<br>Either in snuffs and packings of the Dukes,<br>Or the hard rein which both of them hath borne<br>Against the old kind [[King]]; or something deeper,<br>Whereof perchance these are but furnishings— |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I will talk further with you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, do not.<br>For confirmation that I am much more<br>Than my out-wall, open this purse, and take<br>What it contains. If you shall see [[Cordelia]]—<br>As [[Fear\|fear]] not but you shall—show her this ring<br>And she will tell you who that fellow is<br>That yet you do not know. Fie on this [[Storm\|storm]]!<br>I will go seek the [[King]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Give me your hand. Have you no more to say? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Few words, but to effect more than all yet:<br>That when we have found the [[King]]—in which your<br>[[Pain\|pain]]<br>That way, I’ll this—he that first lights on him<br>Holla the other. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 3, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still. Enter [[King Lear]] and his [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | Blow, winds, and crack your cheeks! Rage, blow,<br>You cataracts and hurricanoes, spout<br>Till you have drenched our steeples, drowned the<br>cocks!<br>You sulph‘rous and thought-executing fires,<br>Vaunt-couriers of oak-cleaving thunderbolts,<br>Singe my white head; and thou all-shaking thunder,<br>Strike flat the thick rotundity o’th’ world,<br>Crack [[Nature\|nature]]’s moulds, all germens spill at once<br>That makes ingrateful man. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | O nuncle, [[Court\|court]] holy [[Water\|water]] in a dry [[house]] is better than this rain-[[Water\|water]] out o’ door. Good nuncle, in, ask thy daughters blessing. Here’s a [[Night\|night]] pities neither wise men nor fools. |
| LEAR | Rumble thy bellyful; spit, [[Fire\|fire]]; spout, rain.<br>Nor rain, [[Wind\|wind]], thunder, [[Fire\|fire]] are my daughters.<br>I tax not you, you elements, with unkindness.<br>I never gave you kingdom, called you children.<br>You owe me no subscription. Then let fall<br>Your horrible pleasure. Here I stand your slave,<br>A poor, infirm, weak and despised old man,<br>But yet I call you servile ministers,<br>That will with two pernicious daughters join<br>Your high-engendered battles ‘gainst a head<br>So old and white as this. O, ho, ’tis foul! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | He that has a [[house]] to put ’s head in has a good head-piece. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | The codpiece that will [[house]]<br>Before the head has any,<br>The head and he shall louse,<br>So beggars marry many.<br>The man that makes his toe<br>What he his heart should make<br>Shall of a corn cry woe,<br>And turn his [[Sleep\|sleep]] to wake—<br>for there was never yet fair woman but she made mouths in a glass. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised |
| LEAR | No, I will be the pattern of all patience.<br>I will say nothing. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Who’s there? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Marry, here’s grace and a codpiece—that’s a wise man and a [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>Alas, sir, are you here? Things that [[Love\|love]] [[Night\|night]]<br>[[Love]] not such nights as these. The wrathful skies<br>Gallow the very wanderers of the dark<br>And make them keep their caves. Since I was man<br>Such sheets of [[Fire\|fire]], such bursts of horrid thunder,<br>Such groans of roaring [[Wind\|wind]] and rain I never<br>Remember to have heard. Man’s [[Nature\|nature]] cannot carry<br>Th’affliction nor the [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| LEAR | Let the great gods,<br>That keep this dreadful pother o’er our heads,<br>Find out their enemies now. Tremble, thou wretch<br>That hast within thee undivulgèd crimes<br>Unwhipped of justice; hide thee, thou bloody hand,<br>Thou perjured and thou simular of virtue<br>That art incestuous; caitiff, to pieces shake,<br>That under covert and convenient seeming<br>Has practised on man’s life; close pent-up guilts,<br>Rive your concealing continents and cry<br>These dreadful summoners grace. I am a man<br>More sinned against than sinning. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Alack, bare-headed?<br>Gracious my lord, hard by here is a hovel.<br>Some [[Friendship\|friendship]] will it lend you ‘gainst the tempest.<br>Repose you there while I to this hard [[house]]—<br>More harder than the stones whereof ’tis raised,<br>Which even but now, demanding after you,<br>Denied me to come in—return and force<br>Their scanted courtesy. |
| LEAR | My wits begin to turn.<br>(To [[Fool]]) Come on, my boy. How dost, my boy? Art<br>cold?<br>I am cold myself.—Where is this straw, my fellow?<br>The art of our necessities is strange,<br>And can make vile things precious. Come, your hovel.—<br>Poor [[Fool\|fool]] and knave, I have one part in my heart<br>That’s sorry yet for thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Fool\|FOOL]] Sings |
| LEAR | He that has and a little tiny wit,<br>With heigh-ho, the [[Wind\|wind]] and the rain,<br>Must make content with his fortunes fit,<br>Though the rain it raineth every day. |
| LEAR | True, boy. (To [[Kent]]) Come, bring us to this hovel. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear and [[Kent]] |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | This is a brave [[Night\|night]] to cool a courtesan. I’ll speak a prophecy ere I go:<br>When priests are more in word than matter;<br>When brewers mar their malt with [[Water\|water]];<br>When nobles are their tailors’ tutors,<br>No heretics burned, but wenches’ suitors,<br>Then shall the realm of Albion<br>Come to great confusion.<br><br>When every case in law is right;<br>No squire in debt nor no poor knight;<br>When slanders do not live in tongues,<br>Nor cutpurses come not to throngs;<br>When usurers tell their gold i‘th’ field,<br>And bawds and whores do churches build,<br>Then comes the [[Time\|time]], who lives to see’t,<br>That going shall be used with feet.<br>This prophecy Merlin shall make; for I live before his [[Time\|time]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] and Edmond |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, alack, Edmond, I like not this unnatural dealing. When I desired their leave that I might pity him, they took from me the use of mine own [[house]], charged me on [[Pain\|pain]] of perpetual displeasure neither to speak of him, entreat for him, or any way sustain him. |
| EDMOND | Most savage and unnatural! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Go to, say you nothing. There is division between the Dukes, and a worse matter than that. I have received a letter this [[Night\|night]]—‘tis dangerous to be spoken—I have locked the letter in my closet. These injuries the [[King]] now bears will be revenged [[Home\|home]]. There is part of a power already footed. We must incline to the [[King]]. I will look him and privily relieve him. Go you and maintain talk with the Duke, that my [[Charity\|charity]] be not of him perceived. If he ask for me, I am ill and gone to bed. If I die for’t—as no less is threatened me—the [[King]] my old master must be relieved. There is strange things toward, Edmond; pray you be careful. Exit |
| EDMOND | This courtesy, forbid thee, shall the Duke<br>Instantly know, and of that letter too.<br>This seems a fair deserving, and must draw me<br>That which my father loses: no less than all.<br>The younger rises when the old doth fall. Exit |

### Atto 3, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Here is the place, my lord. Good my lord, enter.<br>The tyranny of the open [[Night\|night]]’s too rough<br>For [[Nature\|nature]] to endure. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still |
| LEAR | Let me [[Alone\|alone]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my lord, enter here. |
| LEAR | Wilt break my heart? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I had rather break mine own. Good my lord, enter. |
| LEAR | Thou think‘st ’tis much that this contentious [[Storm\|storm]]<br>Invades us to the skin. So ‘tis to thee;<br>But where the greater malady is fixed,<br>The lesser is scarce felt. Thou’dst shun a bear,<br>But if thy flight lay toward the roaring sea<br>Thou‘dst meet the bear i’th’ mouth. When the mind’s<br>free,<br>The [[Body\|body]]’s delicate. This tempest in my mind<br>Doth from my senses take all feeling else<br>Save what beats there: filial ingratitude.<br>Is it not as this mouth should tear this hand<br>For lifting food to’t? But I will punish [[Home\|home]].<br>No, I will weep no more.—In such a [[Night\|night]]<br>To shut me out? Pour on, I will endure.<br>In such a [[Night\|night]] as this! O [[Regan]], [[Goneril]],<br>Your old kind father, whose frank heart gave all—<br>O, that way [[Madness\|madness]] lies. Let me shun that.<br>No more of that. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my lord, enter here. |
| LEAR | Prithee, go in thyself. Seek thine own ease.<br>This tempest will not give me leave to ponder<br>On things would hurt me more; but I’ll go in. |
| *(didascalia)* | (To [[Fool]]) In, boy; go first. Kneeling You houseless |
| LEAR | [[Poverty\|poverty]]—<br>Nay, get thee in. I’ll pray, and then I’ll [[Sleep\|sleep]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Fool]] |
| LEAR | Poor naked wretches, wheresoe‘er you are,<br>That bide the pelting of this pitiless [[Storm\|storm]],<br>How shall your houseless heads and unfed sides,<br>Your looped and windowed raggedness, defend you<br>From [[Seasons\|seasons]] such as these? O, I have ta’en<br>Too little care of this. Take physic, pomp,<br>Expose thyself to feel what wretches feel,<br>That thou mayst shake the superflux to them<br>And show the heavens more just. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Lear’s [[Fool]], and [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar in the hovel |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Fathom and half! Fathom and half! Poor Tom! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Come not in here, nuncle. Here’s a spirit. Help me, help me! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Give me thy hand. Who’s there? [[Fool\|FOOL]] A spirit, a spirit. He says his name’s Poor Tom. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What art thou that dost grumble there i’th’ straw?<br>Come forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] comes forth |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Away, the foul fiend follows me.<br>Thorough the sharp hawthorn blow the winds. Hm!<br>Go to thy cold bed and warm thee. |
| LEAR | Didst thou give all to thy two daughters,<br>And art thou come to this? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Who gives anything to Poor Tom, whom the foul fiend hath led through [[Fire\|fire]] and through flame, through ford and whirlpool, o’er bog and quagmire; that hath laid knives under his pillow and halters in his pew, set ratsbane by his porridge, made him proud of heart to ride on a bay trotting-[[Horse\|horse]] over four-inched bridges, to course his own [[Shadow\|shadow]] for a traitor. Bless thy five wits, Tom’s a-cold! O, do, de, do, de, do de. Bless thee from whirlwinds, star-blasting, and taking. Do Poor Tom some [[Charity\|charity]], whom the foul fiend vexes. There could I have him now, and there, and there again, and there. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still |
| LEAR | Has his daughters brought him to this pass?<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Couldst thou save nothing? Wouldst thou<br>give ’em all? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Nay, he reserved a blanket, else we had been all shamed. |
| LEAR | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Now all the plagues that in the pendulous air<br>Hang fated o’er men’s faults light on thy daughters! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | He hath no daughters, sir. |
| LEAR | [[Death]], traitor! Nothing could have subdued [[Nature\|nature]]<br>To such a lowness but his unkind daughters.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Is it the fashion that discarded fathers<br>Should have thus little [[Mercy\|mercy]] on their flesh?<br>Judicious punishment: ’twas this flesh begot<br>Those pelican daughters. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Pillicock sat on Pillicock [[Hill]]; alow, alow, loo, loo. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | This cold [[Night\|night]] will turn us all to fools and madmen. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Take heed o’th’ foul fiend; obey thy parents; keep thy words’ justice; swear not; commit not with man’s sworn spouse; set not thy sweet heart on proud array. Tom’s a-cold. |
| LEAR | What hast thou been? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | A servingman, proud in heart and mind, that curled my hair, wore gloves in my cap, served the lust of my mistress’ heart, and did the act of darkness with her; swore as many oaths as I spake words, and broke them in the sweet face of heaven; one that slept in the contriving of lust, and waked to do it. Wine loved I deeply, dice dearly, and in woman out-paramoured the Turk. False of heart, light of ear, bloody of hand; hog in sloth, fox in stealth, wolf in greediness, dog in [[Madness\|madness]], [[Lion\|lion]] in prey. Let not the creaking of shoes nor the rustling of silks betray thy poor heart to woman. Keep thy foot out of brothels, thy hand out of plackets, thy pen from lenders’ books, and defy the foul fiend. Still through the hawthorn blows the cold [[Wind\|wind]], says suum, mun, nonny. [[Dauphin]], my boy! Boy, cessez; let him trot by. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still |
| LEAR | Thou wert better in a [[Grave\|grave]] than to answer with thy uncovered [[Body\|body]] this extremity of the skies. Is man no. more than this? Consider him [[Well\|well]]. Thou owest the worm no silk, the beast no hide, the sheep no wool, the cat no perfume. Ha, here’s three on ’s are sophisticated; thou art the thing itself. Unaccommodated man is no more but such a poor, bare, forked animal as thou art. Off, off, you lendings! Come, unbutton here. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] with a torch |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Prithee, nuncle, be contented. ’Tis a naughty [[Night\|night]] to swim in. Now a little [[Fire\|fire]] in a wild field were like an old lecher’s heart—a small spark, all the rest on ’s [[Body\|body]] cold. Look, here comes a walking [[Fire\|fire]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] This is the foul fiend Flibbertigibbet. He begins at curfew and walks till the first cock. He gives the web and the pin, squints the eye, and makes the harelip; mildews the white wheat, and hurts the poor creature of earth. Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Swithin footed thrice the wold,<br>A met the [[Night\|night]] mare and her nine foal,<br>Bid her alight<br>And her troth plight,<br>And aroint thee, [[Witch\|witch]], aroint thee! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>How fares your grace? |
| LEAR | What’s he? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]]) Who’s there? What is’t you seek? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What are you there? Your names? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom, that eats the swimming frog, the toad, the tadpole, the wall-newt and the [[Water\|water]]; that in the fury of his heart, when the foul fiend rages, eats cowdung for salads, swallows the old rat and the ditch-dog, drinks the green mantle of the standing pool; who is whipped from tithing to tithing, and stocked, punished, and imprisoned; who hath had three suits to his back, six shirts to his [[Body\|body]],<br>[[Horse]] to ride, and weapon to wear;<br>But mice and rats and such small deer<br>Have been Tom’s food for seven long year.<br>Beware my follower. Peace, Smulkin; peace, thou fiend! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Lear)<br>What, hath your grace no better company? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The Prince of Darkness is a gentleman.<br>Modo he’s called, and Mahu. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Lear)<br>Our flesh and [[Blood\|blood]], my lord, is grown so vile<br>That it doth hate what gets it. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom’s a-cold. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Lear)<br>Go in with me. My duty cannot suffer<br>T’obey in all your daughters’ hard commands.<br>Though their injunction be to bar my doors<br>And let this tyrannous [[Night\|night]] take hold upon you,<br>Yet have I ventured to come seek you out<br>And bring you where both [[Fire\|fire]] and food is ready. |
| LEAR | First let me talk with this philosopher.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) What is the cause of thunder? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my lord, take his offer; go into th’ [[house]]. |
| LEAR | I’ll talk a word with this same learned Theban.<br>(To [[Edgar]]) What is your study? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | How to prevent the fiend, and to kill vermin. |
| LEAR | Let me ask you one word in private.<br>They converse apart |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>Importune him once more to go, my lord.<br>His wits begin t’unsettle. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Canst thou blame him? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] still |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | His daughters seek his [[Death\|death]]. Ah, that good [[Kent]],<br>He said it would be thus, poor banished man!<br>Thou sayst the [[King]] grows mad; I’ll tell thee, friend,<br>I am almost mad myself. I had a son,<br>Now outlawed from my [[Blood\|blood]]; a sought my life<br>But lately, very late. I loved him, friend;<br>No father his son dearer. True to tell thee,<br>The grief hath crazed my wits. What a [[Night\|night]]’s this!<br>(To Lear) I do beseech your grace— |
| LEAR | O, cry you [[Mercy\|mercy]], sir!<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Noble philosopher, your company. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Tom’s a-cold. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | In, fellow, there in t’hovel; keep thee warm. |
| LEAR | Come, let’s in all. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | This way, my lord. |
| LEAR | With him!<br>I will keep still with my philosopher. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>Good my lord, soothe him; let him take the fellow. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Take him you on. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent\|KENT]] to [[Edgar]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sirrah, come on. Go along with us. |
| LEAR | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Come, good Athenian. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No words, no words. Hush. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | [[Child]] Roland to the dark tower came,<br>His word was still ‘Fie, fo, and fum;<br>I smell the [[Blood\|blood]] of a British man.’ |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall and Edmond |
| CORNWALL | I will have my [[Revenge\|revenge]] ere I depart his [[house]]. |
| EDMOND | How, my lord, I may be censured, that [[Nature\|nature]] thus gives way to loyalty, something fears me to think of. |
| CORNWALL | I now perceive it was not altogether your brother’s evil disposition made him seek his [[Death\|death]], but a provoking merit set a-work by a reprovable badness in himself. 8 |
| EDMOND | How malicious is my fortune, that I must repent to be just! This is the letter which he spoke of, which approves him an intelligent party to the advantages of France. O heavens, that this treason were not, or not I the detector! |
| CORNWALL | Go with me to the Duchess. |
| EDMOND | If the matter of this paper be certain, you have mighty business in hand. |
| CORNWALL | True or false, it hath made thee Earl of [[Gloucester]]. Seek out where thy father is, that he may be ready for our apprehension. |
| *(didascalia)* | EDMOND aside If I find him comforting the [[King]], it will stuff his suspicion more fully. (To Cornwall) I will persever in my course of loyalty, though the conflict be sore between that and my [[Blood\|blood]]. |
| CORNWALL | I will lay trust upon thee, and thou shalt find a dearer father in my [[Love\|love]]. Exeunt |

### Atto 3, Scena 6

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Here is better than the open air; take it thankfully. I will piece out the comfort with what addition I can. I will not be long from you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | All the power of his wits have given way to his impatience; the gods reward your kindness! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Gloucester]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar, and Lear’s [[Fool]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Frateretto calls me, and tells me Nero is an angler in the lake of darkness. Pray, [[Innocent\|innocent]], and beware the foul fiend. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Prithee, nuncle, tell me whether a madman be a gentleman or a yeoman. |
| LEAR | A [[King\|king]], a [[King\|king]]! |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | No, he’s a yeoman that has a gentleman to his son; for he’s a mad yeoman that sees his son a gentleman before him. |
| LEAR | To have a thousand with red burning spits<br>Come hissing in upon ’em! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bless thy five wits. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>O, pity! Sir, where is the patience now<br>That you so oft have boasted to retain? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>My [[Tears\|tears]] begin to take his part so much<br>They mar my counterfeiting. |
| LEAR | The little [[dogs]] and all,<br>Tray, Blanch, and Sweetheart—see, they bark at me. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Tom will throw his head at them.—Avaunt, you curs!<br>Be thy mouth or black or white,<br>Tooth that poisons if it bite,<br>Mastiff, greyhound, mongrel grim,<br>Hound or spaniel, brach or him,<br>Bobtail tyke or trundle-tail,<br>Tom will make him weep and wail;<br>For with throwing thus my head,<br>[[dogs\|Dogs]] leapt the hatch, and all are fled.<br>Do, de, de, de. Sese! Come, march to wakes and fairs<br>And market towns. Poor Tom, thy horn is dry. |
| LEAR | Then let them anatomize [[Regan]]; see what breeds about her heart. Is there any cause in [[Nature\|nature]] that makes these hard-hearts? (To [[Edgar]]) You, sir, I entertain for one of my hundred, only I do not like the fashion of your garments. You will say they are Persian; but let them be changed. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Now, good my lord, lie here and rest a while. |
| LEAR | Make no noise, make no noise. Draw the curtains.<br>So, so. We’ll go to supper i’th’ morning. |
| *(didascalia)* | He steps |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | And I’ll go to bed at [[Noon\|noon]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[Kent]])<br>Come hither, friend. Where is the [[King]] my master? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Here, sir, but trouble him not; his wits are gone. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Good friend, I prithee take him in thy arms.<br>I have o’erheard a plot of [[Death\|death]] upon him.<br>There is a litter ready. Lay him in’t<br>And drive toward Dover, friend, where thou shalt meet<br>Both welcome and protection. Take up thy master.<br>If thou shouldst dally half an hour, his life,<br>With thine and all that offer to defend him,<br>Stand in assured loss. Take up, take up,<br>And follow me, that will to some provision<br>Give thee quick conduct. Come, come away. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt, [[Kent]] carrying Lear in his arms |

### Atto 3, Scena 7

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall, [[Regan]], [[Goneril]], Edmond the bastard, and Servants |
| CORNWALL | (to [[Goneril]])<br>Post speedily to my lord your husband.<br>Show him this letter. The army of France is landed.<br>(To Servants) Seek out the traitor [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Hang him instantly. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Pluck out his eyes. |
| CORNWALL | Leave him to my displeasure.<br>Edmond, keep you our sister company.<br>The revenges we are bound to take upon your traitorous<br>father are not fit for your beholding. Advise the Duke<br>where you are going, to a most festinate preparation;<br>we are bound to the like. Our posts shall be swift and<br>intelligent betwixt us. (To [[Goneril]]) Farewell, dear sister.<br>(To Edmond) Farewell, my lord of [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| CORNWALL | How now, where’s the [[King]]? |
| OSWALD | My lord of [[Gloucester]] hath conveyed him hence.<br>Some five- or six-and-thirty of his knights,<br>Hot questrists after him, met him at gate,<br>Who, with some other of the lord’s dependants,<br>Are gone with him toward Dover, where they boast<br>To have [[Well\|well]]-armèd friends. |
| CORNWALL | Get horses for your mistress. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit Oswald |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Farewell, sweet lord, and sister. |
| CORNWALL | Edmond, farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt [[Goneril]] and Edmond |
| CORNWALL | (To Servants) Go seek the traitor [[Gloucester]].<br>Pinion him like a thief; bring him before us. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt other Servants |
| CORNWALL | Though [[Well\|well]] we may not pass upon his life<br>Without the form of justice, yet our power<br>Shall do a curtsy to our wrath, which men<br>May blame but not control. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] and Servants |
| CORNWALL | Who’s there—the traitor? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Ingrateful fox, ’tis he. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants) Bind fast his corky arms. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What means your graces? Good my friends, consider<br>You are my guests. Do me no foul play, friends. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants)<br>Bind him, I say. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Hard, hard! O filthy traitor! |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Unmerciful lady as you are, I’m none. |
| CORNWALL | (to Servants)<br>To this chair bind him. (To [[Gloucester]]) [[Villain]], thou shalt find—<br>[[Regan]] plucks [[Gloucester]]’s beard |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By the kind gods, ’tis most ignobly done,<br>To pluck me by the beard. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | So white, and such a traitor? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Naughty lady,<br>These hairs which thou dost ravish from my chin<br>Will quicken and accuse thee. I am your host.<br>With robbers’ hands my hospitable favours<br>You should not ruffle thus. What will you do? |
| CORNWALL | Come, sir, what letters had you late from France? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Be simple-answered, for we know the [[Truth\|truth]]. |
| CORNWALL | And what confederacy have you with the traitors<br>Late footed in the kingdom? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | To whose hands<br>You have sent the lunatic [[King]]. Speak. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I have a letter guessingly set down,<br>Which came from one that’s of a neutral heart,<br>And not from one opposed. |
| CORNWALL | Cunning. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And false. |
| CORNWALL | Where hast thou sent the [[King]]? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | To Dover. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Wherefore to Dover? Wast thou not charged at peril— |
| CORNWALL | Wherefore to Dover?—Let him answer that. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I am tied to th’ stake, and I must stand the course. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Wherefore to Dover? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Because I would not see thy cruel nails<br>Pluck out his poor old eyes, nor thy fierce sister<br>In his anointed flesh stick boarish fangs.<br>The sea, with such a [[Storm\|storm]] as his bare head<br>In hell-black [[Night\|night]] endured, would have buoyed up<br>And quenched the stellèd fires.<br>Yet, poor old heart, he holp the heavens to rain.<br>If wolves had at thy gate howled that stern [[Time\|time]],<br>Thou shouldst have said ‘Good porter, turn the key;<br>All cruels I’ll subscribe.’ But I shall see<br>The winged vengeance overtake such children. |
| CORNWALL | See’t shalt thou never.—Fettows, hold the chair.—<br>Upon these eyes of thine I’ll set my foot. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He that will think to live till he be old<br>Give me some help!—O cruel! O you gods! |
| *(didascalia)* | Cornwall pulls out one of [[Gloucester]]’s eyes and stamps on it |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to Cornwall)<br>One side will mock another; th’other, too. |
| CORNWALL | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>If you see vengeance— |
| SERVANT | Hold your hand, my lord.<br>I have served you ever since I was a [[Child\|child]],<br>But better service have I never done you<br>Than now to bid you hold. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | How now, you dog! |
| SERVANT | If you did wear a beard upon your chin |
| *(didascalia)* | I’d shake it on this quarrel. To Cornwall What do |
| SERVANT | you mean? |
| CORNWALL | My villein! |
| SERVANT | Nay then, come on, and take the chance of anger.<br>They draw and fight |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to another Servant)<br>Give me thy [[Sword\|sword]]. A peasant stand up thus! |
| *(didascalia)* | She takes a [[Sword\|sword]] and runs at him behind |
| SERVANT | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>O, I am slain. My lord, you have one eye left<br>To see some mischief on him. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Regan]] stabs him again |
| SERVANT | O!<br>He dies |
| CORNWALL | Lest it see more, prevent it. Out, vile jelly! |
| *(didascalia)* | He pulls out [[Gloucester]]’s other eye |
| CORNWALL | Where is thy lustre now? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | All dark and comfortless. Where’s my son Edmond?<br>Edmond, enkindle all the sparks of [[Nature\|nature]]<br>To quite this horrid act. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Out, treacherous [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>Thou call’st on him that hates thee. It was he<br>That made the overture of thy treasons to us,<br>Who is too good to pity thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O, my follies! Then [[Edgar]] was abused.<br>Kind gods, forgive me that, and prosper him! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to Servants)<br>Go thrust him out at gates, and let him smell<br>His way to Dover. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more with [[Gloucester]] |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | How is’t, my lord? How look you? |
| CORNWALL | I have received a hurt. Follow me, lady.<br>(To Servants) Turn out that eyeless [[Villain\|villain]]. Throw this<br>slave<br>Upon the dunghill. [[Regan]], I bleed apace.<br>Untimely comes this hurt. Give me your arm. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with the [[Body\|body]] |

### Atto 4, Scena 1

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] as a Bedlam beggar |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Yet better thus and known to be contemned<br>Than still contemned and flattered. To be worst,<br>The low’st and most dejected thing of fortune,<br>Stands still in esperance, lives not in [[Fear\|fear]].<br>The lamentable change is from the best;<br>The worst returns to laughter. Welcome, then,<br>Thou unsubstantial air that I embrace.<br>The wretch that thou hast blown unto the worst<br>Owes nothing to thy blasts. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] led by an Old Man |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | But who comes here?<br>My father, parti-eyed? World, world, O world!<br>But that thy strange mutations make us hate thee,<br>Life would not yield to age. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] stands aside |
| OLD MAN | (to [[Gloucester]]) O my good lord,<br>I have been your tenant and your father’s tenant<br>These fourscore years. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away, get thee away, good friend, be gone.<br>Thy comforts can do me no good at all;<br>Thee they may hurt. |
| OLD MAN | You cannot see your way. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I have no way, and therefore want no eyes.<br>I stumbled when I saw. Full oft ’tis seen<br>Our means secure us, and our mere defects<br>Prove our commodities. O dear son [[Edgar]],<br>The food of thy abused father’s wrath-<br>Might I but live to see thee in my touch<br>I’d say I had eyes again. |
| OLD MAN | How, now? Who’s there? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>O gods! Who is’t can say ‘I am at the worst’?<br>I am worse than e’er I was. |
| OLD MAN | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>’Tis poor mad Tom. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>And worse I may be yet. The worst is not<br>So long as we can say ‘This is the worst.’ |
| OLD MAN | (to [[Edgar]]) Fellow, where goest? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Is it a beggarman? |
| OLD MAN | Madman and beggar too. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A has some reason, else he could not beg.<br>I’th’ last [[Night\|night]]’s [[Storm\|storm]] I such a fellow saw,<br>Which made me think a man a worm. My son<br>Came then into my mind, and yet my mind<br>Was then scarce friends with him. I have heard more<br>since.<br>As flies to wanton boys are we to th’ gods;<br>They kill us for their sport. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside) How should this be?<br>Bad is the trade that must play [[Fool\|fool]] to sorrow,<br>Ang’ring itself and others. |
| *(didascalia)* | He comes forward |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bless thee, master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Is that the naked fellow? |
| OLD MAN | Ay, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Get thee away. If for my sake<br>Thou wilt o‘ertake us hence a mile or twain<br>I’th’ way toward Dover, do it for ancient [[Love\|love]],<br>And bring some covering for this naked [[Soul\|soul]],<br>Which I’ll entreat to lead me. |
| OLD MAN | Alack, sir, he is mad. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | ’Tis the [[Time\|time]]’s plague when madmen lead the blind.<br>Do as I bid thee; or rather do thy pleasure.<br>Above the rest, be gone. |
| OLD MAN | I’ll bring him the best ’parel that I have, so<br>Come on’t what will. Exit |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sirrah, naked fellow! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Poor Tom’s a-cold. (Aside) I cannot daub it further. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Come hither, fellow. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>And yet I must.<br>(To [[Gloucester]]) Bless thy sweet eyes, they bleed. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Know’st thou the way to Dover? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Both stile and gate, horseway and footpath. Poor Tom hath been scared out of his good wits. Bless thee, goodman’s son, from the foul fiend. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Here, take this purse, thou whom the heavens’<br>plagues<br>Have humbled to all strokes. That I am wretched<br>Makes thee the happier. Heavens deal so still.<br>Let the superfluous and lust-dieted man<br>That slaves your ordinance, that will not see<br>Because he does not feel, feel your power quickly.<br>So distribution should undo excess,<br>And each man have enough. Dost thou know Dover? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Ay, master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | There is a cliff whose high and bending head<br>Looks fearfully in the confined deep.<br>Bring me but to the very brim of it<br>And I’ll repair the misery thou dost bear<br>With something rich about me. From that place<br>I shall no leading need. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me thy arm.<br>Poor Tom shall lead thee. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] guiding [[Gloucester]] |

### Atto 4, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Goneril]] and Edmond the bastard at one door and Oswald the steward at another |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Welcome, my lord. I marvel our mild husband<br>Not met us on the way. (To Oswald) Now, where’s<br>your master? |
| OSWALD | Madam, within; but never man so changed.<br>I told him of the army that was landed;<br>He smiled at it. I told him you were coming;<br>His answer was ‘The worse’. Of [[Gloucester]]’s treachery<br>And of the loyal service of his son<br>When I informed him, then he called me sot,<br>And told me I had turned the wrong side out.<br>What most he should dislike seems pleasant to him;<br>What like, offensive. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (to Edmond) Then shall you go no further.<br>It is the cowish terror of his spirit<br>That dares not undertake. He’ll not feel wrongs<br>Which tie him to an answer. Our wishes on the way<br>May prove effects. Back, Edmond, to my brother.<br>Hasten his musters and conduct his powers.<br>I must change names at [[Home\|home]], and give the distaff<br>Into my husband’s hands. This trusty servant<br>Shall pass between us. Ere long you are like to hear,<br>If you dare venture in your own behalf,<br>A mistress’s command. Wear this. Spare speech.<br>Decline your head. This kiss, if it durst speak,<br>Would stretch thy spirits up into the air. |
| *(didascalia)* | She kisses him |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Conceive, and fare thee [[Well\|well]]. |
| EDMOND | Yours in the ranks of [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | My most dear [[Gloucester]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Edmond<br>O, the difference of man and man!<br>To thee a woman’s services are due;<br>My [[Fool\|fool]] usurps my [[Body\|body]]. |
| OSWALD | Madam, here comes my lord. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of |
| OSWALD | Albany |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | I have been worth the whistling. |
| ALBANY | O [[Goneril]],<br>You are not worth the [[Dust\|dust]] which the rude [[Wind\|wind]]<br>Blows in your face. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Milk-livered man,<br>That bear’st a cheek for blows, a head for wrongs;<br>Who hast not in thy brows an eye discerning<br>Thine [[Honour\|honour]] from thy [[Suffering\|suffering]]— |
| ALBANY | See thyself, devil.<br>Proper deformity shows not in the fiend<br>So horrid as in woman. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | O vain [[Fool\|fool]]! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | O my good lord, the Duke of Cornwall’s dead,<br>Slain by his servant going to put out<br>The other eye of [[Gloucester]]. |
| ALBANY | [[Gloucester]]’s eyes? |
| MESSENGER | A servant that he bred, thrilled with remorse,<br>Opposed against the act, bending his [[Sword\|sword]]<br>To his great master, who thereat enraged<br>Flew on him, and amongst them felled him dead,<br>But not without that harmful stroke which since<br>Hath plucked him after. |
| ALBANY | This shows you are above,<br>You justicers, that these our nether crimes<br>So speedily can venge. But O, poor [[Gloucester]]!<br>Lost he his other eye? |
| MESSENGER | Both, both, my lord.—µ<br>This letter, madam, craves a speedy answer.<br>’Tis from your sister. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (aside)<br>One way I like this [[Well\|well]];<br>But being widow, and my [[Gloucester]] with her,<br>May all the building in my fancy pluck<br>Upon my hateful life. Another way<br>The news is not so tart.—I’ll read and answer. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | with Oswald |
| ALBANY | Where was his son when they did take his eyes? |
| MESSENGER | Come with my lady hither. |
| ALBANY | He is not here. |
| MESSENGER | No, my good lord; I met him back again. |
| ALBANY | Knows he the wickedness? |
| MESSENGER | Ay, my good lord; ’twas he informed against him,<br>And quit the [[house]] on purpose that their punishment<br>Might have the freer course. |
| ALBANY | [[Gloucester]], I live<br>To thank thee for the [[Love\|love]] thou showed‘st the [[King]],<br>And to [[Revenge\|revenge]] thine eyes.—Come hither, friend.<br>Tell me what more thou know’st. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter with a drummer and colours, [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]], Gentlemen, and soldiers |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Alack, ’tis he! Why, he was met even now,<br>As mad as the vexed sea, singing aloud,<br>Crowned with rank fumitor and furrow-weeds,<br>With burdocks, hemlock, nettles, cuckoo-[[Flowers\|flowers]],<br>Darnel, and all the idle weeds that grow<br>In our sustaining corn. A century send forth.<br>Search every acre in the high-grown field,<br>And bring him to our eye. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | one or more |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | What can man’s wisdom<br>In the restoring his<br>He that helps him take all my outward worth. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN There is means, madam. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Our foster-[[Nurse\|nurse]] of [[Nature\|nature]] is repose,<br>The which he lacks. That to provoke in him<br>Are many simples operative, whose power<br>Will close the eye of anguish. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | All blest [[Secrets\|secrets]],<br>All you unpublished virtues of the earth,<br>[[spring\|Spring]] with my [[Tears\|tears]], be aidant and remediate<br>In the good man’s distress!—Seek, seek for him,<br>Lest his ungoverned rage dissolve the life<br>That wants the means to lead it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | a Messenger |
| MESSENGER | News, madam.<br>The British powers are marching hitherward. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | ’Tis known before; our preparation stands<br>In expectation of them.—O dear father,<br>It is thy business that I go about;<br>Therefore great France<br>My mourning and importuned [[Tears\|tears]] hath pitied.<br>No blown [[Ambition\|ambition]] doth our arms incite,<br>But [[Love\|love]], dear [[Love\|love]], and our aged father’s right.<br>Soon may I hear and see him! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

### Atto 4, Scena 4

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Regan]] and Oswald the steward |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | But are my brother’s powers set forth? |
| OSWALD | Ay, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Himself in person there? |
| OSWALD | Madam, with much ado.<br>Your sister is the better soldier. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Lord Edmond spake not with your lord at [[Home\|home]]? |
| OSWALD | No, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What might import my sister’s letters to him? |
| OSWALD | I know not, lady. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | [[Faith]], he is posted hence on serious matter.<br>It was great ignorance, [[Gloucester]]’s eyes being out,<br>To let him live. Where he arrives he moves<br>All hearts against us. Edmond, I think, is gone,<br>In pity of his misery, to dispatch<br>His ‘nighted life, moreover to descry<br>The strength o’th’ enemy. |
| OSWALD | I must needs after, madam, with my letter. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Our troops set forth tomorrow. Stay with us.<br>The ways are dangerous. |
| OSWALD | I may not, madam.<br>My lady charged my duty in this business. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why should she write to Edmond? Might not you<br>Transport her purposes by word? Belike—<br>Some things—I know not what. I’ll [[Love\|love]] thee much:<br>Let me unseal the letter. |
| OSWALD | Madam, I had rather— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I know your lady does not [[Love\|love]] her husband.<br>I am sure of that, and at her late being here<br>She gave strange oeillades and most speaking looks<br>To noble Edmond. I know you are of her bosom. |
| OSWALD | I, madam? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I speak in understanding. Y’are, I know’t.<br>Therefore I do advise you take this note.<br>My lord is dead. Edmond and I have talked,<br>And more convenient is he for my hand<br>Than for your lady’s. You may gather more.<br>If you do find him, pray you give him this,<br>And when your mistress hears thus much from you,<br>I pray [[Desire\|desire]] her call her wisdom to her.<br>So, fare you [[Well\|well]].<br>If you do chance to hear of that blind traitor,<br>Preferment falls on him that cuts him off. |
| OSWALD | Would I could meet him, madam. I should show<br>What party I do follow. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Fare thee [[Well\|well]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |

### Atto 4, Scena 5

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] disguised as a peasant, with a staff, guiding the blind [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | When shall I come to th’ top of that same [[Hill\|hill]]? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | You do climb up it now. Look how we labour. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Methinks the ground is even. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Horrible steep.<br>Hark, do you hear the sea? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No, truly. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Why, then your other senses grow imperfect<br>By your eyes’ anguish. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | So may it be indeed.<br>Methinks thy voice is altered, and thou speak’st<br>In better phrase and matter than thou didst. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | You’re much deceived. In nothing am I changed<br>But in my garments. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Methinks you’re better spoken. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Come on, sir, here’s the place. Stand still. How fearful<br>And dizzy ‘tis to cast one’s eyes so low!<br>The crows and choughs that wing the midway air<br>Show scarce so gross as beetles. Halfway down<br>Hangs one that gathers samphire, dreadful trade!<br>Methinks he seems no bigger than his head.<br>The fishermen that walk upon the beach<br>Appear like mice, and yon tall anchoring barque<br>Diminished to her cock, her cock a buoy<br>Almost too small for sight. The murmuring surge<br>That on th’unnumbered idle pebble chafes<br>Cannot be heard so high. I’ll look no more,<br>Lest my brain turn and the deficient sight<br>Topple down headlong. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Set me where you stand. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your hand. You are now within a foot<br>Of th’extreme verge. For all beneath the [[Moon\|moon]]<br>Would I not leap upright. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let go my hand.<br>Here, friend, ’s another purse; in it a [[Jewel\|jewel]]<br>[[Well]] worth a poor man’s taking. Fairies and gods<br>Prosper it with thee! Go thou further off.<br>Bid me farewell, and let me hear thee going. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Now fare ye [[Well\|well]], good sir.<br>He stands aside |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | With all my heart. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>Why I do trifle thus with his [[Despair\|despair]]<br>Is done to cure it. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (keeling) O you mighty gods,<br>This world I do renounce, and in your sights<br>Shake patiently my great affliction off!<br>If I could bear it longer, and not fall<br>To quarrel with your great opposeless wills,<br>My snuff and loathed part of [[Nature\|nature]] should<br>Burn itself out. If [[Edgar]] live, O bless him!—<br>Now, fellow, fare thee [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Gone, sir. Farewell.<br>[[Gloucester]] falls forward<br>(Aside) And yet I know not how conceit may rob<br>The treasury of life, when life itself<br>Yields to the theft. Had he been where he thought,<br>By this had thought been past.—Alive or dead?<br>(To [[Gloucester]]) Ho, you, sir, friend; hear you, sir?<br>Speak.<br>(Aside) Thus might he pass indeed. Yet he revives.<br>(To [[Gloucester]]) What are you, sir? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Away, and let me die. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Hadst thou been aught but gossamer, feathers, air,<br>So many fathom down precipitating<br>Thou‘dst shivered like an egg. But thou dost breathe,<br>Hast heavy substance, bleed’st not, speak’st, art sound.<br>Ten masts a-length make not the altitude<br>Which thou hast perpendicularly fell.<br>Thy life’s a miracle. Speak yet again. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But have I fall’n, or no? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | From the dread summit of this chalky bourn.<br>Look up a-height. The shrill-gorged lark so far<br>Cannot be seen or heard. Do but look up. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, I have no eyes.<br>Is wretchedness deprived that benefit<br>To end itself by [[Death\|death]]? ’Twas yet some comfort<br>When misery could beguile the [[Tyrant\|tyrant]]’s rage<br>And frustrate his proud will. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your arm.<br>Up, so. How is’t? Feel you your legs? You stand. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Too [[Well\|well]], too [[Well\|well]]. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | This is above all strangeness.<br>Upon the [[Crown\|crown]] o’th’ cliff what thing was that<br>Which parted from you? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | A poor unfortunate beggar. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | As I stood here below, methoughts his eyes<br>Were two full moons. He had a thousand noses,<br>Horns whelked and waved like the enraged sea.<br>It was some fiend. Therefore, thou happy father,<br>Think that the clearest gods, who make them honours<br>Of men’s impossibilities, have preserved thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I do remember now. Henceforth I’ll bear<br>Affliction till it do cry out itself<br>‘Enough, enough,’ and die. That thing you speak of,<br>I took it for a man. Often ‘twould say<br>‘The fiend, the fiend!’ He led me to that place. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Bear free and patient thoughts. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] mad, crowned with weeds and [[Flowers\|flowers]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | But who comes here?<br>The safer sense will ne’er accommodate<br>His master thus. |
| LEAR | No, they cannot touch me for crying. I am the [[King]] himself. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | O thou side-piercing sight! |
| LEAR | [[Nature]]’s above art in that respect. There’s your press-money. That fellow handles his bow like a crow-keeper. Draw me a clothier’s yard. Look, look, a mouse! Peace, peace, this piece of toasted [[cheese]] will do’t. There’s my gauntlet. I’ll prove it on a giant. Bring up the brown bills. O, [[Well\|well]] flown, bird, i‘th’ clout, i’th’ clout! Whew! Give the word. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sweet marjoram. |
| LEAR | Pass. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I know that voice. |
| LEAR | Ha! [[Goneril]] with a white beard? They flattered me like a dog, and told me I had the white hairs in my beard ere the black ones were there. To say ‘ay’ and ‘no’ to everything that I said ‘ay’ and ‘no’ to was no good divinity. When the rain came to wet me once, and the [[Wind\|wind]] to make me chatter; when the thunder would not peace at my bidding, there I found ‘em, there I smelt ’em out. Go to, they are not men o’ their words. They told me I was everything; ’tis a lie, I am not ague-proof. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The trick of that voice I do [[Well\|well]] remember.<br>Is’t not the [[King]]? |
| LEAR | Ay, every inch a [[King\|king]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester]] kneels |
| LEAR | When I do stare, see how the subject quakes!<br>I pardon that man’s life. What was thy cause?<br>Adultery? Thou shalt not die. Die for adultery!<br>No, the wren goes to‘t, and the small gilded fly<br>Does lecher in my sight. Let copulation thrive,<br>For [[Gloucester]]’s bastard son<br>Was kinder to his father than my daughters<br>Got ’tween the lawful sheets. To‘t, luxury, pell-mell,<br>For I lack soldiers. Behold yon simp’ring dame,<br>Whose face between her forks presages [[Snow\|snow]],<br>That minces virtue, and does shake the head<br>To hear of pleasure’s name.<br>The fitchew nor the soilèd [[Horse\|horse]] goes to’t<br>With a more riotous appetite. Down from the waist<br>They’re centaurs, though women all above.<br>But to the girdle do the gods inherit;<br>Beneath is all the fiend’s. There’s hell, there’s darkness,<br>there is the sulphurous pit, burning, scalding, stench,<br>consumption. Fie, fie, fie; pah, pah! Give me an ounce<br>of civet, good apothecary, sweeten my [[Imagination\|imagination]].<br>There’s money for thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O, let me kiss that hand! |
| LEAR | Let me wipe it first; it smells of [[Mortality\|mortality]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O ruined piece of [[Nature\|nature]]! This great world<br>Shall so wear out to naught. Dost thou know me? |
| LEAR | I remember thine eyes [[Well\|well]] enough. Dost thou squiny at me?<br>No, do thy worst, blind Cupid, I’ll not [[Love\|love]].<br>Read thou this challenge. Mark but the penning of it. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Were all thy letters suns, I could not see. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>I would not take this from report; it is,<br>And my heart breaks at it. |
| LEAR | (to [[Gloucester]]) Read. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What—with the case of eyes? |
| LEAR | O ho, are you there with me? No eyes in your head, nor no money in your purse? Your eyes are in a heavy case, your purse in a light; yet you see how this world goes. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I see it feelingly. |
| LEAR | What, art mad? A man may see how this world goes with no eyes; look with thine ears. See how yon justice rails upon yon simple thief. Hark in thine ear: change places, and handy-dandy, which is the justice, which is the thief? Thou hast seen a farmer’s dog bark at a beggar? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, sir. |
| LEAR | An the creature run from the cur, there thou mightst behold the great image of authority. A dog’s obeyed in office.<br>Thou rascal beadle, hold thy bloody hand.<br>Why dost thou lash that whore? Strip thy own back.<br>Thou hotly lusts to use her in that kind<br>For which thou whip‘st her. The usurer hangs the<br>cozener.<br>Through tattered clothes great vices do appear;<br>Robes and furred gowns hide all. Plate sin with gold,<br>And the strong lance of justice hurtless breaks;<br>Arm it in rags, a pygmy’s straw does pierce it.<br>None does offend, none, I say none. I’ll able ’em.<br>Take that of me, my friend, who have the power<br>To seal th’accuser’s lips. Get thee glass eyes,<br>And, like a scurvy politician, seem<br>To see the things thou dost not. Now, now, now, now!<br>Pull off my boots. Harder, harder! So. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (aside)<br>O, matter and impertinency mixed—170<br>Reason in [[Madness\|madness]]! |
| LEAR | If thou wilt weep my fortunes, take my eyes.<br>I know thee [[Well\|well]] enough: thy name is [[Gloucester]].<br>Thou must be patient. We came crying hither.<br>Thou know’st the first [[Time\|time]] that we smell the air<br>We waul and cry. I will preach to thee. Mark. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, alack the day! |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR removing his [[Crown\|crown]] of weeds |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | When we are born, we cry that we are come<br>To this great stage of fools. This’ a good block.<br>It were a delicate stratagem to shoe 180<br>A troop of [[Horse\|horse]] with felt. I’ll put’t in proof,<br>And when I have stol’n upon these son-in-laws,<br>Then kill, kill, kill, kill, kill, kill! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter two Gentlemen |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| *(didascalia)* | O, here he is. Lay hand upon him. To LearSir, |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Your most dear daughter— |
| LEAR | No rescue? What, a prisoner? I am even<br>The natural [[Fool\|fool]] of fortune. Use me [[Well\|well]].<br>You shall have ransom. Let me have surgeons;<br>I am cut to th’ brains. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN You shall have anything. |
| LEAR | No seconds? All myself?<br>Why, this would make a man a man of salt,<br>To use his eyes for [[Garden\|garden]] [[Water\|water]]-pots.<br>I will die bravely, like a smug bridegroom. What,<br>I will be jovial. Come, come, I am a [[King\|king]].<br>Masters, know you that? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | You are a royal one, and we obey you. |
| LEAR | Then there’s life in’t. Come, an you get it, you shall get it by running. Sa, sa, sa, sa! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit running pursued by a Gentleman |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | A sight most pitiful in the meanest wretch,<br>Past speaking in a [[King\|king]]. Thou hast a daughter<br>Who redeems [[Nature\|nature]] from the general curse<br>Which twain have brought her to. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Hail, gentle sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN Sir, [[Speed\|speed]] you. What’s your will? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Do you hear aught, sir, of a battle toward? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST+GENTLEMAN |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Most sure and vulgar, everyone hears that<br>That can distinguish sound. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | But, by your favour,<br>How near’s the other army? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN, |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Near and on speedy foot. The main descry<br>Stands in the hourly thought. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I thank you, sir. That’s all. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Though that the [[Queen]] on special cause is here,<br>Her army is moved on. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I thank you, sir. Exit Gentleman |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | You ever gentle gods, take my breath from me.<br>Let not my worser spirit tempt me again<br>To die before you please. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | [[Well]] pray you, father. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now, good sir, what are you? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | A most poor man, made tame to fortune’s blows,<br>Who by the art of known and feeling sorrows<br>Am pregnant to good pity. Give me your hand,<br>I’ll lead you to some biding. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] rising |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Hearty thanks.<br>The bounty and the benison of heaven<br>To boot and boot. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| OSWALD | A proclaimed prize! Most happy!<br>That eyeless head of thine was first framed flesh<br>To raise my fortunes. Thou old unhappy traitor,<br>Briefly thyself remember. The [[Sword\|sword]] is out<br>That must destroy thee. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now let thy friendly hand<br>Put strength enough to’t. |
| OSWALD | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Wherefore, bold peasant,<br>Durst thou support a published traitor? Hence,<br>Lest that th’infection of his fortune take<br>Like hold on thee. Let go his arm. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | ‘Chill not let go, sir, without vurther ’cagion. |
| OSWALD | Let go, slave, or thou diest. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Good gentleman, go your gate, and let poor volk pass. An ‘chud ha’ been swaggered out of my life, ’twould not ha’ been so long as ‘tis by a vortnight. Nay, come not near th’old man. Keep out, ‘che vor’ ye, or I’s’ try whether your [[Costard\|costard]] or my baton be the harder; I’ll be plain with you. |
| OSWALD | Out, dunghill! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | ’Chill pick your teeth, sir. Come, no matter vor your foins. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] knocks him down |
| OSWALD | Slave, thou hast slain me. [[Villain]], take my purse.<br>If ever thou wilt thrive, bury my [[Body\|body]],<br>And give the letters which thou find’st about me<br>To Edmond, Earl of [[Gloucester]]. Seek him out<br>Upon the English party. O untimely [[Death\|death]]! [[Death]]!<br>He dies |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I know thee [[Well\|well]]—a serviceable [[Villain\|villain]],<br>As duteous to the vices of thy mistress<br>As badness would [[Desire\|desire]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | What, is he dead? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sit you down, father. Rest you.<br>[[Gloucester]] sits<br>Let’s see these pockets. The letters that he speaks of<br>May be my friends. He’s dead; I am only sorrow<br>He had no other deathsman. Let us see.<br>Leave, gentle wax, and manners; blame us not.<br>To know our enemies’ minds we rip their hearts;<br>Their papers is more lawful.<br>He reads the letter<br>’Let our reciprocal vows be remembered. You have<br>many opportunities to cut him off. If your will want<br>not, [[Time\|time]] and place will be fruitfully offered. There is<br>nothing done if he return the conqueror; then am I<br>the prisoner, and his bed my jail, from the loathed<br>warmth whereof, deliver me, and supply the place for<br>your labour.<br>Your—wife, so I would say,—affectionate<br>servant, and for you her own for venture,<br>[[Goneril]].’<br>O indistinguished space of woman’s will—<br>A plot upon her virtuous husband’s life,<br>And the exchange my brother!—Here in the sands<br>Thee I’ll rake up, the post unsanctified<br>Of murderous lechers, and in the mature [[Time\|time]]<br>With this ungracious paper strike the sight<br>Of the [[Death\|death]]-practised Duke. For him ’tis [[Well\|well]]<br>That of thy [[Death\|death]] and business I can tell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit with the [[Body\|body]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is mad. How stiff is my vile sense,<br>That I stand up and have ingenious feeling<br>Of my huge sorrows! Better I were distraught,<br>So should my thoughts be severed from my griefs, |
| *(didascalia)* | Drum afar off |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And woes by wrong imaginations lose<br>The knowledge of themselves. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Give me your hand.<br>Far off methinks I hear the beaten drum.<br>Come, father, I’ll bestow you with a friend. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] guiding [[Gloucester]] |
| *(didascalia)* | 4.6 Enter [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]], the Earl of [[Kent]] disguised, and the First Gentleman |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O thou good [[Kent]], how shall I live and work<br>To match thy goodness? My life will be too short,<br>And every measure fail me. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | To be acknowledged, madam, is o’erpaid.<br>All my reports go with the modest [[Truth\|truth]],<br>Nor more, nor clipped, but so. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Be better suited.<br>These weeds are memories of those worser hours.<br>I prithee put them off. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Pardon, dear madam.<br>Yet to be known shortens my made intent.<br>My boon I make it that you know me not<br>Till [[Time\|time]] and I think meet. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Then be’t so, my good lord.—<br>How does the [[King]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN Madam, sleeps still. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O you kind gods,<br>Cure this great breach in his abused [[Nature\|nature]];<br>Th’untuned and jarring senses O [[Wind\|wind]] up<br>Of this [[Child\|child]]-changed father! |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | So please your majesty<br>That we may wake the [[King]]? He hath slept long. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Be governed by your knowledge, and proceed<br>I’th’ sway of your own will. Is he arrayed? |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Ay, madam. In the heaviness of [[Sleep\|sleep]]<br>We put fresh garments on him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] asleep, in a chair carried by servants |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Be by, good madam, when we do awake him.<br>I [[Doubt\|doubt]] not of his temperance. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | O my dear father, restoration hang<br>Thy medicine on my lips, and let this kiss<br>Repair those violent harms that my two sisters<br>Have in thy reverence made! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Kind and dear princess! |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Had you not been their father, these white flakes<br>Did challenge pity of them. Was this a face<br>To be opposed against the warring winds?<br>Mine enemy’s dog, though he had bit me, should<br>have stood<br>That [[Night\|night]] against my [[Fire\|fire]]. And wast thou fain, poor<br>father,<br>To hovel thee with swine and rogues forlorn<br>In short and musty straw? Alack, alack,<br>’Tis [[Wonder\|wonder]] that thy life and wits at once<br>Had not concluded all! (To the Gentleman) He wakes.<br>Speak to him. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN Madam, do you; ’tis fittest. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear)<br>How does my royal lord? How fares your majesty? |
| LEAR | You do me wrong to take me out o’th’ [[Grave\|grave]].<br>Thou art a [[Soul\|soul]] in bliss, but I am bound<br>Upon a [[Wheel\|wheel]] of [[Fire\|fire]], that mine own [[Tears\|tears]]<br>Do scald like molten lead. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Sir, do you know me? |
| LEAR | You are a spirit, I know. Where did you die? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to the Gentleman) Still, still far wide! |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | He’s scarce awake. Let him [[Alone\|alone]] a while. |
| LEAR | Where have I been? Where am I? Fair daylight?<br>I am mightily abused. I should ev’n die with pity<br>To see another thus. I know not what to say.<br>I will not swear these are my hands. Let’s see:<br>I feel this pin prick. Would I were assured<br>Of my condition. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (kneeling) O look upon me, sir,<br>And hold your hands in benediction o’er me.<br>You must not kneel. |
| LEAR | Pray do not mock.<br>I am a very foolish, fond old man,<br>Fourscore and upward,<br>Not an hour more nor less; and to deal plainly,<br>I [[Fear\|fear]] I am not in my perfect mind.<br>Methinks I should know you, and know this man;<br>Yet I am doubtful, for I am mainly ignorant<br>What place this is; and all the skill I have<br>Remembers not these garments; nor I know not<br>Where I did lodge last [[Night\|night]]. Do not laugh at me,<br>For as I am a man, I think this lady<br>To be my [[Child\|child]], [[Cordelia]]. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | And so I am, I am. |
| LEAR | Be your [[Tears\|tears]] wet? Yes, [[Faith\|faith]]. I pray, weep not.<br>If you have [[Poison\|poison]] for me, I will drink it.<br>I know you do not [[Love\|love]] me; for your sisters<br>Have, as I do remember, done me wrong.<br>You have some cause; they have not. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | No cause, no cause. |
| LEAR | Am I in France? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | In your own kingdom, sir. |
| LEAR | Do not abuse me. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRSTGENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | Be comforted, good madam. The great rage<br>You see is killed in him. [[Desire]] him to go in.<br>Trouble him no more till further settling. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear) Will’t please your highness walk? |
| LEAR | You must bear with me. Pray you now, forget<br>And forgive. I am old and foolish. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |
| LEAR | 5.1 Enter with a drummer and colours Edmond, [[Regan]],<br>Gentlemen, and soldiers |
| EDMOND | Know of the Duke if his last purpose hold,<br>Or whether since he is advised by aught<br>To change the course. He’s full of abdication<br>And [[Self\|self]]-reproving. Bring his constant pleasure. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Our sister’s man is certainly miscarried. |
| EDMOND | ’Tis to be doubted, madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Now, sweet lord,<br>You know the goodness I intend upon you.<br>Tell me but truly—but then speak the [[Truth\|truth]]-<br>Do you not [[Love\|love]] my sister? |
| EDMOND | In honoured [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | But have you never found my brother’s way<br>To the forfended place? |
| EDMOND | No, by mine [[Honour\|honour]], madam. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I never shall endure her. Dear my lord,<br>Be not familiar with her. |
| EDMOND | [[Fear]] me not.<br>She and the Duke her husband— |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter with a drummer and colours the Duke of Albany, [[Goneril]], and soldiers |
| ALBANY | (to [[Regan]])<br>Our very loving sister, [[Well\|well]] bemet.<br>(To Edmond) Sir, this I heard: the [[King]] is come to his<br>daughter,<br>With others whom the rigour of our state<br>Forced to cry out. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why is this reasoned? |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Combine together ’gainst the enemy;<br>For these domestic and particular broils<br>Are not the question here. |
| ALBANY | Let’s then determine with th’ensign of war<br>On our proceeding. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sister, you’ll go with us? |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | No. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | ’Tis most convenient. Pray go with us. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (aside)<br>O ho, I know the [[Riddle\|riddle]]! (To [[Regan]]) I will go. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] disguised as a peasant |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (to Albany)<br>If e’er your grace had speech with man so poor,<br>Hear me one word. |
| ALBANY | (to the others) I’ll overtake you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt both the armies |
| ALBANY | Speak. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Before you fight the battle, ope this letter.<br>If you have victory, let the trumpet sound<br>For him that brought it. Wretched though I seem,<br>I can produce a champion that will prove<br>What is avouchèd there. If you miscarry,<br>Your business of the world hath so an end,<br>And machination ceases. Fortune [[Love\|love]] you. |
| ALBANY | Stay till I have read the letter. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I was forbid it.<br>When [[Time\|time]] shall serve, let but the herald cry,<br>And I’ll appear again. |
| ALBANY | Why, fare thee [[Well\|well]].<br>I will o’erlook thy paper. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Edmond |
| EDMOND | The enemy’s in view; draw up your powers. |
| *(didascalia)* | He offersAlbany a paper |
| EDMOND | Here is the guess of their true strength and forces<br>By diligent discovery; but your haste<br>Is now urged on you. |
| ALBANY | We will greet the [[Time\|time]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| EDMOND | To both these sisters have I sworn my [[Love\|love]],<br>Each jealous of the other as the stung<br>Are of the adder. Which of them shall I take?—<br>Both?—one?—or neither? Neither can be enjoyed<br>If both remain alive. To take the widow<br>Exasperates, makes mad, her sister [[Goneril]],<br>And hardly shall I carry out my side,<br>Her husband being alive. Now then, we’ll use<br>His countenance for the battle, which being done,<br>Let her who would be rid of him devise<br>His speedy taking off. As for the [[Mercy\|mercy]]<br>Which he intends to Lear and to [[Cordelia]],<br>The battle done, and they within our power,<br>Shall never see his pardon; for my state<br>Stands on me to defend, not to [[debate]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |

### Atto 5, Scena 2

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum within. Enter with a drummer and colours [[King Lear]], [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]], and soldiers over the stage; and exeunt. Enter [[Edgar]] disguised as a peasant, guiding the blind [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Here, father, take the [[Shadow\|shadow]] of this tree<br>For your good host; pray that the right may thrive.<br>If ever I return to you again<br>I’ll bring you comfort. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Grace go with you, sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarum and retreat within. Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Away, old man. Give me thy hand. Away.<br>[[King Lear]] hath lost, he and his daughter ta’en.<br>Give me thy hand. Come on. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No further, sir. A man may rot even here. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | What, in ill thoughts again? Men must endure<br>Their going hence even as their coming hither.<br>Ripeness is all. Come on. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | And that’s true, too. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] guiding [[Gloucester]] |

### Atto 5, Scena 3

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter in conquest with a drummer and colours Edmond; [[King Lear]] and [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]] as prisoners; soldiers; a Captain |
| EDMOND | Some officers take them away. Good guard<br>Until their greater pleasures first be known<br>That are to censure them. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear)<br>We are not the first<br>Who with best meaning have incurred the worst.<br>For thee, oppressed [[King]], I am cast down,<br>Myself could else outfrown false fortune’s frown.<br>Shall we not see these daughters and these sisters? |
| LEAR | No, no, no, no. Come, let’s away to [[Prison\|prison]].<br>We two [[Alone\|alone]] will sing like [[Birds\|birds]] i’th’ cage.<br>When thou dost ask me blessing, I’ll kneel down<br>And ask of thee [[Forgiveness\|forgiveness]]; so we’ll live,<br>And pray, and sing, and tell old tales, and laugh<br>At gilded butterflies, and hear poor rogues<br>Talk of [[Court\|court]] news, and we’ll talk with them too—<br>Who loses and who wins, who’s in, who’s out,<br>And take upon ’s the mystery of things<br>As if we were God’s spies; and we’ll wear out<br>In a walled [[Prison\|prison]] packs and sects of great ones<br>That ebb and flow by th’ [[Moon\|moon]]. |
| EDMOND | (to soldiers)<br>Take them away. |
| LEAR | Upon such sacrifices, my [[Cordelia]],<br>The gods themselves throw incense. Have I caught<br>thee?<br>He that parts us shall bring a brand from heaven<br>And [[Fire\|fire]] us hence like foxes. Wipe thine eyes.<br>The goodyear shall devour them, flesh and fell,<br>Ere they shall make us weep. We’ll see ’em starved<br>first. Come. Exeunt all but Edmond and the Captain |
| EDMOND | Come hither, captain. Hark.<br>Take thou this note. Go follow them to [[Prison\|prison]].<br>One step I have advanced thee; if thou dost<br>As this instructs thee, thou dost make thy way<br>To noble fortunes. Know thou this: that men<br>Are as the [[Time\|time]] is. To be tender-minded<br>Does not become a [[Sword\|sword]]. Thy great employment<br>Will not bear question. Either say thou‘It do’t,<br>Or thrive by other means. |
| CAPTAIN | I’ll do’t, my lord. |
