| EDMOND | About it, and write ‘happy’ when thou’st done.<br>Mark, I say, instantly, and carry it so<br>As I have set it down. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the Captain |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter the Duke of Albany, [[Goneril]], [[Regan]], drummer, trumpeter |
| EDMOND | and soldiers |
| ALBANY | Sir, you have showed today your valiant strain,<br>And fortune led you [[Well\|well]]. You have the captives<br>Who were the opposites of this day’s strife.<br>I do require them of you, so to use them<br>As we shall find their merits and our safety<br>May equally determine. |
| EDMOND | Sir, I thought it fit<br>To send the old and miserable [[King]]<br>To some retention and appointed guard,<br>Whose age had charms in it, whose title more,<br>To pluck the common bosom on his side<br>And turn our impressed lances in our eyes<br>Which do command them. With him I sent the [[Queen]],<br>My reason all the same, and they are ready<br>Tomorrow, or at further space, t’appear<br>Where you shall hold your session. |
| ALBANY | Sir, by your patience,<br>I hold you but a subject of this war,<br>Not as a brother. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | That’s as we list to grace him.<br>Methinks our pleasure might have been demanded<br>Ere you had spoke so far. He led our powers,<br>Bore the commission of my place and person,<br>The which immediacy may [[Well\|well]] stand up<br>And call itself your brother. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Not so hot.<br>In his own grace he doth exalt himself<br>More than in your addition. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | In my rights<br>By me invested, he compeers the best. |
| ALBANY | That were the most if he should husband you. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Jesters do oft prove prophets. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Holla, holla—<br>That eye that told you so looked but asquint. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Lady, I am not [[Well\|well]], else I should answer<br>From a full-flowing stomach. (To Edmond) General,<br>Take thou my soldiers, prisoners, patrimony.<br>Dispose of them, of me. The walls is thine.<br>Witness the world that I create thee here<br>My lord and master. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Mean you to enjoy him? |
| ALBANY | The let-[[Alone\|alone]] lies not in your good will. |
| EDMOND | Nor in thine, lord. |
| ALBANY | Half-blooded fellow, yes. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | (to Edmond)<br>Let the drum strike and prove my title thine. |
| ALBANY | Stay yet, hear reason. Edmond, I arrest thee<br>On capital treason, and in thy attaint<br>This gilded serpent. (To [[Regan]]) For your claim, fair<br>sister,<br>I bar it in the interest of my wife.<br>’Tis she is subcontracted to this lord,<br>And I, her husband, contradict your banns.<br>If you will marry, make your loves to me.<br>My lady is bespoke. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | An interlude! |
| ALBANY | Thou art armed, [[Gloucester]]. Let the trumpet sound.<br>If none appear to prove upon thy person<br>Thy heinous, manifest, and many treasons,<br>There is my pledge. |
| *(didascalia)* | He |
| ALBANY | throws down a |
| *(didascalia)* | glove |
| ALBANY | I’ll make it on thy heart,<br>Ere I taste bread, thou art in nothing less<br>Than I have here proclaimed thee. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sick, O sick! |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (aside) If not, I’ll ne’er trust medicine. |
| *(didascalia)* | EDMOND (to Albany, throwing down a glovel |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | There’s my exchange. What in the world he is<br>That names me traitor, [[Villain\|villain]]-like he lies.<br>Call by the trumpet. He that dares, approach;<br>On him, on you,—who not?—I will maintain<br>My [[Truth\|truth]] and [[Honour\|honour]] firmly. |
| ALBANY | A herald, ho! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Herald |
| ALBANY | (To Edmond) Trust to thy single virtue, for thy soldiers,<br>All levied in my name, have in my name<br>Took their discharge. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | My sickness grows upon me. |
| ALBANY | She is not [[Well\|well]]. Convey her to my tent. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more with [[Regan]] |
| ALBANY | Come hither, herald. Let the trumpet sound,<br>And read out this.<br>A trumpet sounds |
| HERALD | (reads) ‘If any man of quality or degree within the lists of the army will maintain upon Edmond, supposed Earl of [[Gloucester]], that he is a manifold traitor, let him appear by the third sound of the trumpet. He is bold in his defence.’<br>First trumpet<br>Again.<br>Second trumpet<br>Again.<br>Third trumpet. |
| *(didascalia)* | Trumpet answers within. Enter [[Edgar]], armed |
| ALBANY | (to the Herald)<br>Ask him his purposes, why he appears<br>Upon this call o’th’ trumpet. |
| HERALD | (to [[Edgar]])<br>What are you?<br>Your name, your quality, and why you answer<br>This present summons? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Know, my name is lost,<br>By treason’s tooth bare-gnawn and canker-bit.<br>Yet am I noble as the adversary<br>I come to cope. |
| ALBANY | Which is that adversary? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | What’s he that speaks for Edmond, Earl of [[Gloucester]]? |
| EDMOND | Himself. What sayst thou to him? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Draw thy [[Sword\|sword]],<br>That if my speech offend a noble heart<br>Thy arm may do thee justice. Here is mine.<br>He draws his [[Sword\|sword]]<br>Behold, it is the privilege of mine [[Honour\|honour]],<br>My oath, and my profession. I protest,<br>Maugre thy strength, place, youth, and eminence,<br>Despite thy victor-[[Sword\|sword]] and [[Fire\|fire]]-new fortune,<br>Thy valour and thy heart, thou art a traitor,<br>False to thy gods, thy brother, and thy father,<br>Conspirant ‘gainst this high illustrious prince,<br>And from th’extremest upward of thy head<br>To the descent and [[Dust\|dust]] below thy foot<br>A most toad-spotted traitor. Say thou no,<br>This [[Sword\|sword]], this arm, and my best spirits are bent<br>To prove upon thy heart, whereto I speak,<br>Thou liest. |
| EDMOND | In wisdom I should ask thy name,<br>But since thy outside looks so fair and warlike,<br>And that thy tongue some say of breeding breathes,<br>What safe and nicely I might [[Well\|well]] demand<br>By rule of knighthood I disdain and spurn.<br>Back do I toss those treasons to thy head,<br>With the hell-hated lie o’erwhelm thy heart,<br>Which, for they yet glance by and scarcely bruise,<br>This [[Sword\|sword]] of mine shall give them instant way<br>Where they shall rest for ever. Trumpets, speak! |
| *(didascalia)* | Alarums. They fight. Edmond is vanquished |
| *(didascalia)* | ALL |
| EDMOND | Save him, save him! |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | This is practice, [[Gloucester]].<br>By th’ law of arms thou wast not bound to answer<br>An unknown opposite. Thou art not vanquished,<br>But cozened and beguiled. |
| ALBANY | Shut your mouth, dame,<br>Or with this paper shall I stopple it. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Edmond Hold, sir, thou worse than any name: |
| ALBANY | read thine own evil.<br>(To [[Goneril]]) No tearing, lady. I perceive you know it. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Say if I do, the laws are mine, not thine.<br>Who can arraign me for’t? |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| ALBANY | Most monstrous!—<br>O, know’st thou this paper? |
| EDMOND | Ask me not what I know. |
| ALBANY | Go after her. She’s desperate. Govern her. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit one or more |
| EDMOND | What you have charged me with, that have I done,<br>And more, much more. The [[Time\|time]] will bring it out.<br>‘Tis past, and so am I. (To [[Edgar]]) But what art thou,<br>That hast this fortune on me? If thou’rt noble,<br>I do forgive thee. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Let’s exchange [[Charity\|charity]].<br>I am no less in [[Blood\|blood]] than thou art, Edmond.<br>If more, the more thou’st wronged me. |
| *(didascalia)* | He |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | takes off his |
| *(didascalia)* | helmet |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | My name is [[Edgar]], and thy father’s son.<br>The gods are just, and of our pleasant vices<br>Make instruments to plague us.<br>The dark and vicious place where thee he got<br>Cost him his eyes. |
| EDMOND | Thou‘st spoken right. ’Tis true.<br>The [[Wheel\|wheel]] is come full circle. I am here. |
| ALBANY | (to [[Edgar]])<br>Methought thy very gait did prophesy<br>A royal nobleness. I must embrace thee.<br>Let sorrow split my heart if ever I<br>Did hate thee or thy father. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Worthy prince, I know’t. |
| ALBANY | Where have you hid yourself?<br>How have you known the miseries of your father? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | By nursing them, my lord. List a brief tale,<br>And when ‘tis told, O that my heart would burst!<br>The bloody proclamation to escape<br>That followed me so near—O, our lives’ sweetness,<br>That we the [[Pain\|pain]] of [[Death\|death]] would hourly die<br>Rather than die at once!—taught me to shift<br>Into a madman’s rags, t’assume a semblance<br>That very [[dogs]] disdained; and in this habit<br>Met I my father with his bleeding rings,<br>Their precious stones new-lost; became his guide,<br>Led him, begged for him, saved him from [[Despair\|despair]];<br>Never—O fault!—revealed myself unto him<br>Until some half hour past, when I was armed.<br>Not sure, though hoping, of this good [[success]],<br>I asked his blessing, and from first to last<br>Told him our pilgrimage; but his flawed heart—<br>Alack, too weak the conflict to support—<br>’Twixt two extremes of passion, joy and grief,<br>Burst smilingly. |
| EDMOND | This speech of yours hath moved me,<br>And shall perchance do good. But speak you on—<br>You look as you had something more to say. |
| ALBANY | If there be more, more woeful, hold it in,<br>For I am almost ready to dissolve,<br>Hearing of this. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Gentleman with a bloody knife |
| GENTLEMAN | Help, help, O help! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | What kind of help? |
| ALBANY | Speak, man. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | What means this bloody knife? |
| GENTLEMAN | ’Tis hot, it smokes.<br>It came even from the heart of—O, she’s dead! |
| ALBANY | Who dead? Speak, man. |
| GENTLEMAN | Your lady, sir, your lady; and her sister<br>By her is poisoned. She confesses it. |
| EDMOND | I was contracted to them both; all three<br>Now marry in an instant. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Here comes [[Kent]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]] as himself |
| ALBANY | Produce the bodies, be they alive or dead.<br>[[Goneril]]’s and [[Regan]]’s bodies brought out<br>This judgement of the heavens, that makes us tremble,<br>Touches us not with pity.—O, is this he?<br>(To [[Kent]]) The [[Time\|time]] will not allow the compliment<br>Which very manners urges. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I am come<br>To bid my [[King\|king]] and master aye good [[Night\|night]].<br>Is he not here? |
| ALBANY | Great thing of us forgot!—<br>Speak, Edmond; where’s the [[King]], and where’s<br>[[Cordelia]]?-<br>Seest thou this object, [[Kent]]? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Alack, why thus? |
| EDMOND | Yet Edmond was beloved.<br>The one the other poisoned for my sake,<br>And after slew herself. |
| ALBANY | Even so.—Cover their faces. |
| EDMOND | I pant for life. Some good I mean to do,<br>Despite of mine own [[Nature\|nature]]. Quickly send,<br>Be brief in it, to th’ [[Castle\|castle]]; for my writ<br>Is on the life of Lear and on [[Cordelia]].<br>Nay, send in [[Time\|time]]. |
| ALBANY | Run, run, O run! |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | To who, my lord?—Who has the office? Send Thy token of reprieve. |
| EDMOND | [[Well]] thought on! Take my [[Sword\|sword]]. The captain, Give it the captain. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Haste thee for thy life. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit the Gentleman |
| EDMOND | (to Albany)<br>He hath commission from thy wife and me<br>To hang [[Cordelia]] in the [[Prison\|prison]], and<br>To lay the blame upon her own [[Despair\|despair]],<br>That she fordid herself. |
| ALBANY | The gods defend her!—Bear him hence a while. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some with Edmond |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] with [[Queen]] [[Cordelia]] in his arms, |
| *(didascalia)* | followed by the Gentleman |
| LEAR | Howl, howl, howl, howl! O, you are men of stones.<br>Had I your tongues and eyes, I’d use them so<br>That heaven’s vault should crack. She’s gone for ever.<br>I know when one is dead and when one lives.<br>She’s dead as earth. |
| *(didascalia)* | He lays her down |
| LEAR | Lend me a looking-glass.<br>If that her breath will mist or stain the stone,<br>Why, then she lives. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Is this the promised end? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Or image of that horror? |
| ALBANY | Fall and cease. |
| LEAR | This feather stirs. She lives. If it be so,<br>It is a chance which does redeem all sorrows<br>That ever I have felt. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent\|KENT]] kneeling |
| LEAR | O, my good master! |
| LEAR | Prithee, away. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | ’Tis noble [[Kent]], your friend. |
| LEAR | A plague upon you, murderers, traitors all.<br>I might have saved her; now she’s gone for ever.-<br>[[Cordelia]], [[Cordelia]]: stay a little. Ha?<br>What is’t thou sayst?—Her voice was ever soft,<br>Gentle, and low, an excellent thing in woman.—<br>I killed the slave that was a-hanging thee. |
| GENTLEMAN | ’Tis true, my lords, he did. |
| LEAR | Did I not, fellow?<br>I have seen the day with my good biting falchion<br>I would have made them skip. I am old now,<br>And these same crosses spoil me. (To [[Kent]]) Who are<br>you?<br>Mine eyes are not o’th’ best, I’ll tell you straight. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | If fortune brag of two she loved and hated,<br>One of them we behold. |
| LEAR | This’ a dull sight.<br>Are you not [[Kent]]? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | The same, your servant [[Kent]].<br>Where is your servant Caius? |
| LEAR | He’s a good fellow, I can tell you that.<br>He’ll strike, and quickly too. He’s dead and rotten. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, my good lord, I am the very man— |
| LEAR | I’ll see that straight. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | That from your first of difference and [[Decay\|decay]]<br>Have followed your sad steps. |
| LEAR | You’re welcome hither. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Nor no man else. All’s cheerless, dark, and deadly.<br>Your eldest daughters have fordone themselves,<br>And desperately are dead. |
| LEAR | Ay, so think I. |
| ALBANY | He knows not what he says; and vain is it<br>That we present us to him. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter a Messenger |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Very bootless. |
| MESSENGER | (to Albany)<br>Edmond is dead, my lord. |
| ALBANY | That’s but a trifle here.—<br>You lords and noble friends, know our intent.<br>What comfort to this great [[Decay\|decay]] may come<br>Shall be applied; for us, we will resign<br>During the life of this old majesty<br>To him our absolute power;<br>(To [[Edgar]] and [[Kent]]) you to your rights,<br>With boot and such addition as your honours<br>Have more than merited. All friends shall taste<br>The wages of their virtue, and all foes<br>The cup of their deservings.—O see, see! |
| LEAR | And my poor [[Fool\|fool]] is hanged. No, no, no life?<br>Why should a dog, a [[Horse\|horse]], a rat have life,<br>And thou no breath at all? Thou’lt come no more.<br>Never, never, never, never, never. |
| *(didascalia)* | To [[Kent]] Pray you, undo this button. Thank you, sir. |
| LEAR | Do you see this? Look on her. Look, her lips.<br>Look there, look there. He dies |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | He faints. (To Lear) My lord, my lord! |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Kent\|KENT]] to Lear |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Break, heart, I prithee break. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | (to Lear)<br>Look up, my lord. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Vex not his [[Ghost\|ghost]]. O, let him pass. He hates him<br>That would upon the rack of this tough world<br>Stretch him out longer. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | He is gone indeed. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | The [[Wonder\|wonder]] is he hath endured so long.<br>He but usurped his life. |
| ALBANY | Bear them from hence. Our present business<br>Is general woe. (To [[Edgar]] and [[Kent]]) Friends of my<br>[[Soul\|soul]], you twain<br>Rule in this realm, and the gored state sustain. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I have a [[Journey\|journey]], sir, shortly to go:<br>My master calls me; I must not say no. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The weight of this sad [[Time\|time]] we must obey,<br>Speak what we feel, not what we ought to say.<br>The oldest hath borne most. We that are young<br>Shall never see so much, nor live so long. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt with a dead march, carrying the bodies |
| OUR | first reference to [[Cymbeline]] is a note by the astrologer Simon Forman that he saw the play, probably not long before his [[Death\|death]] on 8 September 1611. He refers to the heroine as ‘[[Innogen]]’, and this name occurs in the sources; the form ‘Imogen’, found only in the Folio, appears to be a misprint. The play’s courtly tone, and the [[Masque\|masque]]-like quality of, particularly, the episode (5.5.186.1-2) in which Jupiter ‘descends in thunder and [[Lightning\|lightning]], sitting upon an eagle’ and ‘throws a thunderbolt’, suggests that as Shakespeare wrote he may have had in mind the audiences and the stage equipment of the Blackfriars theatre, which his company used from the [[Autumn\|autumn]] of 1609; and stylistic evidence places the play in about 1610-11. It was first printed in the 1623 Folio, as the last of the tragedies. In fact it is a [[Tragicomedy\|tragicomedy]], or a [[Romance\|romance]], telling a complex and implausible tale of events which cause the deaths of certain subsidiary characters ([[Cloten]], and the [[Queen]]) and bring major characters (including the heroine, [[Innogen]]) close to [[Death\|death]], but which are miraculously resolved in the reunions and reconciliations of the closing scene.<br>Shakespeare’s plot reflects a wide range of reading. He took his title and setting from the name and reign of the legendary British [[King\|king]] [[Cymbeline]], or Cunobelinus, said to have reigned from 33 BC till shortly after the birth of [[Christ]]. [[Cymbeline]] is no chronicle [[history]], but Shakespeare derived some ideas, and many of his characters’ names, from accounts of early British [[history]] in Holinshed’s Chronicles and elsewhere. Drawing partially, it seems, on an old play, The Rare Triumphs of [[Love]] and Fortune (acted 1582, printed 1589), he gives [[Cymbeline]] a daughter, [[Innogen]], and a wicked second [[Queen]] with a loutish, vicious son, [[Cloten]], whom she wishes to see on the throne in her husband’s place. [[Cymbeline]], disapproving of his daughter’s [[Marriage\|marriage]] to ‘a poor but worthy gentleman’, [[Posthumus]] Leonatus, banishes him. The strand of plot showing the outcome of a wager that [[Posthumus]], in Rome, lays on his wife’s chastity is indebted, directly or indirectly, to Boccaccio’s Decameron. Another old play, Sir Clyomon and Clamydes (printed in 1599), may have suggested the bizarre scene (4.2) in which [[Innogen]] mistakes [[Cloten]]’s headless [[Body\|body]] for that of [[Posthumus]]; and IIolinshed’s Ilistory of Scotland supplied the episode in which [[Cymbeline]]’s two sons, [[Guiderius]] and [[Arviragus]], helped only by the old man ([[Belarius]]) who has brought them up in the wilds of Wales, defeat the entire Roman army.<br>The tone of [[Cymbeline]] has puzzled commentators. Its prose and verse style is frequently ornate, sometimes [[Grotesque\|grotesque]]. Its characterization often seems deliberately artificial. Extremes are violently juxtaposed, most daringly when [[Innogen]], supposed dead, is laid beside [[Cloten]]’s headless [[Body\|body]]: the [[Beauty\|beauty]] of the verse in which she is mourned, and of the [[Flowers\|flowers]] strewn over the bodies, contrasts with the hideous spectacle of the headless corpse; her waking speech is one of Shakespeare’s most thrillingly difficult challenges to his performers. The appearance of Jupiter lifts the action to a new level of even greater implausibility, preparing us for the extraordinary series of revelations by which the play advances to its impossibly happy ending. [[Cymbeline]] has been valued mostly for its portrayal of [[Innogen]], ideal of womanhood to, especially, Victorian readers and theatre-goers. The play as a whole is a fantasy, an experimental exercise in virtuosity. |
| THE PERSONS OF THE | PLAY<br>[[Cymbeline\|CYMBELINE]], [[King]] of Britain<br>Princess [[Innogen\|INNOGEN]], his daughter, later disguised as a man named Fidele<br>[[Queen\|QUEEN]], [[Cymbeline]]’s wife, [[Innogen]]’s stepmother<br>Lord [[Cloten\|CLOTEN]], her son<br><br>[[Belarius\|BELARIUS]], a banished lord, calling himself Morgan<br>CORNELIUS, a physician<br>[[Helen\|HELEN]], a lady attending on [[Innogen]]<br>Two LORDS attending on [[Cloten]]<br>Two GENTLEMEN<br>Two British CAPTAINS<br>Two JAILERS |
| [[Posthumus\|POSTHUMUS]] | Leonatus, a poor gentleman, [[Innogen]]’s husband<br>PISANIO, his servant<br>FILARIO, a friend of [[Posthumus]]<br>Caius [[Lucius\|LUCIUS]], ambassador from Rome, later General of the Roman forces<br>Two Roman SENATORS<br>Roman TRIBUNES<br>A Roman CAPTAIN<br>Philharmonus, a SOOTHSAYER |
| JUPITER | [[Ghost]] of SICILIUS Leonatus, father of [[Posthumus]]<br>[[Ghost]] of the MOTHER of [[Posthumus]]<br>[[Ghosts]] of the BROTHERS of [[Posthumus]]<br><br>Lords attending on [[Cymbeline]], ladies attending on the [[Queen]], musicians attending on [[Cloten]], messengers, soldiers |




<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_1" data-title="The Tragedy of King Lear (Folio Text) — Act I, Scene 1" data-chapter="Scene 1" data-kind="scene" data-tags="topos/quest,archetype/child,archetype/king,motif/blood,motif/death,motif/grave,motif/leaves,motif/love,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/spring,motif/time,motif/well,concept/beauty,concept/faith,concept/freedom,concept/history,concept/honour,concept/nature,concept/pride,concept/self,concept/truth,form/history,form/tragedy,setting/court,character/apollo,character/cordelia,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/gloucester,character/goneril,character/kent,character/king_lear,character/king_of_france,character/queen,character/regan"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and Edmond |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I thought the [[King]] had more affected the Duke of Albany than Cornwall.<br>[[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]) It did always seem so to us, but now in the division of the kingdom it appears not which of the Dukes he values most; for qualities are so weighed that curiosity in neither can make choice of either’s moiety. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Is not this your son, my lord? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | His breeding, sir, hath been at my charge. I have so often blushed to acknowledge him that now I am brazed to’t. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I cannot conceive you. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sir, this young fellow’s mother could, whereupon she grew round-wombed and had indeed, sir, a son for her cradle ere she had a husband for her bed. Do you smell a fault? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I cannot wish the fault undone, the issue of it being so proper. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But I have a son, sir, by order of law, some year older than this, who yet is no dearer in my account. Though this knave came something saucily to the world before he was sent for, yet was his mother fair, there was good sport at his making, and the whoreson must be acknowledged. (To Edmond) Do you know this noble gentleman, Edmond? |
| EDMOND | No, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Edmond) My lord of [[Kent]]. Remember him hereafter as my honourable friend. |
| EDMOND | (to [[Kent]]) My services to your lordship. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I must [[Love\|love]] you, and sue to know you better. |
| EDMOND | Sir, I shall study deserving. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to [[Kent]]) He hath been out nine years, and away he shall again. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sennet |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is coming. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], the Dukes of Cornwall and Albany, [[Goneril]], [[Regan]], [[Cordelia]], and attendants |
| LEAR | Attend the lords of France and Burgundy, [[Gloucester]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I shall, my lord. Exit |
| LEAR | Meantime we shall express our darker purpose.<br>Give me the map there. Know that we have divided<br>In three our kingdom, and ’tis our fast intent<br>To shake all cares and business from our age,<br>Conferring them on younger strengths while we<br>Unburdened crawl toward [[Death\|death]]. Our son of Cornwall,<br>And you, our no less loving son of Albany,<br>We have this hour a constant will to publish<br>Our daughters’ several dowers, that future strife<br>May be prevented now. The princes France and<br>Burgundy—<br>Great rivals in our youngest daughter’s [[Love\|love]]—<br>Long in our [[Court\|court]] have made their amorous sojourn,<br>And here are to be answered. Tell me, my daughters—<br>Since now we will divest us both of rule,<br>Interest of territory, cares of state—<br>Which of you shall we say doth [[Love\|love]] us most,<br>That we our largest bounty may extend<br>Where [[Nature\|nature]] doth with merit challenge? [[Goneril]],<br>Our eldest born, speak first. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Sir, I [[Love\|love]] you more than words can wield the matter;<br>Dearer than eyesight, space, and liberty;<br>Beyond what can be valued, rich or rare,<br>No less than life; with grace, health, [[Beauty\|beauty]], [[Honour\|honour]];<br>As much as [[Child\|child]] e’er loved or father found;<br>A [[Love\|love]] that makes breath poor and speech unable.<br>Beyond all manner of so much I [[Love\|love]] you. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (aside)<br>What shall [[Cordelia]] speak? [[Love]] and be silent. |
| LEAR | (to [[Goneril]])<br>Of all these bounds even from this line to this,<br>With shadowy forests and with champaigns riched,<br>With plenteous rivers and wide-skirted meads,<br>We make thee lady. To thine and Albany’s issues<br>Be this perpetual.—What says our second daughter?<br>Our dearest [[Regan]], wife of Cornwall? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I am made of that [[Self\|self]] mettle as my sister,<br>And prize me at her worth. In my true heart<br>I find she names my very deed of [[Love\|love]]—<br>Only she comes too short, that I profess<br>Myself an enemy to all other joys<br>Which the most precious square of sense possesses,<br>And find I am [[Alone\|alone]] felicitate<br>In your dear highness’ [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (aside) Then poor [[Cordelia]]—<br>And yet not so, since I am sure my [[Love\|love]]’s<br>More ponderous than my tongue. |
| LEAR | (to [[Regan]])<br>To thee and thine hereditary ever<br>Remain this ample third of our fair kingdom,<br>No less in space, validity, and pleasure<br>Than that conferred on [[Goneril]]. (To [[Cordelia]]) Now our<br>joy,<br>Although our last and least, to whose young [[Love\|love]]<br>The vines of France and milk of Burgundy<br>Strive to be interessed: what can you say to draw<br>A third more opulent than your sisters? Speak. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Nothing, my lord. |
| LEAR | Nothing? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Nothing. |
| LEAR | Nothing will come of nothing. Speak again. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Unhappy that I am, I cannot heave<br>My heart into my mouth. I [[Love\|love]] your majesty<br>According to my bond, no more nor less. |
| LEAR | How, how, [[Cordelia]]? Mend your speech a little<br>Lest you may mar your fortunes. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Good my lord,<br>You have begot me, bred me, loved me.<br>I return those duties back as are right fit-<br>Obey you, [[Love\|love]] you, and most [[Honour\|honour]] you.<br>Why have my sisters husbands if they say<br>They [[Love\|love]] you all? Haply when I shall wed<br>That lord whose hand must take my plight shall carry<br>Half my [[Love\|love]] with him, half my care and duty.<br>Sure, I shall never marry like my sisters. |
| LEAR | But goes thy heart with this? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| LEAR | So young and so untender? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | So young, my lord, and true. |
| LEAR | Let it be so. Thy [[Truth\|truth]] then be thy dower;<br>For by the sacred radiance of the sun,<br>The mysteries of Hecate and the [[Night\|night]],<br>By all the operation of the orbs<br>From whom we do exist and cease to be,<br>Here I disclaim all my paternal care,<br>Propinquity, and property of [[Blood\|blood]],<br>And as a stranger to my heart and me<br>Hold thee from this for ever. The barbarous Scythian,<br>Or he that makes his generation messes<br>To gorge his appetite, shall to my bosom<br>Be as [[Well\|well]] neighboured, pitied, and relieved<br>As thou, my sometime daughter. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good my liege— |
| LEAR | Peace, [[Kent]].<br>Come not between the dragon and his wrath.<br>I loved her most, and thought to set my rest |
| *(didascalia)* | On her kind nursery. To [[Cordelia]] Hence, and avoid |
| LEAR | my sight!—<br>So be my [[Grave\|grave]] my peace as here I give<br>Her father’s heart from her. Call France. Who stirs?<br>Call Burgundy. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | one or |
| *(didascalia)* | more |
| LEAR | Cornwall and Albany,<br>With my two daughters’ dowers digest the third.<br>Let [[Pride\|pride]], which she calls plainness, marry her.<br>I do invest you jointly with my power,<br>Pre-eminence, and all the large effects<br>That troop with majesty. Ourself by monthly course,<br>With reservation of an hundred knights<br>By you to be sustained, shall our abode<br>Make with you by due turn. Only we shall retain<br>The name and all th’addition to a [[King\|king]]. The sway,<br>Revenue, execution of the rest,<br>Beloved sons, be yours; which to confirm,<br>This crownet part between you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Royal Lear,<br>Whom I have ever honoured as my [[King\|king]],<br>Loved as my father, as my master followed,<br>As my great patron thought on in my prayers— |
| LEAR | The bow is bent and drawn; make from the shaft. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Let it fall rather, though the fork invade<br>The region of my heart. Be [[Kent]] unmannerly<br>When Lear is mad. What wouldst thou do, old man?<br>Think’st thou that duty shall have dread to speak<br>When power to flattery bows? To plainness [[Honour\|honour]]’s<br>bound<br>When majesty falls to folly. Reserve thy state,<br>And in thy best consideration check<br>This hideous rashness. Answer my life my judgement,<br>Thy youngest daughter does not [[Love\|love]] thee least,<br>Nor are those empty-hearted whose low sounds<br>Reverb no hollowness. |
| LEAR | [[Kent]], on thy life, no more! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My life I never held but as a pawn<br>To wage against thine enemies, ne’er feared to lose it,<br>Thy safety being motive. |
| LEAR | Out of my sight! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | See better, Lear, and let me still remain<br>The true blank of thine eye. |
| LEAR | Now, by [[Apollo]]— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Now, by [[Apollo]], [[King]], thou swear’st thy gods in vain. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR making to strike him |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | O vassal! Miscreant! |
| *(didascalia)* | ALBANY and [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] Dear sir, forbear. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (to Lear)<br>Kill thy physician, and thy fee bestow<br>Upon the foul disease. Revoke thy gift,<br>Or whilst I can vent clamour from my throat<br>I’ll tell thee thou dost evil. |
| LEAR | Hear me, recreant; on thine allegiance hear me!<br>That thou hast sought to make us break our vows,<br>Which we durst never yet, and with strained [[Pride\|pride]]<br>To come betwixt our sentence and our power,<br>Which nor our [[Nature\|nature]] nor our place can bear,<br>Our potency made good take thy reward:<br>Five days we do allot thee for provision<br>To shield thee from disasters of the world,<br>And on the sixth to turn thy hated back<br>Upon our kingdom. If on the seventh day following<br>Thy banished trunk be found in our dominions,<br>The moment is thy [[Death\|death]]. Away! By Jupiter,<br>This shall not be revoked. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fare thee [[Well\|well]], [[King]]; sith thus thou wilt appear,<br>[[Freedom]] lives hence, and banishment is here.<br>(To [[Cordelia]]) The gods to their dear shelter take thee,<br>maid,<br>That justly think’st, and hast most rightly said.<br>(To [[Goneril]] and [[Regan]]) And your large speeches may<br>your deeds approve,<br>That good effects may [[spring]] from words of [[Love\|love]].<br>Thus [[Kent]], O princes, bids you all adieu;<br>He’ll shape his old course in a country new. Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] with the |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | [[King of France]], the Duke of Burgundy, and attendants |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Here’s France and Burgundy, my noble lord. |
| LEAR | My lord of Burgundy,<br>We first address toward you, who with this [[King]]<br>Hath rivalled for our daughter: what in the least<br>Will you require in present dower with her<br>Or cease your [[Quest\|quest]] of [[Love\|love]]? |
| BURGUNDY | Most royal majesty,<br>I crave no more than hath your highness offered;<br>Nor will you tender less. |
| LEAR | Right noble Burgundy,<br>When she was dear to us we did hold her so;<br>But now her price is fallen. Sir, there she stands.<br>If aught within that little seeming substance,<br>Or all of it, with our displeasure pieced,<br>And nothing more, may fitly like your grace,<br>She’s there, and she is yours. |
| BURGUNDY | I know no answer. |
| LEAR | Will you with those infirmities she owes,<br>Unfriended, new adopted to our hate,<br>Dowered with our curse and strangered with our oath,<br>Take her or leave her? |
| BURGUNDY | Pardon me, royal sir.<br>Election makes not up in such conditions. |
| LEAR | Then leave her, sir; for by the power that made me,<br>I tell you all her wealth. (To France) For you, great [[King]],<br>I would not from your [[Love\|love]] make such a stray<br>To match you where I hate, therefore beseech you<br>T‘avert your liking a more worthier way<br>Than on a wretch whom [[Nature\|nature]] is ashamed<br>Almost t’acknowledge hers. |
| FRANCE | This is most strange,<br>That she whom even but now was your best object,<br>The argument of your praise, balm of your age,<br>The best, the dear’st, should in this trice of [[Time\|time]]<br>Commit a thing so monstrous to dismantle<br>So many folds of favour. Sure, her offence<br>Must be of such unnatural degree<br>That monsters it, or your fore-vouched affection<br>Fall into taint; which to believe of her<br>Must be a [[Faith\|faith]] that reason without miracle<br>Should never plant in me. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (to Lear)<br>I yet beseech your majesty,<br>If for I want that glib and oily art<br>To speak and purpose not—since what I [[Well\|well]] intend,<br>I’ll do’t before I speak—that you make known<br>It is no vicious blot, murder, or foulness,<br>No unchaste action or dishonoured step<br>That hath deprived me of your grace and favour,<br>But even the want of that for which I am richer—<br>A still-soliciting eye, and such a tongue<br>That I am glad I have not, though not to have it<br>Hath lost me in your liking. |
| LEAR | Better thou<br>Hadst not been born than not t’have pleased me better. |
| FRANCE | Is it but this—a tardiness in [[Nature\|nature]],<br>Which often [[Leaves\|leaves]] the [[history]] unspoke<br>That it intends to do?—My lord of Burgundy,<br>What say you to the lady? [[Love]]’s not [[Love\|love]]<br>When it is mingled with regards that stands<br>Aloof from th’entire point. Will you have her?<br>She is herself a dowry. |
| BURGUNDY | (to Lear) Royal [[King]],<br>Give but that portion which yourself proposed,<br>And here I take [[Cordelia]] by the hand,<br>Duchess of Burgundy. |
| LEAR | Nothing. I have sworn. I am firm. |
| BURGUNDY | (to [[Cordelia]])<br>I am sorry, then, you have so lost a father<br>That you must lose a husband. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Peace be with Burgundy;<br>Since that respect and fortunes are his [[Love\|love]],<br>I shall not be his wife. |
| FRANCE | Fairest [[Cordelia]], that art most rich, being poor;<br>Most choice, forsaken; and most loved, despised:<br>Thee and thy virtues here I seize upon.<br>Be it lawful, I take up what’s cast away.<br>Gods, gods! ‘Tis strange that from their cold’st neglect<br>My [[Love\|love]] should kindle to inflamed respect.—<br>Thy dowerless daughter, [[King]], thrown to my chance,<br>Is [[Queen\|queen]] of us, of ours, and our fair France.<br>Not all the dukes of wat’rish Burgundy<br>Can buy this unprized precious maid of me.—<br>Bid them farewell, [[Cordelia]], though unkind.<br>Thou losest here, a better where to find. |
| LEAR | Thou hast her, France. Let her be thine, for we<br>Have no such daughter, nor shall ever see<br>That face of hers again. Therefore be gone,<br>Without our grace, our [[Love\|love]], our benison.—<br>Come, noble Burgundy. Flourish. Exeunt all but France<br><br>and the sisters |
| FRANCE | Bid farewell to your sisters. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Ye jewels of our father, with washed eyes<br>[[Cordelia]] [[Leaves\|leaves]] you. I know you what you are,<br>And like a sister am most loath to call<br>Your faults as they are named. [[Love]] [[Well\|well]] our father.<br>To your professed bosoms I commit him.<br>But yet, alas, stood I within his grace<br>I would prefer him to a better place.<br>So farewell to you both. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Prescribe not us our duty. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Let your study<br>Be to content your lord, who hath received you<br>At fortune’s alms. You have obedience scanted,<br>And [[Well\|well]] are worth the want that you have wanted. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | [[Time]] shall unfold what pleated cunning hides,<br>Who covert faults at last with shame derides.<br>[[Well]] may you prosper. |
| FRANCE | Come, my fair [[Cordelia]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt France and [[Cordelia]] |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Sister, it is not little I have to say of what most nearly appertains to us both. I think our father will hence tonight. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | That’s most certain, and with you. Next month with us. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | You see how full of changes his age is. The observation we have made of it hath been little. He always loved our sister most, and with what poor judgement he hath now cast her off appears too grossly. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | ’Tis the infirmity of his age; yet he hath ever but slenderly known himself. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | The best and soundest of his [[Time\|time]] hath been but rash; then must we look from his age to receive not [[Alone\|alone]] the imperfections of long-engrafted condition, but therewithal the unruly waywardness that infirm and choleric years bring with them. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Such unconstant starts are we like to have from him as this of [[Kent]]’s banishment. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | There is further compliment of leave-taking between France and him. Pray you, let us sit together. If our father carry authority with such disposition as he bears, this last surrender of his will but offend us. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | We shall further think of it. [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] We must do something, and i’th’ heat. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt |

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il conte di [[Kent]], il [[Duke of Gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], ed Edmond |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Credevo che il [[King\|Re]] prediligesse il duca d'Albany più di quello di Cornwall.<br>[[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]) Così è sempre parso anche a noi, ma ora, nella divisione del regno, non appare quale dei duchi egli stimi di più; ché le qualità sono soppesate in modo tale che il più scrupoloso esame non sa scegliere tra l'una e l'altra metà. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Non è questo vostro figlio, mio signore? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | La sua educazione, signore, è stata a mie spese. Ho arrossito così spesso nel riconoscerlo che ormai ci ho fatto il callo. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Non vi comprendo. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Signore, la madre di questo giovanotto seppe comprendere, donde le si arrotondò il ventre ed ebbe invero, signore, un figlio per la culla prima d'avere un marito per il letto. Sentite odore di fallo? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Non posso desiderare disfatto il fallo, essendone il frutto così ben riuscito. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ma ho un figlio, signore, per ordine di legge, di qualche anno più vecchio di questo, che tuttavia non m'è più caro nel mio conto. Benché questo furfante sia venuto al mondo con una certa sfacciataggine prima d'esser chiamato, pure bella era sua madre, ci fu buon sollazzo nel farlo, e il bastardo dev'essere riconosciuto. (A Edmond) Conoscete questo nobile gentiluomo, Edmond? |
| EDMOND | No, mio signore. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (a Edmond) Il mio signore di [[Kent]]. Ricordatelo d'ora innanzi come mio onorevole amico. |
| EDMOND | (a [[Kent]]) I miei servigi a vostra signoria. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Devo [[Love\|amarvi]], e chiedo istanza di conoscervi meglio. |
| EDMOND | Signore, studierò di meritarlo. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (a [[Kent]]) È stato fuori nove anni, e di nuovo dovrà partire. |
| *(didascalia)* | Squillo di trombe |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Il [[King\|Re]] sta venendo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[King Lear]], i duchi di Cornwall e d'Albany, [[Goneril]], [[Regan]], [[Cordelia]] e il seguito |
| LEAR | Assistete i signori di Francia e di Borgogna, [[Gloucester]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Lo farò, mio signore. Esce |
| LEAR | Intanto esprimeremo il nostro più oscuro proposito.<br>Datemi qua la mappa. Sappiate che abbiamo diviso<br>in tre il nostro regno, ed è nostra ferma intenzione<br>scuotere ogni cura e ogni affanno dalla nostra età,<br>conferendoli a più giovani forze, mentre noi<br>sgravati striscieremo verso la [[Death\|morte]]. Nostro figlio di Cornwall,<br>e voi, non meno amato nostro figlio d'Albany,<br>abbiamo in quest'ora ferma volontà di render note<br>le distinte doti delle nostre figlie, sì che future contese<br>siano ora prevenute. I principi di Francia e<br>di Borgogna—<br>grandi rivali nell'[[Love\|amore]] della nostra figlia minore—<br>a lungo alla nostra [[Court\|corte]] han fatto amoroso soggiorno,<br>e qui devono avere risposta. Ditemi, figlie mie—<br>poiché ora vogliamo spogliarci del governo,<br>dell'interesse del territorio, delle cure di Stato—<br>chi di voi diremo che ci [[Love\|ami]] di più,<br>affinché la nostra più larga munificenza si stenda<br>là dove [[Nature\|natura]] con merito contende? [[Goneril]],<br>nostra primogenita, parla per prima. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Signore, io vi [[Love\|amo]] più di quanto parola possa reggere la materia;<br>più cara della vista, dello spazio e della libertà;<br>oltre ciò che si possa stimare, ricco o raro,<br>non meno della vita; con grazia, salute, [[Beauty\|bellezza]], [[Honour\|onore]];<br>quanto mai [[Child\|figlio]] amò o padre trovò;<br>un [[Love\|amore]] che fa povero il fiato e inetta la parola.<br>Oltre ogni maniera di tanto io vi [[Love\|amo]]. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (a parte)<br>Che dirà [[Cordelia]]? [[Love\|Amare]] e tacere. |
| LEAR | (a [[Goneril]])<br>Di tutti questi confini, da questa linea a questa,<br>ricchi d'ombrose foreste e d'aperte campagne,<br>di fiumi copiosi e di prati dalle larghe distese,<br>ti facciamo signora. Alla tua discendenza e a quella d'Albany<br>sia questo perpetuo.—Che dice la nostra seconda figlia?<br>La nostra carissima [[Regan]], moglie di Cornwall? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Io son fatta di quello [[Self\|stesso]] metallo di mia sorella,<br>e mi stimo al suo valore. Nel mio cuore verace<br>trovo ch'ella nomina proprio il mio atto d'[[Love\|amore]]—<br>solo che resta troppo corta, ché io professo<br>d'essere nemica a tutte le altre gioie<br>che il più prezioso ambito dei sensi possiede,<br>e trovo d'essere [[Alone\|soltanto]] felice<br>nell'[[Love\|amore]] di vostra cara altezza. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (a parte) Allora povera [[Cordelia]]—<br>eppure no, ché son certa che il mio [[Love\|amore]]<br>è più ponderoso della mia lingua. |
| LEAR | (a [[Regan]])<br>A te e ai tuoi in eredità per sempre<br>rimanga questo ampio terzo del nostro bel regno,<br>non minore in spazio, validità e diletto<br>di quello conferito a [[Goneril]]. (A [[Cordelia]]) Ora nostra<br>gioia,<br>benché ultima e minima, al cui giovane [[Love\|amore]]<br>le viti di Francia e il latte di Borgogna<br>contendono d'essere interessati: che sapete dire per trarre<br>un terzo più opulento di quello delle vostre sorelle? Parla. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Nulla, mio signore. |
| LEAR | Nulla? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Nulla. |
| LEAR | Dal nulla non verrà nulla. Parla di nuovo. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Infelice quale io sono, non so sollevare<br>il mio cuore fino alla bocca. Io [[Love\|amo]] vostra maestà<br>secondo il mio vincolo, né più né meno. |
| LEAR | Come, come, [[Cordelia]]? Correggi un poco il tuo discorso<br>affinché tu non guasti la tua fortuna. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Mio buon signore,<br>voi m'avete generata, allevata, amata.<br>Io rendo indietro quei doveri quali son giusti e convenienti—<br>obbedirvi, [[Love\|amarvi]] e sommamente [[Honour\|onorarvi]].<br>Perché le mie sorelle hanno mariti, se dicono<br>d'[[Love\|amare]] voi solo? Forse, quando mi sposerò,<br>quel signore la cui mano prenderà la mia fede porterà<br>metà del mio [[Love\|amore]] con sé, metà della mia cura e del mio dovere.<br>Certo, non mi sposerò mai come le mie sorelle. |
| LEAR | Ma il tuo cuore va con questo? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Sì, mio buon signore. |
| LEAR | Così giovane e così poco tenera? |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Così giovane, mio signore, e verace. |
| LEAR | E sia così. La tua [[Truth\|verità]] sia dunque la tua dote;<br>ché per il sacro splendore del sole,<br>per i misteri d'Ecate e della [[Night\|notte]],<br>per tutte le operazioni degli astri<br>dai quali esistiamo e cessiamo d'essere,<br>qui io ripudio ogni mia cura paterna,<br>ogni prossimità e proprietà di [[Blood\|sangue]],<br>e come straniera al mio cuore e a me<br>ti tengo da quest'ora per sempre. Lo Scita barbaro,<br>o colui che fa della sua progenie vivande<br>per ingozzare il proprio appetito, sarà al mio petto<br>tanto [[Well\|bene]] accolto, compianto e soccorso<br>quanto te, mia figlia d'un tempo. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Mio buon sovrano— |
| LEAR | Pace, [[Kent]].<br>Non t'interporre tra il drago e la sua ira.<br>L'amavo più di tutte, e pensavo di riporre il mio riposo |
| *(didascalia)* | Nella sua tenera cura. A [[Cordelia]] Va' via, e fuggi |
| LEAR | dalla mia vista!—<br>Così la mia [[Grave\|tomba]] mi sia pace, come qui io tolgo<br>a lei il cuore di suo padre. Chiamate Francia. Chi si muove?<br>Chiamate Borgogna. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| LEAR | uno o |
| *(didascalia)* | più |
| LEAR | Cornwall e Albany,<br>con le doti delle mie due figlie digerite la terza.<br>Che l'[[Pride\|orgoglio]], ch'ella chiama schiettezza, la mariti.<br>Io v'investo congiuntamente del mio potere,<br>della preminenza e di tutti i larghi effetti<br>che s'accompagnano alla maestà. Noi stessi, con turno mensile,<br>con riserva di cento cavalieri<br>da voi mantenuti, faremo la nostra dimora<br>presso di voi a debito turno. Solo riterremo<br>il nome e tutti gli attributi d'un [[King\|re]]. Il governo,<br>le rendite, l'esecuzione del resto,<br>figli diletti, siano vostri; a conferma di che<br>dividetevi questa coroncina. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Regale Lear,<br>che io ho sempre onorato come mio [[King\|re]],<br>amato come mio padre, seguito come mio signore,<br>ricordato nelle mie preghiere come mio gran patrono— |
| LEAR | L'arco è teso e tirato; scòstati dal dardo. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Che cada piuttosto, benché la punta invada<br>la regione del mio cuore. Sia [[Kent]] scortese<br>quando Lear è pazzo. Che vorresti fare, vecchio?<br>Credi tu che il dovere avrà timore di parlare<br>quando il potere s'inchina all'adulazione? Alla schiettezza l'[[Honour\|onore]] è<br>tenuto<br>quando la maestà cade in follia. Serba il tuo stato,<br>e nella tua miglior ponderazione frena<br>questa orrenda avventatezza. Rispondo con la vita del mio giudizio,<br>la tua figlia minore non ti [[Love\|ama]] di meno,<br>né sono vuoti di cuore quelli i cui suoni bassi<br>non riverberano vacuità. |
| LEAR | [[Kent]], sulla tua vita, non una parola di più! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | La mia vita non l'ho mai tenuta che come pegno<br>da giocare contro i tuoi nemici, né mai temetti di perderla,<br>essendo la tua salvezza il motivo. |
| LEAR | Fuori dalla mia vista! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Vedi meglio, Lear, e lascia ch'io rimanga<br>il vero bersaglio del tuo occhio. |
| LEAR | Ora, per [[Apollo]]— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ora, per [[Apollo]], [[King\|Re]], tu giuri invano per i tuoi dèi. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR facendo per colpirlo |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | O vassallo! Miscredente! |
| *(didascalia)* | ALBANY e [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] Caro signore, trattenetevi. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (a Lear)<br>Uccidi il tuo medico, e il suo onorario concedi<br>al morbo immondo. Revoca il tuo dono,<br>o finché posso sfogare clamore dalla mia gola<br>ti dirò che fai il male. |
| LEAR | Ascoltami, rinnegato; sulla tua fedeltà, ascoltami!<br>Poiché hai cercato di farci rompere i nostri voti,<br>ciò che mai finora osammo, e con ostinato [[Pride\|orgoglio]]<br>di frapporti tra la nostra sentenza e il nostro potere,<br>ciò che né la nostra [[Nature\|natura]] né il nostro grado può sopportare,<br>affermata la nostra potenza, prendi la tua ricompensa:<br>cinque giorni ti concediamo per provvederti<br>a ripararti dai disastri del mondo,<br>e al sesto per volgere il tuo odiato dorso<br>al nostro regno. Se al settimo giorno seguente<br>il tuo tronco bandito sarà trovato nei nostri domìni,<br>quel momento è la tua [[Death\|morte]]. Via! Per Giove,<br>questo non sarà revocato. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Che tu stia [[Well\|bene]], [[King\|Re]]; poiché così vuoi apparire,<br>la [[Freedom\|libertà]] vive lontano di qui, e qui è l'esilio.<br>(A [[Cordelia]]) Gli dèi al loro caro riparo ti prendano,<br>fanciulla,<br>che giustamente pensi, e hai rettissimamente parlato.<br>(A [[Goneril]] e [[Regan]]) E i vostri larghi discorsi possano<br>le vostre opere confermare,<br>sì che buoni effetti possano [[spring\|sbocciare]] da parole d'[[Love\|amore]].<br>Così [[Kent]], o principi, vi dice addio a tutti;<br>egli seguirà il suo vecchio corso in un paese nuovo. Esce |
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Entra il [[Duke of Gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]] con il |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | [[King of France\|re di Francia]], il duca di Borgogna, e il seguito |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ecco Francia e Borgogna, mio nobile signore. |
| LEAR | Mio signore di Borgogna,<br>a voi per primo ci rivolgiamo, che con questo [[King\|Re]]<br>avete gareggiato per nostra figlia: che cosa al minimo<br>richiederete in dote presente con lei,<br>o cesserete la vostra [[Quest\|ricerca]] d'[[Love\|amore]]? |
| BURGUNDY | Regalissima maestà,<br>non bramo più di quanto vostra altezza ha offerto;<br>né voi offrirete meno. |
| LEAR | Nobilissimo Borgogna,<br>quando ci era cara la tenevamo per tale;<br>ma ora il suo prezzo è caduto. Signore, eccola là.<br>Se qualcosa in quella piccola parvenza di sostanza,<br>o tutta quanta, rappezzata col nostro sdegno,<br>e nulla più, può convenire a vostra grazia,<br>eccola là, ed è vostra. |
| BURGUNDY | Non so quale risposta dare. |
| LEAR | Volete voi, con quelle infermità ch'ella possiede,<br>senza amici, nuovamente adottata dal nostro odio,<br>dotata della nostra maledizione e resa estranea dal nostro giuramento,<br>prenderla o lasciarla? |
| BURGUNDY | Perdonatemi, regale signore.<br>La scelta non si compie a tali condizioni. |
| LEAR | Allora lasciatela, signore; ché per la potenza che mi fece,<br>vi dico tutta la sua ricchezza. (A Francia) Quanto a voi, gran [[King\|Re]],<br>non vorrei dal vostro [[Love\|amore]] fare tale traviamento<br>da accasarvi dove io odio, perciò vi supplico<br>di volgere il vostro affetto a via più degna<br>che non su una misera di cui [[Nature\|natura]] si vergogna<br>quasi di riconoscerla sua. |
| FRANCE | Questo è stranissimo,<br>che colei che pur ora era il vostro miglior oggetto,<br>l'argomento della vostra lode, balsamo della vostra età,<br>la migliore, la più cara, debba in questo attimo di [[Time\|tempo]]<br>commettere cosa tanto mostruosa da smantellare<br>tante pieghe di favore. Certo, la sua offesa<br>dev'essere di grado così snaturato<br>da renderla mostro, o il vostro affetto già dichiarato<br>cade in sospetto; il che, a crederlo di lei,<br>dovrebb'essere una [[Faith\|fede]] che ragione senza miracolo<br>non pianterebbe mai in me. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | (a Lear)<br>Ancora supplico vostra maestà,<br>se è perché mi manca quell'arte scorrevole e oleosa<br>di parlare senza intendere—poiché ciò che [[Well\|bene]] intendo,<br>lo faccio prima di dirlo—che facciate noto<br>che non è viziosa macchia, omicidio o turpitudine,<br>nessuna azione impudica o passo disonorato<br>che m'abbia privata della vostra grazia e del vostro favore,<br>ma appunto il difetto di ciò per cui sono più ricca—<br>un occhio sempre supplicante, e una lingua tale<br>che son lieta di non avere, benché il non averla<br>m'abbia perduta nel vostro affetto. |
| LEAR | Meglio<br>non fossi nata, che non avermi meglio compiaciuto. |
| FRANCE | È solo questo—una lentezza di [[Nature\|natura]],<br>che spesso [[Leaves\|lascia]] non detta la [[history\|storia]]<br>di ciò che intende fare?—Mio signore di Borgogna,<br>che dite voi della dama? L'[[Love\|amore]] non è [[Love\|amore]]<br>quando è mescolato a riguardi che stanno<br>lontani dal punto intero. La volete?<br>Ella è essa stessa una dote. |
| BURGUNDY | (a Lear) Regale [[King\|Re]],<br>date solo quella porzione che voi stesso proponeste,<br>e qui io prendo [[Cordelia]] per mano,<br>duchessa di Borgogna. |
| LEAR | Nulla. L'ho giurato. Sono fermo. |
| BURGUNDY | (a [[Cordelia]])<br>Mi dispiace, allora, che abbiate perduto un padre<br>tanto da dover perdere un marito. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Pace sia con Borgogna;<br>poiché rispetto e fortune sono il suo [[Love\|amore]],<br>io non sarò sua moglie. |
| FRANCE | Bellissima [[Cordelia]], che sei ricchissima essendo povera;<br>elettissima, essendo abbandonata; e amatissima, essendo disprezzata:<br>te e le tue virtù qui io afferro.<br>Sia lecito, io raccolgo ciò che è gettato via.<br>Dèi, dèi! È strano che dal loro più freddo disprezzo<br>il mio [[Love\|amore]] debba accendersi in infiammato rispetto.—<br>La tua figlia senza dote, [[King\|Re]], gettata alla mia sorte,<br>è [[Queen\|regina]] di noi, dei nostri, e della nostra bella Francia.<br>Non tutti i duchi dell'acquosa Borgogna<br>possono comprarmi questa fanciulla preziosa e non prezzata.—<br>Di' loro addio, [[Cordelia]], benché scortesi.<br>Tu perdi qui, per trovare un altrove migliore. |
| LEAR | Tu l'hai, Francia. Sia tua, ché noi<br>non abbiamo tale figlia, né mai più vedremo<br>quel suo volto. Perciò andatevene,<br>senza la nostra grazia, il nostro [[Love\|amore]], la nostra benedizione.—<br>Venite, nobile Borgogna. Squilli. Escono tutti tranne Francia<br><br>e le sorelle |
| FRANCE | Di' addio alle tue sorelle. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Voi gioielli di nostro padre, con occhi lavati<br>[[Cordelia]] vi [[Leaves\|lascia]]. Io so quel che siete,<br>e come sorella son restia a chiamare<br>i vostri falli col nome che hanno. [[Love\|Amate]] [[Well\|bene]] nostro padre.<br>Ai vostri seni professati lo affido.<br>Ma pure, ahimè, s'io stessi nella sua grazia<br>lo vorrei porre in luogo migliore.<br>Così addio a voi entrambe. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Non prescrivere a noi il nostro dovere. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Sia il tuo studio<br>contentare il tuo signore, che t'ha ricevuta<br>per elemosina della fortuna. Hai lesinato l'obbedienza,<br>e [[Well\|bene]] meriti il difetto che t'è mancato. |
| [[Cordelia\|CORDELIA]] | Il [[Time\|tempo]] svelerà ciò che la piegata astuzia nasconde,<br>chi copre i falli, alla fine con vergogna li deride.<br>[[Well\|Bene]] possiate prosperare. |
| FRANCE | Venite, mia bella [[Cordelia]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono Francia e [[Cordelia]] |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Sorella, non è poco quello che ho da dire di ciò che tocca da vicino entrambe noi. Credo che nostro padre partirà di qui stanotte. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | È certissimo, e con te. Il mese prossimo con noi. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Vedi quanto la sua età sia piena di mutamenti. L'osservazione che ne abbiamo fatta è stata poca. Ha sempre amato nostra sorella più di tutte, e con che povero giudizio ora l'abbia respinta appare troppo grossolanamente. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | È l'infermità della sua età; eppure non ha mai conosciuto se stesso che scarsamente. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Il meglio e il più sano del suo [[Time\|tempo]] non è stato che avventato; dobbiamo dunque aspettarci dalla sua vecchiaia di ricevere non [[Alone\|soltanto]] le imperfezioni d'una disposizione da lungo tempo innestata, ma con esse anche l'indocile capricciosità che gli anni infermi e collerici portano con sé. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Simili scatti incostanti siamo destinate ad averne da lui, come questo del bando di [[Kent]]. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | C'è ancora un ulteriore cerimoniale di commiato tra Francia e lui. Ti prego, sediamo insieme. Se nostro padre porta autorità con la disposizione che ha, quest'ultima sua cessione non farà che offenderci. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Ci penseremo ancora. [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] Dobbiamo fare qualcosa, e a caldo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_2" data-title="The Tragedy of King Lear (Folio Text) — Act I, Scene 2" data-chapter="Scene 2" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/child,archetype/king,archetype/monster,archetype/villain,motif/love,motif/moon,motif/nature,motif/night,motif/sleep,motif/stars,motif/time,motif/well,motif/wind,concept/fear,concept/friendship,concept/honour,concept/hope,concept/melancholy,concept/nature,form/comedy,form/tragedy,character/duke_of_gloucester,character/edgar,character/gloucester,character/kent,character/king_lear,character/speed"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Edmond the bastard |
| EDMOND | Thou, [[Nature\|nature]], art my goddess. To thy law<br>My services are bound. Wherefore should I<br>Stand in the plague of custom and permit<br>The curiosity of nations to deprive me<br>For that I am some twelve or fourteen moonshines<br>Lag of a brother? Why ‘bastard’? Wherefore ‘base’,<br>When my dimensions are as [[Well\|well]] compact,<br>My mind as generous, and my shape as true<br>As honest madam’s issue? Why brand they us<br>With ‘base’, with ‘baseness, bastardy—base, base’—<br>Who in the lusty stealth of [[Nature\|nature]] take<br>More composition and fierce quality<br>Than doth within a dull, stale, tirèd bed<br>Go to th’ creating a whole tribe of fops<br>Got ‘tween a [[Sleep\|sleep]] and wake? [[Well]] then,<br>Legitimate [[Edgar]], I must have your land.<br>Our father’s [[Love\|love]] is to the bastard Edmond<br>As to th’ legitimate. Fine word, ‘legitimate’.<br>[[Well]], my legitimate, if this letter [[Speed\|speed]]<br>And my invention thrive, Edmond the base<br>Shall to th’ legitimate. I grow, I prosper.<br>Now gods, stand up for bastards! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]]. Edmond reads a letter |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Kent]] banished thus, and France in choler parted,<br>And the [[King]] gone tonight, prescribed his power,<br>Confined to exhibition—all this done<br>Upon the gad?—Edmond, how now? What news? |
| EDMOND | So please your lordship, none. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Why so earnestly seek you to put up that letter? |
| EDMOND | I know no news, my lord. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] What paper were you reading? |
| EDMOND | Nothing, my lord. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No? What needed then that terrible dispatch of it into your pocket? The quality of nothing hath not such need to hide itself. Let’s see. Come, if it be nothing I shall not need spectacles. |
| EDMOND | I beseech you, sir, pardon me. It is a letter from my brother that I have not all o‘er-read; and for so much as I have perused, I find it not fit for your o’erlooking. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] Give me the letter, sir. |
| EDMOND | I shall offend either to detain or give it. The contents, as in part I understand them, are to blame. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] Let’s see, let’s see. |
| EDMOND | I [[Hope\|hope]] for my brother’s justification he wrote this but as an assay or taste of my virtue.<br>He gives [[Gloucester]] a letter |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (reads) ‘This policy and reverence of age makes the world bitter to the best of our times, keeps our fortunes from us till our oldness cannot relish them. I begin to find an idle and fond bondage in the oppression of aged tyranny, who sways not as it hath power but as it is suffered. Come to me, that of this I may speak more. If our father would [[Sleep\|sleep]] till I waked him, you should enjoy half his revenue for ever and live the beloved of your brother,<br>[[Edgar]].’<br>Hum, conspiracy! ‘[[Sleep]] till I wake him, you should enjoy half his revenue’—my son [[Edgar]]! Had he a hand to write this, a heart and brain to breed it in? When came you to this? Who brought it? |
| EDMOND | It was not brought me, my lord, there’s the cunning of it. I found it thrown in at the casement of my closet. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | You know the character to be your brother’s? |
| EDMOND | If the matter were good, my lord, I durst swear it were his; but in respect of that, I would fain think it were not. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]) It is his. |
| EDMOND | It is his hand, my lord, but I [[Hope\|hope]] his heart is not in the contents. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Has he never before sounded you in this business? |
| EDMOND | Never, my lord; but I have heard him oft maintain it to be fit that, sons at perfect age and fathers declined, the father should be as ward to the son, and the son manage his revenue. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O [[Villain\|villain]], [[Villain\|villain]]—his very opinion in the letter! Abhorred [[Villain\|villain]], unnatural, detested, brutish [[Villain\|villain]]—worse than brutish! Go, sirrah, seek him. I’ll apprehend him. Abominable [[Villain\|villain]]! Where is he? |
| EDMOND | I do not [[Well\|well]] know, my lord. If it shall please you to suspend your indignation against my brother till you can derive from him better testimony of his intent, you should run a certain course; where if you violently proceed against him, mistaking his purpose, it would make a great gap in your own [[Honour\|honour]] and shake in pieces the heart of his obedience. I dare pawn down my life for him that he hath writ this to feel my affection to your [[Honour\|honour]], and to no other pretence of danger. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Think you so? |
| EDMOND | If your [[Honour\|honour]] judge it meet, I will place you where you shall hear us confer of this, and by an auricular assurance have your satisfaction, and that without any further delay than this very evening. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He cannot be such a [[Monster\|monster]]. Edmond, seek him out, [[Wind\|wind]] me into him, I pray you. Frame the business after your own wisdom. I would unstate myself to be in a due resolution. |
| EDMOND | I will seek him, sir, presently, convey the business as I shall find means, and acquaint you withal. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | These late eclipses in the sun and [[Moon\|moon]] portend no good to us. Though the wisdom of [[Nature\|nature]] can reason it thus and thus, yet [[Nature\|nature]] finds itself scourged by the sequent effects. [[Love]] cools, [[Friendship\|friendship]] falls off, brothers divide; in cities, mutinies; in countries, discord; in palaces, treason; and the bond cracked ‘twixt son and father. This [[Villain\|villain]] of mine comes under the prediction: there’s son against father. The [[King]] falls from bias of [[Nature\|nature]]: there’s father against [[Child\|child]]. We have seen the best of our [[Time\|time]]. Machinations, hollowness, treachery, and all ruinous disorders follow us disquietly to our graves. Find out this [[Villain\|villain]], Edmond; it shall lose thee nothing. Do it carefully. And the noble and true-hearted [[Kent]] banished, his offence honesty! ’Tis strange. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| EDMOND | This is the excellent foppery of the world: that when we are sick in fortune—often the surfeits of our own behaviour—we make guilty of our disasters the sun, the [[Moon\|moon]], and [[Stars\|stars]], as if we were villains on necessity, fools by heavenly compulsion, knaves, thieves, and treachers by spherical predominance, drunkards, liars, and adulterers by an enforced obedience of planetary influence, and all that we are evil in by a divine thrusting on. An admirable evasion of whore-master man, to lay his goatish disposition on the charge of a star! My father compounded with my mother under the Dragon’s tail and my nativity was under Ursa Major, so that it follows I am rough and lecherous. Fut! I should have been that I am had the maidenliest star in the firmament twinkled on my bastardizing. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | Pat he comes, like the catastrophe of the old [[Comedy\|comedy]]. My cue is villainous [[Melancholy\|melancholy]], with a sigh like Tom o’ Bedlam. |
| *(didascalia)* | He reads a book |
| EDMOND | —O, these eclipses do portend these divisions. Fa, so, la, mi. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | How now, brother Edmond, what serious contemplation are you in? |
| EDMOND | I am thinking, brother, of a prediction I read this other day, what should follow these eclipses. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Do you busy yourself with that? |
| EDMOND | I promise you, the effects he writes of succeed unhappily. When saw you my father last? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | The [[Night\|night]] gone by. |
| EDMOND | Spake you with him? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Ay, two hours together. |
| EDMOND | Parted you in good terms? Found you no displeasure in him by word nor countenance? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | None at all. |
| EDMOND | Bethink yourself wherein you may have offended him, and at my entreaty forbear his presence until some little [[Time\|time]] hath qualified the heat of his displeasure, which at this instant so rageth in him that with the mischief of your person it would scarcely allay. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Some [[Villain\|villain]] hath done me wrong. |
| EDMOND | That’s my [[Fear\|fear]]. I pray you have a continent forbearance till the [[Speed\|speed]] of his rage goes slower; and, as I say, retire with me to my lodging, from whence I will fitly bring you to hear my lord speak. Pray ye, go. There’s my key. If you do stir abroad, go armed. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Armed, brother? |
| EDMOND | Brother, I advise you to the best. I am no honest man if there be any good meaning toward you. I have told you what I have seen and heard but faintly, nothing like the image and horror of it. Pray you, away. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Shall I hear from you anon? |
| EDMOND | I do serve you in this business. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | A credulous father, and a brother noble,<br>Whose [[Nature\|nature]] is so far from doing harms<br>That he suspects none; on whose foolish honesty<br>My practices ride easy. I see the business.<br>Let me, if not by birth, have lands by wit.<br>All with me’s meet that I can fashion fit. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Edmond il bastardo |
| EDMOND | Tu, [[Nature\|natura]], sei la mia dea. Alla tua legge<br>sono legati i miei servigi. Perché dovrei<br>starmene nella peste della consuetudine e permettere<br>che la scrupolosità delle nazioni mi privi<br>solo perché sono di dodici o quattordici lune<br>in ritardo su un fratello? Perché 'bastardo'? Perché 'vile',<br>quando le mie membra sono altrettanto [[Well\|bene]] compatte,<br>la mia mente altrettanto generosa, e la mia forma altrettanto schietta<br>quanto la prole d'un'onesta madama? Perché ci marchiano<br>con 'vile', con 'viltà, bastardia—vile, vile'—<br>noi che nel vigoroso furto della [[Nature\|natura]] prendiamo<br>più tempra e più fiera qualità<br>di quanta, dentro un letto ottuso, stantio e stanco,<br>se ne spenda a creare un'intera tribù di zerbinotti<br>generati tra un [[Sleep\|sonno]] e una veglia? [[Well\|Bene]] dunque,<br>legittimo [[Edgar]], devo avere la tua terra.<br>L'[[Love\|amore]] di nostro padre è per il bastardo Edmond<br>quanto per il legittimo. Bella parola, 'legittimo'.<br>[[Well\|Bene]], mio legittimo, se questa lettera [[Speed\|corre a segno]]<br>e la mia invenzione prospera, Edmond il vile<br>sormonterà il legittimo. Cresco, prospero.<br>Ora, dèi, sostenete i bastardi! |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra il [[Duke of Gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]]. Edmond legge una lettera |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Kent]] bandito a questo modo, e Francia partito in collera,<br>e il [[King\|Re]] andato via stanotte, limitato il suo potere,<br>ridotto a un assegno—tutto questo fatto<br>su due piedi?—Edmond, ebbene? Che notizie? |
| EDMOND | Con licenza di vostra signoria, nessuna. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Perché cercate con tanto ardore di riporre quella lettera? |
| EDMOND | Non so nessuna notizia, mio signore. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] Che carta stavate leggendo? |
| EDMOND | Nulla, mio signore. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | No? Che bisogno c'era allora di quel terribile spaccio di essa dentro la vostra tasca? La qualità del nulla non ha tanto bisogno di nascondersi. Vediamo. Su, se non è nulla non avrò bisogno d'occhiali. |
| EDMOND | Vi supplico, signore, perdonatemi. È una lettera di mio fratello che non ho ancora letta tutta quanta; e per quanto ne ho scorso, la trovo non adatta al vostro esame. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] Datemi la lettera, signore. |
| EDMOND | Offenderò tanto a trattenerla quanto a darla. Il contenuto, per quanto in parte lo intenda, è da biasimare. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] Vediamo, vediamo. |
| EDMOND | [[Hope\|Spero]], a giustificazione di mio fratello, che l'abbia scritta solo come saggio o assaggio della mia virtù.<br>Dà a [[Gloucester]] una lettera |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (legge) 'Questa politica e questa reverenza per la vecchiaia rendono il mondo amaro ai migliori dei nostri anni, ci tengono lontane le fortune finché la nostra vecchiezza non sa più gustarle. Comincio a trovare una vana e sciocca schiavitù nell'oppressione della tirannia senile, che regna non perché ne abbia il potere, ma perché è tollerata. Vieni da me, che di questo io possa dirti di più. Se nostro padre [[Sleep\|dormisse]] finché io non lo svegliassi, tu godresti per sempre metà delle sue rendite e vivresti amato da tuo fratello,<br>[[Edgar]].'<br>Ehm, congiura! '[[Sleep\|Dormisse]] finché io non lo svegliassi, tu godresti metà delle sue rendite'—mio figlio [[Edgar]]! Ebbe una mano per scrivere questo, un cuore e un cervello per covarlo? Quando v'è giunta? Chi l'ha portata? |
| EDMOND | Non m'è stata portata, mio signore, sta qui l'astuzia. L'ho trovata gettata dentro la finestra del mio gabinetto. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Riconoscete la scrittura per quella di vostro fratello? |
| EDMOND | Se la materia fosse buona, mio signore, oserei giurare che è la sua; ma trattandosi di questa, vorrei volentieri pensare che non lo sia. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]]) È sua. |
| EDMOND | È la sua mano, mio signore, ma [[Hope\|spero]] che il suo cuore non sia nel contenuto. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Non v'ha mai prima sondato in questo affare? |
| EDMOND | Mai, mio signore; ma l'ho udito spesso sostenere che sarebbe conveniente che, essendo i figli in età perfetta e i padri in declino, il padre fosse come pupillo del figlio, e il figlio amministrasse le sue rendite. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O [[Villain\|scellerato]], [[Villain\|scellerato]]—proprio la sua opinione nella lettera! Aborrito [[Villain\|scellerato]], snaturato, detestato, bestiale [[Villain\|scellerato]]—peggio che bestiale! Va', tu, cercalo. Lo farò arrestare. Abominevole [[Villain\|scellerato]]! Dov'è? |
| EDMOND | Non lo so [[Well\|bene]], mio signore. Se vi piacesse sospendere la vostra indignazione contro mio fratello finché non possiate ricavare da lui migliore testimonianza della sua intenzione, seguireste un corso sicuro; laddove, se procedete violentemente contro di lui, fraintendendo il suo proposito, ciò farebbe gran breccia nel vostro stesso [[Honour\|onore]] e frantumerebbe il cuore della sua obbedienza. Oso impegnare la mia vita per lui, che ha scritto questo per saggiare il mio affetto verso il vostro [[Honour\|onore]], e per nessun'altra intenzione di pericolo. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Lo credete? |
| EDMOND | Se il vostro [[Honour\|onore]] lo giudica conveniente, vi collocherò dove ci udrete conferire di questo, e con auricolare certezza avrete la vostra soddisfazione, e ciò senza altro indugio che questa sera stessa. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Non può essere un tale [[Monster\|mostro]]. Edmond, cercalo, [[Wind\|insinuami]] dentro di lui, ti prego. Conduci l'affare secondo la tua saggezza. Mi spoglierei del mio stato per essere in una debita certezza. |
| EDMOND | Lo cercherò, signore, all'istante, condurrò l'affare come troverò modo, e ve ne informerò. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Queste recenti eclissi del sole e della [[Moon\|luna]] non ci presagiscono nulla di buono. Benché la saggezza della [[Nature\|natura]] possa ragionarne così e così, pure la [[Nature\|natura]] si trova flagellata dagli effetti che ne conseguono. L'[[Love\|amore]] si raffredda, l'[[Friendship\|amicizia]] vien meno, i fratelli si dividono; nelle città, ammutinamenti; nelle campagne, discordia; nei palazzi, tradimento; e spezzato il vincolo tra figlio e padre. Questo mio [[Villain\|scellerato]] rientra nella predizione: ecco il figlio contro il padre. Il [[King\|Re]] devia dall'inclinazione della [[Nature\|natura]]: ecco il padre contro il [[Child\|figlio]]. Abbiamo visto il meglio del nostro [[Time\|tempo]]. Macchinazioni, vuotezza, tradimento e ogni rovinoso disordine ci seguono inquieti fino alla tomba. Scopri questo [[Villain\|scellerato]], Edmond; non ti costerà nulla. Fallo con cura. E il nobile e sincero [[Kent]] bandito, la sua colpa l'onestà! È strano. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| EDMOND | Questa è la squisita scempiaggine del mondo: che quando siamo malati nella fortuna—spesso per le crapule della nostra stessa condotta—facciamo colpevoli dei nostri disastri il sole, la [[Moon\|luna]] e le [[Stars\|stelle]], come se fossimo scellerati per necessità, sciocchi per celeste costrizione, furfanti, ladri e traditori per predominio delle sfere, ubriaconi, bugiardi e adulteri per una forzata obbedienza all'influsso planetario, e tutto ciò in cui siamo cattivi per una spinta divina. Ammirevole scappatoia dell'uomo puttaniere, addossare la sua caprigna disposizione al conto d'una stella! Mio padre s'accoppiò con mia madre sotto la coda del Dragone e la mia natività fu sotto l'Orsa Maggiore, sicché ne segue che sono rozzo e lascivo. Puah! Sarei stato quello che sono anche se la più verginale stella del firmamento avesse ammiccato sul mio imbastardimento. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | Eccolo puntuale, come la catastrofe della vecchia [[Comedy\|commedia]]. La mia battuta è una scellerata [[Melancholy\|malinconia]], con un sospiro come Tom di Bedlam. |
| *(didascalia)* | Legge un libro |
| EDMOND | —Oh, queste eclissi presagiscono queste divisioni. Fa, sol, la, mi. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Ebbene, fratello Edmond, in che seria contemplazione siete? |
| EDMOND | Sto pensando, fratello, a una predizione che ho letto l'altro giorno, su quel che dovrebbe seguire a queste eclissi. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Vi occupate di ciò? |
| EDMOND | Vi assicuro, gli effetti di cui egli scrive si avverano infelicemente. Quando avete visto mio padre l'ultima volta? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | La [[Night\|notte]] scorsa. |
| EDMOND | Gli avete parlato? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Sì, per due ore di seguito. |
| EDMOND | Vi siete lasciati in buoni termini? Non avete trovato in lui alcun dispiacere, né in parola né in volto? |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Nessuno affatto. |
| EDMOND | Ripensate in che possiate averlo offeso, e per mia preghiera evitate la sua presenza finché un poco di [[Time\|tempo]] non abbia temperato l'ardore del suo dispiacere, il quale in questo istante talmente infuria in lui che a stento si placherebbe con lo scempio della vostra persona. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Qualche [[Villain\|scellerato]] m'ha fatto torto. |
| EDMOND | È questo il mio [[Fear\|timore]]. Vi prego, abbiate contenuta pazienza finché la [[Speed\|foga]] della sua ira non rallenti; e, come dico, ritiratevi con me nel mio alloggio, donde a tempo debito vi condurrò a udire parlare il mio signore. Vi prego, andate. Ecco la mia chiave. Se uscite fuori, andate armato. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Armato, fratello? |
| EDMOND | Fratello, vi consiglio per il meglio. Non sono uomo onesto se v'è qualche buona intenzione verso di voi. V'ho detto quel che ho visto e udito solo debolmente, nulla di simile all'immagine e all'orrore della cosa. Vi prego, via. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Avrò vostre notizie fra poco? |
| EDMOND | Vi servo in questo affare. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | Un padre credulo, e un fratello nobile,<br>la cui [[Nature\|natura]] è così lontana dal fare del male<br>che non ne sospetta in nessuno; sulla cui sciocca onestà<br>le mie manovre cavalcano leggere. Vedo l'affare.<br>Ch'io abbia, se non per nascita, terre per ingegno.<br>Tutto m'è acconcio, purché lo sappia foggiare a modo. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |



<span class="atom-split" data-atom="act_1--scene_3" data-title="The Tragedy of King Lear (Folio Text) — Act I, Scene 3" data-chapter="Scene 3" data-kind="scene" data-tags="archetype/fool,motif/night,motif/well,form/tragedy,character/goneril,character/king_lear"></span>


| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Goneril]] and Oswald, her steward [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] |
| *(didascalia)* | Did my father strike my gentleman<br>For chiding of his [[Fool\|fool]]? |
| OSWALD | Ay, madam. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | By day and [[Night\|night]] he wrongs me. Every hour<br>He flashes into one gross crime or other<br>That sets us all at odds. I’ll not endure it.<br>His knights grow riotous, and himself upbraids us<br>On every trifle. When he returns from hunting<br>I will not speak with him. Say I am sick.<br>If you come slack of former services<br>You shall do [[Well\|well]]; the fault of it I’ll answer. |
| *(didascalia)* | Horns within |
| OSWALD | He’s coming, madam. I hear him. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Put on what weary negligence you please,<br>You and your fellows. I’d have it come to question.<br>If he distaste it, let him to my sister,<br>Whose mind and mine I know in that are one.<br>Remember what I have said. |
| OSWALD | [[Well]], madam.<br>GONERI,<br>And let his knights have colder looks among you.<br>What grows of it, no matter. Advise your fellows so.<br>I’ll write straight to my sister to hold my course.<br>Prepare for dinner. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt severally |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano [[Goneril]] e Oswald, il suo maggiordomo [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] |
