| *(didascalia)* | Enter Edmond the bastard, and Curan, severally EDMOND Save thee, Curan. |
| CURAN | And you, sir. I have been with your father, and given him notice that the Duke of Cornwall and [[Regan]] his duchess will be here with him this [[Night\|night]]. |
| EDMOND | How comes that? CURAN Nay, I know not. You have heard of the news abroad?—I mean the whispered ones, for they are yet but ear-kissing arguments. |
| EDMOND | Not I. Pray you, what are they? |
| CURAN | Have you heard of no likely wars toward twixt the Dukes of Cornwall and Albany? |
| EDMOND | Not a word. |
| CURAN | You may do then in [[Time\|time]]. Fare you [[Well\|well]], sir. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| EDMOND | The Duke be here tonight! The better, best.<br>This weaves itself perforce into my business. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] at a [[Window\|window]] above |
| EDMOND | My father hath set guard to take my brother,<br>And I have one thing of a queasy question<br>Which I must act. Briefness and fortune work!—<br>Brother, a word, descend. Brother, I say. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] climbs down |
| EDMOND | My father watches. O sir, fly this place.<br>Intelligence is given where you are hid.<br>You have now the good advantage of the [[Night\|night]].<br>Have you not spoken ‘gainst the Duke of Cornwall?<br>He’s coming hither, now, i’th’ [[Night\|night]], i‘th’ haste,<br>And [[Regan]] with him. Have you nothing said<br>Upon his party ’gainst the Duke of Albany?<br>Advise yourself. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I am sure on’t, not a word. |
| EDMOND | I hear my father coming. Pardon me.<br>In cunning I must draw my [[Sword\|sword]] upon you.<br>Draw. Seem to defend yourself. Now, quit you [[Well\|well]].<br>(Calling) Yield, come before my father. Light ho, here!<br>(To [[Edgar]]) Fly, brother! (Calling) Torches, torches!<br>(To [[Edgar]]) So, farewell. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | Some [[Blood\|blood]] drawn on me would beget opinion<br>Of my more fierce endeavour.<br>He wounds his arm<br>I have seen drunkards<br>Do more than this in sport. (Calling) Father, father!<br>Stop, stop! Ho, help! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and servants with torches |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Now, Edmond, where’s the [[Villain\|villain]]? |
| EDMOND | Here stood he in the dark, his sharp [[Sword\|sword]] out,<br>Mumbling of wicked charms, conjuring the [[Moon\|moon]]<br>To stand ’s auspicious mistress. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | But where is he? |
| EDMOND | Look, sir, I bleed. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Where is the [[Villain\|villain]], Edmond? |
| EDMOND | Fled this way, sir, when by no means he could— |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Pursue him, ho! Go after. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt servants |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | By no means what? |
| EDMOND | Persuade me to the murder of your lordship,<br>But that I told him the revenging gods<br>‘Gainst parricides did all the thunder bend,<br>Spoke with how manifold and strong a bond<br>The [[Child\|child]] was bound to th’ father. Sir, in fine,<br>Seeing how loathly opposite I stood<br>To his unnatural purpose, in fell motion<br>With his prepared [[Sword\|sword]] he charges [[Home\|home]]<br>My unprovided [[Body\|body]], latched mine arm;<br>And when he saw my best alarumed spirits<br>Bold in the quarrel’s right, roused to th’encounter,<br>Or whether ghasted by the noise I made,<br>Full suddenly he fled. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let him fly far,<br>Not in this land shall he remain uncaught,<br>And found, dispatch. The noble Duke my master,<br>My worthy arch and patron, comes tonight.<br>By his authority I will proclaim it<br>That he which finds him shall deserve our thanks,<br>Bringing the murderous coward to the stake;<br>He that conceals him, [[Death\|death]]. |
| EDMOND | When I dissuaded him from his intent<br>And found him pitched to do it, with curst speech<br>I threatened to discover him. He replied,<br>‘Thou unpossessing bastard, dost thou think<br>If I would stand against thee, would the reposal<br>Of any trust, virtue, or worth in thee<br>Make thy words faithed? No, what I should deny—<br>As this I would, ay, though thou didst produce<br>My very character—I’d turn it all<br>To thy suggestion, plot, and damned practice,<br>And thou must make a dullard of the world<br>If they not thought the profits of my [[Death\|death]]<br>Were very pregnant and potential spirits<br>To make thee seek it.’ |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O strange and fastened [[Villain\|villain]]!<br>Would he deny his letter, said he? |
| *(didascalia)* | Tucket within |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Hark, the Duke’s trumpets. I know not why he comes.<br>All ports I’ll bar. The [[Villain\|villain]] shall not scape.<br>The Duke must grant me that; besides, his picture<br>I will send far and near, that all the kingdom<br>May have due note of him—and of my land,<br>Loyal and natural boy, I’ll work the means<br>To make thee capable. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall, [[Regan]], and attendants |
| CORNWALL | How now, my noble friend? Since I came hither,<br>Which I can call but now, I have heard strange news. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | If it be true, all vengeance comes too short<br>Which can pursue th’offender. How dost, my lord? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O madam, my old heart is cracked, it’s cracked. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What, did my father’s godson seek your life?<br>He whom my father named, your [[Edgar]]? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | O lady, lady, shame would have it hid! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Was he not companion with the riotous knights<br>That tend upon my father? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I know not, madam. ’Tis too bad, too bad. |
| EDMOND | Yes, madam, he was of that consort. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | No marvel, then, though he were ill affected.<br>‘Tis they have put him on the old man’s [[Death\|death]],<br>To have th’expense and spoil of his revenues.<br>I have this present evening from my sister<br>Been [[Well\|well]] informed of them, and with such cautions<br>That if they come to sojourn at my [[house]]<br>I’ll not be there. |
| CORNWALL | Nor I, assure thee, [[Regan]]. Edmond, I hear that you have shown your father A childlike office. |
| EDMOND | It was my duty, sir. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] (to Cornwall)<br>He did bewray his practice, and received<br>This hurt you see striving to apprehend him. |
| CORNWALL | Is he pursued? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| CORNWALL | If he be taken, he shall never more<br>Be feared of doing harm. Make your own purpose<br>How in my strength you please. For you, Edmond,<br>Whose virtue and obedience doth this instant<br>So much commend itself, you shall be ours.<br>Natures of such deep trust we shall much need.<br>You we first seize on. |
| EDMOND | I shall serve you, sir,<br>Truly, however else. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (to Cornwall) For him I thank your grace. |
| CORNWALL | You know not why we came to visit you— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Thus out of season, threading dark-eyed [[Night\|night]]—<br>Occasions, noble [[Gloucester]], of some poise,<br>Wherein we must have use of your advice.<br>Our father he hath writ, so hath our sister,<br>Of differences which I least thought it fit<br>To answer from our [[Home\|home]]. The several messengers<br>From hence attend dispatch. Our good old friend,<br>Lay comforts to your bosom, and bestow<br>Your needful counsel to our businesses,<br>Which craves the instant use. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I serve you, madam.<br>Your graces are right welcome. |
| *(didascalia)* | Flourish. Exeunt |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | 2.2 Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], disguised, and Oswald the steward, severally |
| OSWALD | Good dawning to thee, friend. Art of this [[house]]? [[Kent\|KENT]] Ay. |
| OSWALD | Where may we set our horses? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’th’ mire. |
| OSWALD | Prithee, if thou lov’st me, tell me. 5 [[Kent\|KENT]] I [[Love\|love]] thee not. |
| OSWALD | Why then, I care not for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | If I had thee in Lipsbury pinfold I would make thee care for me. |
| OSWALD | Why dost thou use me thus? I know thee not. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fellow, I know thee. |
| OSWALD | What dost thou know me for? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A knave, a rascal, an eater of broken meats, a base, proud, shallow, beggarly, three-suited, hundred-pound, filthy worsted-stocking knave; a lily-livered, action-taking, whoreson, glass-gazing, super-serviceable, finical rogue; one-trunk-inheriting slave; one that wouldst be a bawd in way of good service, and art nothing but the composition of a knave, beggar, coward, pander, and the son and heir of a mongrel bitch, one whom I will beat into clamorous whining if thou deniest the least syllable of thy addition. |
| OSWALD | Why, what a monstrous fellow art thou, thus to rail on one that is neither known of thee nor knows thee! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What a brazen-faced varlet art thou, to deny thou knowest me! Is it two days since I tripped up thy heels and beat thee before the [[King]]? Draw, you rogue; for though it be [[Night\|night]], yet the [[Moon\|moon]] shines. |
| *(didascalia)* | He draws his [[Sword\|sword]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’ll make a sop o’th’ moonshine of you, you whoreson, cullionly barber-monger, draw! |
| OSWALD | Away. I have nothing to do with thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Draw, you rascal. You come with letters against the [[King]], and take [[Vanity]] the puppet’s part against the royalty of her father. Draw, you rogue, or I’ll so carbonado your shanks—draw, you rascal, come your ways! |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, help! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Strike, you slave! Stand, rogue! Stand, you neat slave, strike! 40 |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, murder! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Edmond the bastard, then the Duke of |
| OSWALD | Cornwall, [[Regan]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and<br><br>servants |
| EDMOND | How now, what’s the matter? Part. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With you, goodman boy. If you please, come, I’ll flesh ye. Come on, young master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Weapons? Arms? What’s the matter here? |
| CORNWALL | Keep peace, upon your lives. He dies that strikes again.<br>What is the matter? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | The messengers from our sister and the [[King]]. CORNWALL (to [[Kent]] and Oswald) What is your difference?<br>Speak. |
| OSWALD | I am scarce in breath, my lord. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No marvel, you have so bestirred your valour, you cowardly rascal. [[Nature]] disclaims in thee; a tailor made thee. |
| CORNWALL | Thou art a strange fellow—a tailor make a man? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A tailor, sir. A stone-cutter or a painter could not have made him so ill though they had been but two years o’th’ trade. |
| CORNWALL | Speak yet; how grew your quarrel? |
| OSWALD | This ancient ruffian, sir, whose life I have spared at suit of his grey beard— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Thou whoreson Z, thou unnecessary letter—(to Cornwall) my lord, if you’ll give me leave I will tread this unbolted [[Villain\|villain]] into mortar and daub the wall of a jakes with him. (To Oswald) Spare my grey beard, you wagtail? |
| CORNWALL | Peace, sirrah.<br>You beastly knave, know you no reverence? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Yes, sir, but anger hath a privilege. |
| CORNWALL | Why art thou angry? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | That such a slave as this should wear a [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Who wears no honesty. Such smiling rogues as these,<br>Like rats, oft bite the holy cords a-twain<br>Which are too intrince t’unloose, smooth every<br>passion<br>That in the natures of their lords [[Rebel\|rebel]];<br>Being oil to [[Fire\|fire]], [[Snow\|snow]] to the colder moods,<br>Renege, affirm, and turn their halcyon beaks<br>With every gall and vary of their masters,<br>Knowing naught, like [[dogs]], but following. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Oswald A plague upon your epileptic visage! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Smile you my speeches as I were a [[Fool\|fool]]?<br>Goose, an I had you upon Sarum Plain<br>I’d drive ye cackling [[Home\|home]] to Camelot. |
| CORNWALL | What, art thou mad, old fellow? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] to [[Kent]] How fell you out? Say that. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No contraries hold more antipathy Than I and such a knave. |
| CORNWALL | Why dost thou call him knave?<br>What is his fault? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | His countenance likes me not. |
| CORNWALL | No more perchance does mine, nor his, nor hers. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, ’tis my occupation to be plain:<br>I have seen better faces in my [[Time\|time]]<br>Than stands on any shoulder that I see<br>Before me at this instant. |
| CORNWALL | This is some fellow<br>Who, having been praised for bluntness, doth affect<br>A saucy roughness, and constrains the garb<br>Quite from his [[Nature\|nature]]. He cannot flatter, he;<br>An honest mind and plain, he must speak [[Truth\|truth]].<br>An they will take’t, so; if not, he’s plain.<br>These kind of knaves I know, which in this plainness<br>Harbour more craft and more corrupter ends<br>Than twenty silly-ducking observants<br>That stretch their duties nicely. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, in good [[Faith\|faith]], in sincere verity,<br>Under th‘allowance of your great aspect,<br>Whose influence, like the wreath of radiant [[Fire\|fire]]<br>On flick’ring Phoebus’ front— |
| CORNWALL | What mean’st by this? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | To go out of my dialect, which you discommend so much. I know, sir, I am no flatterer. He that beguiled you in a plain accent was a plain knave, which for my part I will not be, though I should win your displeasure to entreat me to’t. |
| CORNWALL | (to Oswald)<br>What was th’offence you gave him? |
| OSWALD | I never gave him any.<br>It pleased the [[King]] his master very late<br>To strike at me upon his misconstruction,<br>When he, compact, and flattering his displeasure,<br>Tripped me behind; being down, insulted, railed,<br>And put upon him such a deal of man<br>That worthied him, got praises of the [[King]]<br>For him attempting who was [[Self\|self]]-subdued,<br>And in the fleshment of this dread exploit<br>Drew on me here again. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None of these rogues and cowards<br>But [[Ajax]] is their [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| CORNWALL | Fetch forth the stocks! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some servants |
| CORNWALL | You stubborn, ancient knave, you reverend braggart,<br>We’ll teach you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, I am too old to learn.<br>Call not your stocks for me. I serve the [[King]],<br>On whose employment I was sent to you.<br>You shall do small respect, show too bold malice<br>Against the grace and person of my master,<br>Stocking his messenger. |
| *(didascalia)* | CORNWALL calling |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fetch forth the stocks!—<br>As I have life and [[Honour\|honour]], there shall he sit till [[Noon\|noon]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Till [[Noon\|noon]]?—till [[Night\|night]], my lord, and all [[Night\|night]] too. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, madam, if I were your father’s dog<br>You should not use me so. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sir, being his knave, I will.<br>Stocks brought out |
| CORNWALL | This is a fellow of the selfsame colour<br>Our sister speaks of.—Come, bring away the stocks. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let me beseech your grace not to do so.<br>The [[King]] his master needs must take it ill<br>That he, so slightly valued in his messenger,<br>Should have him thus restrained. |
| CORNWALL | I’ll answer that. |
| *(didascalia)* | Theg put [[Kent]] in the stocks |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | My sister may receive it much more worse<br>To have her gentlemen abused, assaulted. |
| CORNWALL | Come, my good lord, away! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Gloucester]] and [[Kent]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I am sorry for thee, friend. ’Tis the Duke’s pleasure,<br>Whose disposition, all the world [[Well\|well]] knows,<br>Will not be rubbed nor stopped. I’ll entreat for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Pray do not, sir. I have watched and travelled hard.<br>Some [[Time\|time]] I shall [[Sleep\|sleep]] out; the rest I’ll whistle.<br>A good man’s fortune may grow out at heels.<br>Give you good morrow. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The Duke’s to blame in this; ’twill be ill taken. Exit |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good [[King]], that must approve the common say:<br>Thou out of heaven’s benediction com’st<br>To the warm sun. |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes out a letter |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Approach, thou beacon to this under globe,<br>That by thy comfortable beams I may<br>Peruse this letter. Nothing almost sees miracles<br>But misery. I know ‘tis from [[Cordelia]],<br>Who hath now fortunately been informed<br>Of my obscured course, and shall find [[Time\|time]]<br>For this enormous state, seeking to give<br>Losses their remedies. All weary and o’erwatched,<br>Take vantage, heavy eyes, not to behold<br>This shameful lodging. Fortune, good [[Night\|night]];<br>Smile once more; turn thy [[Wheel\|wheel]].<br>He sleeps |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I heard myself proclaimed,<br>And by the happy hollow of a tree<br>Escaped the hunt. No port is free, no place<br>That guard and most unusual vigilance<br>Does not attend my taking. Whiles I may scape<br>I will preserve myself, and am bethought<br>To take the basest and most poorest shape<br>That ever penury in contempt of man<br>Brought near to beast. My face I’ll grime with filth,<br>Blanket my loins, elf all my hairs in knots,<br>And with presented [[Nakedness\|nakedness]] outface<br>The winds and persecutions of the sky.<br>The country gives me proof and precedent<br>Of Bedlam beggars who with roaring voices<br>Strike in their numbed and mortified arms<br>Pins, wooden pricks, nails, sprigs of rosemary,<br>And with this horrible object from low farms,<br>Poor pelting villages, sheep-cotes and mills<br>Sometime with lunatic bans, sometime with prayers<br>Enforce their [[Charity\|charity]]. ‘Poor Tuelygod, Poor Tom.’<br>That’s something yet. [[Edgar]] I nothing am. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], his [[Fool]], and the First Gentleman |
| LEAR | ’Tis strange that they should so depart from [[Home\|home]]<br>And not send back my messenger. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | As I learned,<br>The [[Night\|night]] before there was no purpose in them<br>Of this remove. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (waking)<br>Hail to thee, noble master. |
| LEAR | Ha! Mak’st thou this shame thy pastime? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, my lord. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Ha, ha, he wears cruel garters! Horses are tied by the heads, [[dogs]] and bears by th’ neck, monkeys by th’ loins, and men by th’ legs. When a man’s overlusty at legs, then he wears wooden nether-stocks. |
| LEAR | (to [[Kent]])<br>What’s he that hath so much thy place mistook To set thee here? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | It is both he and she:<br>Your son and daughter. |
| LEAR | By Jupiter, I swear no. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | By Juno, I swear ay. |
| LEAR | They durst not do’t,<br>They could not, would not do’t. ’Tis worse than<br>murder,<br>To do upon respect such violent outrage.<br>Resolve me with all modest haste which way<br>Thou mightst deserve or they impose this usage,<br>Coming from us. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My lord, when at their [[Home\|home]]<br>I did commend your highness’ letters to them,<br>Ere I was risen from the place that showed<br>My duty kneeling, came there a reeking post<br>Stewed in his haste, half breathless, painting forth<br>From [[Goneril]], his mistress, salutations,<br>Delivered letters spite of intermission,<br>Which presently they read, on whose contents 210<br>They summoned up their meiny, straight took [[Horse\|horse]],<br>Commanded me to follow and attend<br>The leisure of their answer, gave me cold looks;<br>And meeting here the other messenger,<br>Whose welcome I perceived had poisoned mine—<br>Being the very fellow which of late<br>Displayed so saucily against your highness—<br>Having more man than wit about me, drew.<br>He raised the [[house]] with loud and coward cries.<br>Your son and daughter found this trespass worth<br>The shame which here it suffers. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | [[Winter]]’s not gone yet if the wild geese fly that way. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings Fathers that wear rags |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Do make their children blind,<br>But fathers that bear bags<br>Shall see their children kind.<br>Fortune, that arrant whore,<br>Ne’er turns the key to th’ poor.<br>But for all this thou shalt have as many dolours for<br>thy daughters as thou canst tell in a year. |
| LEAR | O, how this mother swells up toward my heart!<br>Histerica passio down, thou climbing sorrow;<br>Thy element’s below.—Where is this daughter? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With the Earl, sir, here within. |
| LEAR | Follow me not; stay here. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN (to [[Kent]]) |
| LEAR | Made you no more offence but what you speak of? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None.<br>How chance the [[King]] comes with so small a number? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | An thou hadst been set i’th’ stocks for that question, thou’dst [[Well\|well]] deserved it. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, [[Fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | We’ll set thee to school to an ant, to teach thee there’s no labouring i’th’ [[Winter\|winter]]. All that follow their noses are led by their eyes but blind men, and there’s not a nose among twenty but can smell him that’s stinking. Let go thy hold when a great [[Wheel\|wheel]] runs down a [[Hill\|hill]], lest it break thy neck with following; but the great one that goes upward, let him draw thee after. When a wise man gives thee better counsel, give me mine again. I would have none but knaves follow it, since a [[Fool\|fool]] gives it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | That sir which serves and seeks for gain<br>And follows but for form,<br>Will pack when it begin to rain,<br>And leave thee in the [[Storm\|storm]].<br>But I will tarry, the [[Fool\|fool]] will stay,<br>And let the wise man fly.<br>The knave turns [[Fool\|fool]] that runs away,<br>The [[Fool\|fool]] no knave, pardie. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Where learned you this, [[Fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Not i’th’ stocks, [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] and the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| LEAR | Deny to speak with me? They are sick, they are weary,<br>They have travelled all the [[Night\|night]]?—mere fetches,<br>The images of revolt and flying off.<br>Fetch me a better answer. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | My dear lord,<br>You know the fiery quality of the Duke,<br>How unremovable and fixed he is<br>In his own course. |
| LEAR | Vengeance, plague, [[Death\|death]], confusion!<br>‘Fiery’? What ‘quality’? Why, [[Gloucester]], [[Gloucester]],<br>I’d speak with the Duke of Cornwall and his wife. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Well]], my good lord, I have informed them so. |
| LEAR | ‘Informed them’? Dost thou understand me, man? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| LEAR | The [[King]] would speak with Cornwall; the dear father<br>Would with his daughter speak, commands, tends<br>service.<br>Are they ‘informed’ of this? My breath and [[Blood\|blood]]—<br>‘Fiery’? The ‘fiery’ Duke—tell the hot Duke that—<br>No, but not yet. Maybe he is not [[Well\|well]].<br>Infirmity doth still neglect all office<br>Whereto our health is bound. We are not ourselves<br>When [[Nature\|nature]], being oppressed, commands the mind<br>To suffer with the [[Body\|body]]. I’ll forbear,<br>And am fallen out with my more headier will,<br>To take the indisposed and sickly fit<br>For the sound man.—[[Death]] on my state, wherefore<br>Should he sit here? This act persuades me<br>That this remotion of the Duke and her<br>Is practice only. Give me my servant forth.<br>Go tell the Duke and’s wife I’d speak with them,<br>Now, presently. Bid them come forth and hear me,<br>Or at their chamber door I’ll beat the drum<br>Till it cry [[Sleep\|sleep]] to [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I would have all [[Well\|well]] betwixt you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | O me, my heart! My rising heart! But down. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Cry to it, nuncle, as the cockney did to the eels when she put ‘em i’th’ paste alive. She knapped ‘em o’th’ coxcombs with a stick, and cried ‘Down, wantons, down!’ ’Twas her brother that, in pure kindness to his [[Horse\|horse]], buttered his hay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall, [[Regan]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and servants |
| LEAR | Good morrow to you both. |
| CORNWALL | Hail to your grace.<br>[[Kent]] here set at liberty |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I am glad to see your highness. |
| LEAR | [[Regan]], I think you are. I know what reason<br>I have to think so. If thou shouldst not be glad<br>I would [[divorce]] me from thy mother’s shrine,<br>Sepulchring an adultress. (To [[Kent]]) O, are you free? |
| *(didascalia)* | Some other [[Time\|time]] for that. Exit [[Kent]] |
| LEAR | Beloved [[Regan]],<br>Thy sister’s naught. O, [[Regan]], she hath tied<br>Sharp-toothed unkindness like a vulture here.<br>I can scarce speak to thee. Thou’lt not believe<br>With how depraved a quality-O, [[Regan]]! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, sir, take patience. I have [[Hope\|hope]]<br>You less know how to value her [[Desert\|desert]]<br>Than she to scant her duty. |
| LEAR | Say, how is that? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I cannot think my sister in the least<br>Would fail her obligation. If, sir, perchance<br>She have restrained the riots of your followers,<br>’Tis on such ground and to such wholesome end<br>As clears her from all blame. |
| LEAR | My curses on her. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, you are old.<br>[[Nature]] in you stands on the very verge<br>Of his confine. You should be ruled and led<br>By some discretion that discerns your state<br>Better than you yourself. Therefore I pray you<br>That to our sister you do make return;<br>Say you have wronged her. |
| LEAR | Ask her [[Forgiveness\|forgiveness]]?<br>Do you but mark how this becomes the [[house]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Kneehng ‘Dear daughter, I confess that I am old. |
| LEAR | Age is unnecessary. On my knees I beg<br>That you’ll vouchsafe me raiment, bed, and food.’ |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, no more. These are unsightly tricks.<br>Return you to my sister. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR rising |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Never, [[Regan]].<br>She hath abated me of half my train,<br>Looked black upon me, struck me with her tongue<br>Most serpent-like upon the very heart.<br>All the stored vengeances of heaven fall<br>On her ingrateful top! Strike her young bones,<br>You taking airs, with lameness! |
| CORNWALL | Fie, sir, fie. |
| LEAR | You nimble lightnings, dart your blinding flames<br>Into her scornful eyes. Infect her [[Beauty\|beauty]],<br>You fen-sucked fogs drawn by the pow’rful sun<br>To fall and blister. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O, the blest gods!<br>So will you wish on me when the rash mood is on. |
| LEAR | No, [[Regan]]. Thou shalt never have my curse.<br>Thy tender-hafted [[Nature\|nature]] shall not give<br>Thee o‘er to harshness. Her eyes are fierce, but thine<br>Do comfort and not burn. ’Tis not in thee<br>To grudge my pleasures, to cut off my train,<br>To bandy hasty words, to scant my sizes,<br>And, in conclusion, to oppose the bolt<br>Against my coming in. Thou better know‘st<br>The offices of [[Nature\|nature]], bond of [[Childhood\|childhood]],<br>Effects of courtesy, dues of gratitude.<br>Thy half o’th’ kingdom hast thou not forgot,<br>Wherein I thee endowed. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, to th’ purpose. |
| LEAR | Who put my man i’th’ stocks? |
| *(didascalia)* | Tucket within |
| CORNWALL | What trumpet’s that? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I know’t, my sister’s. This approves her letter<br>That she would soon be here. (To Oswald) Is your lady<br>come? |
| LEAR | This is a slave whose easy-borrowed [[Pride\|pride]]<br>Dwells in the sickly grace of her a follows.<br>(To Oswald) Out, varlet, from my sight! |
| CORNWALL | What means your grace? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Goneril]] |
| LEAR | Who stocked my servant? [[Regan]], I have good [[Hope\|hope]]<br>Thou didst not know on’t. Who comes here? O heavens,<br>If you do [[Love\|love]] old men, if your sweet sway<br>Allow obedience, if you yourselves are old,<br>Make it your cause! Send down and take my part.<br>(To [[Goneril]]) Art not ashamed to look upon this beard?<br>O [[Regan]], will you take her by the hand? |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why not by th’ hand, sir? How have I offended?<br>All’s not offence that indiscretion finds<br>And dotage terms so. |
| LEAR | O sides, you are too tough!<br>Will you yet hold?—How came my man i’th’ stocks? |
| CORNWALL | I set him there, sir; but his own disorders<br>Deserved much less advancement. |
| LEAR | You? Did you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, father, being weak, seem so.<br>If till the expiration of your month<br>You will return and sojourn with my sister,<br>Dismissing half your train, come then to me.<br>I am now from [[Home\|home]], and out of that provision<br>Which shall be needful for your entertainment. |
| LEAR | Return to her, and fifty men dismissed?<br>No, rather I abjure all roofs, and choose<br>To be a comrade with the wolf and owl,<br>To wage against the enmity o’th’ air<br>Necessity’s sharp pinch. Return with her?<br>Why, the hot-blooded France, that dowerless took<br>Our youngest born—I could as [[Well\|well]] be brought<br>To knee his throne and, squire-like, pension beg<br>To keep base life afoot. Return with her?<br>Persuade me rather to be slave and sumpter<br>To this detested groom. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | At your choice, sir. |
| LEAR | I prithee, daughter, do not make me mad.<br>I will not trouble thee, my [[Child\|child]]. Farewell.<br>We’ll no more meet, no more see one another.<br>But yet thou art my flesh, my [[Blood\|blood]], my daughter—<br>Or rather a disease that’s in my flesh,<br>Which I must needs call mine. Thou art a boil,<br>A plague-sore or embossed carbuncle<br>In my corrupted [[Blood\|blood]]. But I’ll not chide thee.<br>Let shame come when it will, I do not call it.<br>I do not bid the thunder-bearer shoot,<br>Nor tell tales of thee to high-judging Jove.<br>Mend when thou canst; be better at thy leisure.<br>I can be patient, I can stay with [[Regan]],<br>I and my hundred knights. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Not altogether so.<br>I looked not for you yet, nor am provided<br>For your fit welcome. Give ear, sir, to my sister;<br>For those that mingle reason with your passion<br>Must be content to think you old, and so—<br>But she knows what she does. |
| LEAR | Is this [[Well\|well]] spoken? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I dare avouch it, sir. What, fifty followers?<br>Is it not [[Well\|well]]? What should you need of more,<br>Yea, or so many, sith that both charge and danger<br>Speak ‘gainst so great a number? How in one [[house]]<br>Should many people under two commands<br>Hold amity? ’Tis hard, almost impossible. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why might not you, my lord, receive attendance<br>From those that she calls servants, or from mine? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why not, my lord? If then they chanced to slack ye,<br>We could control them. If you will come to me—<br>For now I spy a danger—I entreat you<br>To bring but five-and-twenty; to no more<br>Will I give place or notice. |
| LEAR | I gave you all. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And in good [[Time\|time]] you gave it. |
| LEAR | Made you my guardians, my depositaries,<br>But kept a reservation to be followed<br>With such a number. What, must I come to you<br>With five-and-twenty? [[Regan]], said you so? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And speak’t again, my lord. No more with me. |
| LEAR | Those wicked creatures yet do look [[Well\|well]] favoured<br>When others are more wicked. Not being the worst<br>Stands in some rank of praise. (To [[Goneril]]) I’ll go with<br>thee.<br>Thy fifty yet doth [[Double\|double]] five-and-twenty,<br>And thou art twice her [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Hear me, my lord.<br>What need you five-and-twenty, ten, or five,<br>To follow in a [[house]] where twice so many<br>Have a command to tend you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What need one? |
| LEAR | O, reason not the need! Our basest beggars<br>Are in the poorest thing superfluous.<br>Allow not [[Nature\|nature]] more than [[Nature\|nature]] needs,<br>Man’s life is cheap as beast’s. Thou art a lady.<br>If only to go warm were gorgeous,<br>Why, [[Nature\|nature]] needs not what thou, gorgeous, wear’st,<br>Which scarcely keeps thee warm. But for true need—<br>You heavens, give me that patience, patience I need.<br>You see me here, you gods, a poor old man,<br>As full of grief as age, wretched in both.<br>If it be you that stirs these daughters’ hearts<br>Against their father, [[Fool\|fool]] me not so much<br>To bear it tamely. Touch me with noble anger,<br>And let not women’s weapons, [[Water\|water]]-drops,<br>Stain my man’s cheeks. No, you unnatural hags,<br>I will have such revenges on you both<br>That all the world shall—I will do such things—<br>What they are, yet I know not; but they shall be<br>The terrors of the earth. You think I’ll weep.<br>No, I’ll not weep. I have full cause of weeping, |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] and tempest |
| LEAR | But this heart shall break into a hundred thousand<br>flaws<br>Or ere I’ll weep.—O [[Fool]], I shall go mad! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear, [[Fool]], Gentleman, and [[Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | Let us withdraw. ’Twill be a [[Storm\|storm]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | This [[house]] is little. The old man and ’s people<br>Cannot be [[Well\|well]] bestowed. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | ’Tis his own blame;<br>Hath put himself from rest, and must needs taste his folly. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | For his particular I’ll receive him gladly,<br>But not one follower. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | So am I purposed.<br>Where is my lord of [[Gloucester]]? |
| CORNWALL | Followed the old man forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | He is returned. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is in high rage. |
| CORNWALL | Whither is he going? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He calls to [[Horse\|horse]], but will I know not whither. |
| CORNWALL | ’Tis best to give him way. He leads himself. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>My lord, entreat him by no means to stay. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, the [[Night\|night]] comes on, and the high winds<br>Do sorely ruffle. For many miles about<br>There’s scarce a bush. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, to wilful men<br>The injuries that they themselves procure<br>Must be their schoolmasters. Shut up your doors.<br>He is attended with a desperate train,<br>And what they may incense him to, being apt<br>To have his ear abused, wisdom bids [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| CORNWALL | Shut up your doors, my lord. ‘Tis a wild [[Night\|night]].<br>My [[Regan]] counsels [[Well\|well]]. Come out o’th’ [[Storm\|storm]]. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano Edmond il bastardo, e Curan, separatamente EDMOND Dio ti salvi, Curan. |
| CURAN | E voi, signore. Sono stato da vostro padre, e gli ho dato avviso che il duca di Cornwall e [[Regan]] sua duchessa saranno qui da lui questa [[Night\|notte]]. |
| EDMOND | Come mai? CURAN Ma, non lo so. Avete udito le notizie che corrono?—intendo quelle bisbigliate, ché non sono ancora che discorsi che baciano l'orecchio. |
| EDMOND | Io no. Vi prego, quali sono? |
| CURAN | Non avete udito di probabili guerre imminenti tra i duchi di Cornwall e d'Albany? |
| EDMOND | Non una parola. |
| CURAN | Potrete udirne a suo [[Time\|tempo]]. State [[Well\|bene]], signore. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce |
| EDMOND | Il Duca qui stanotte! Tanto meglio, ottimo.<br>Questo s'intesse per forza nel mio disegno. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra [[Edgar]] a una [[Window\|finestra]] in alto |
| EDMOND | Mio padre ha messo guardie per prendere mio fratello,<br>e io ho una cosa d'incerta questione<br>che devo compiere. Prestezza e fortuna, all'opera!—<br>Fratello, una parola, scendi. Fratello, dico. |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Edgar]] scende |
| EDMOND | Mio padre veglia. Oh signore, fuggi questo luogo.<br>Hanno dato informazione di dove ti nascondi.<br>Hai ora il buon vantaggio della [[Night\|notte]].<br>Non hai parlato contro il duca di Cornwall?<br>Sta venendo qui, ora, nella [[Night\|notte]], in fretta,<br>e [[Regan]] con lui. Non hai detto nulla<br>a favore della sua parte contro il duca d'Albany?<br>Ripensaci. |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | Ne son certo, non una parola. |
| EDMOND | Sento venire mio padre. Perdonami.<br>Per astuzia devo trarre la [[Sword\|spada]] contro di te.<br>Sfodera. Fingi di difenderti. Ora, càvatela [[Well\|bene]].<br>(Chiamando) Arrenditi, vieni davanti a mio padre. Luce, olà, qui!<br>(A [[Edgar]]) Fuggi, fratello! (Chiamando) Torce, torce!<br>(A [[Edgar]]) Così, addio. |
| *(didascalia)* | Esce [[Edgar]] |
| EDMOND | Un po' di [[Blood\|sangue]] versato su di me farebbe credere<br>a uno sforzo più accanito.<br>Si ferisce il braccio<br>Ho visto ubriachi<br>far più di questo per gioco. (Chiamando) Padre, padre!<br>Fermatelo, fermatelo! Olà, aiuto! |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il [[Duke of Gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], e servi con torce |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ora, Edmond, dov'è lo [[Villain\|scellerato]]? |
| EDMOND | Qui stava, nel buio, la sua acuta [[Sword\|spada]] sguainata,<br>borbottando malvagi incantesimi, scongiurando la [[Moon\|luna]]<br>d'essergli propizia signora. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ma dov'è? |
| EDMOND | Guardate, signore, sanguino. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Dov'è lo [[Villain\|scellerato]], Edmond? |
| EDMOND | Fuggito per di qua, signore, quando in nessun modo poté— |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Inseguitelo, olà! Andategli dietro. |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono i servi |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | In nessun modo che cosa? |
| EDMOND | Persuadermi all'assassinio di vostra signoria,<br>ma io gli dissi che gli dèi vendicatori<br>contro i parricidi piegano tutto il loro tuono,<br>gli dissi con quale molteplice e forte vincolo<br>il [[Child\|figlio]] è legato al padre. Signore, insomma,<br>vedendo con quanto ribrezzo io m'opponevo<br>al suo snaturato proposito, con feroce slancio<br>con la [[Sword\|spada]] già pronta egli assalta a [[Home\|fondo]]<br>il mio [[Body\|corpo]] indifeso, e mi coglie il braccio;<br>e quando vide i miei spiriti destati al meglio<br>arditi nel diritto della contesa, eccitati allo scontro,<br>o forse atterrito dal rumore che feci,<br>improvvisissimamente fuggì. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Fugga pur lontano,<br>in questa terra non resterà non catturato,<br>e trovato, sia ucciso. Il nobile Duca mio signore,<br>mio degno capo e patrono, viene stanotte.<br>Con la sua autorità proclamerò<br>che chi lo trovi meriterà i nostri ringraziamenti,<br>portando al palo l'assassino codardo;<br>chi lo nasconda, la [[Death\|morte]]. |
| EDMOND | Quando lo dissuadevo dal suo intento<br>e lo trovai deciso a compierlo, con aspre parole<br>minacciai di denunciarlo. Egli rispose:<br>'Tu bastardo senza beni, credi tu<br>che, se io mi levassi contro di te, il riporre<br>in te fiducia, virtù o valore alcuno<br>farebbe prestar fede alle tue parole? No, ciò che negassi—<br>e questo negherei, sì, anche se tu producessi<br>la mia stessa scrittura—io lo volgerei tutto<br>alla tua istigazione, al tuo intrigo, alla tua dannata trama,<br>e dovresti fare del mondo un allocco<br>se non pensasse che i profitti della mia [[Death\|morte]]<br>fossero spiriti assai gravidi e potenti<br>a spingerti a cercarla.' |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Oh strano e indurito [[Villain\|scellerato]]!<br>Negherebbe la sua lettera, ha detto? |
| *(didascalia)* | Squillo all'interno |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Udite, le trombe del Duca. Non so perché venga.<br>Sbarrerò tutti i porti. Lo [[Villain\|scellerato]] non scapperà.<br>Il Duca me lo deve concedere; inoltre, il suo ritratto<br>manderò per ogni dove, sì che tutto il regno<br>ne abbia debita notizia—e delle mie terre,<br>leale e naturale figliolo, troverò il modo<br>di renderti erede. |
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il duca di Cornwall, [[Regan]] e il seguito |
| CORNWALL | Ebbene, mio nobile amico? Da quando sono giunto qui,<br>che posso dire or ora, ho udito strane notizie. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Se è vero, ogni vendetta riesce troppo corta<br>per inseguire l'offensore. Come state, mio signore? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Oh madama, il mio vecchio cuore è spezzato, è spezzato. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Come, il figlioccio di mio padre attentò alla vostra vita?<br>Colui che mio padre tenne a battesimo, il vostro [[Edgar]]? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Oh signora, signora, la vergogna vorrebbe nasconderlo! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Non era compagno dei turbolenti cavalieri<br>che stanno appresso a mio padre? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Non so, madama. È troppo brutto, troppo brutto. |
| EDMOND | Sì, madama, era di quella brigata. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Nessuna meraviglia, dunque, s'era mal disposto.<br>Son loro che l'hanno spinto alla [[Death\|morte]] del vecchio,<br>per avere lo scialo e il saccheggio delle sue rendite.<br>Proprio questa sera, da mia sorella,<br>ne sono stata [[Well\|bene]] informata, e con tali avvertenze<br>che, se vengono ad alloggiare nella mia [[house\|casa]],<br>io non ci sarò. |
| CORNWALL | Né io, te l'assicuro, [[Regan]]. Edmond, sento che avete reso a vostro padre un ufficio da figlio. |
| EDMOND | Era mio dovere, signore. [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] (a Cornwall)<br>Egli svelò la sua trama, e ricevette<br>questa ferita che vedete cercando d'arrestarlo. |
| CORNWALL | È inseguito? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Sì, mio buon signore. |
| CORNWALL | Se è preso, mai più<br>si temerà che faccia del male. Disponete a vostro grado<br>come vi piaccia della mia forza. Quanto a voi, Edmond,<br>la cui virtù e obbedienza in questo istante<br>tanto si raccomandano, sarete dei nostri.<br>Di nature di così profonda fede avremo gran bisogno.<br>Voi per primo prendiamo con noi. |
| EDMOND | Vi servirò, signore,<br>fedelmente, comunque sia. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | (a Cornwall) Per lui ringrazio vostra grazia. |
| CORNWALL | Non sapete perché siamo venuti a visitarvi— |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Così fuori stagione, infilando la [[Night\|notte]] dagli occhi neri—<br>occasioni, nobile [[Gloucester]], di qualche peso,<br>nelle quali dobbiamo far uso del vostro consiglio.<br>Nostro padre ha scritto, e così nostra sorella,<br>di discordie a cui meno che mai credetti conveniente<br>rispondere dalla nostra [[Home\|casa]]. I diversi messaggeri<br>attendono qui d'esser licenziati. Nostro buon vecchio amico,<br>date conforto al vostro petto, e concedete<br>il vostro necessario consiglio ai nostri affari,<br>che chiedono uso immediato. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Vi servo, madama.<br>Le vostre grazie sono le benvenute. |
| *(didascalia)* | Squilli. Escono |

| Speaker | Line |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Earl of [[Kent]], disguised, and Oswald the steward, severally |
| OSWALD | Good dawning to thee, friend. Art of this [[house]]? [[Kent\|KENT]] Ay. |
| OSWALD | Where may we set our horses? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’th’ mire. |
| OSWALD | Prithee, if thou lov’st me, tell me. 5 [[Kent\|KENT]] I [[Love\|love]] thee not. |
| OSWALD | Why then, I care not for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | If I had thee in Lipsbury pinfold I would make thee care for me. |
| OSWALD | Why dost thou use me thus? I know thee not. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fellow, I know thee. |
| OSWALD | What dost thou know me for? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A knave, a rascal, an eater of broken meats, a base, proud, shallow, beggarly, three-suited, hundred-pound, filthy worsted-stocking knave; a lily-livered, action-taking, whoreson, glass-gazing, super-serviceable, finical rogue; one-trunk-inheriting slave; one that wouldst be a bawd in way of good service, and art nothing but the composition of a knave, beggar, coward, pander, and the son and heir of a mongrel bitch, one whom I will beat into clamorous whining if thou deniest the least syllable of thy addition. |
| OSWALD | Why, what a monstrous fellow art thou, thus to rail on one that is neither known of thee nor knows thee! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | What a brazen-faced varlet art thou, to deny thou knowest me! Is it two days since I tripped up thy heels and beat thee before the [[King]]? Draw, you rogue; for though it be [[Night\|night]], yet the [[Moon\|moon]] shines. |
| *(didascalia)* | He draws his [[Sword\|sword]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | I’ll make a sop o’th’ moonshine of you, you whoreson, cullionly barber-monger, draw! |
| OSWALD | Away. I have nothing to do with thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Draw, you rascal. You come with letters against the [[King]], and take [[Vanity]] the puppet’s part against the royalty of her father. Draw, you rogue, or I’ll so carbonado your shanks—draw, you rascal, come your ways! |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, help! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Strike, you slave! Stand, rogue! Stand, you neat slave, strike! 40 |
| OSWALD | Help, ho, murder, murder! |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Edmond the bastard, then the Duke of |
| OSWALD | Cornwall, [[Regan]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and<br><br>servants |
| EDMOND | How now, what’s the matter? Part. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With you, goodman boy. If you please, come, I’ll flesh ye. Come on, young master. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Weapons? Arms? What’s the matter here? |
| CORNWALL | Keep peace, upon your lives. He dies that strikes again.<br>What is the matter? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | The messengers from our sister and the [[King]]. CORNWALL (to [[Kent]] and Oswald) What is your difference?<br>Speak. |
| OSWALD | I am scarce in breath, my lord. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No marvel, you have so bestirred your valour, you cowardly rascal. [[Nature]] disclaims in thee; a tailor made thee. |
| CORNWALL | Thou art a strange fellow—a tailor make a man? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | A tailor, sir. A stone-cutter or a painter could not have made him so ill though they had been but two years o’th’ trade. |
| CORNWALL | Speak yet; how grew your quarrel? |
| OSWALD | This ancient ruffian, sir, whose life I have spared at suit of his grey beard— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Thou whoreson Z, thou unnecessary letter—(to Cornwall) my lord, if you’ll give me leave I will tread this unbolted [[Villain\|villain]] into mortar and daub the wall of a jakes with him. (To Oswald) Spare my grey beard, you wagtail? |
| CORNWALL | Peace, sirrah.<br>You beastly knave, know you no reverence? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Yes, sir, but anger hath a privilege. |
| CORNWALL | Why art thou angry? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | That such a slave as this should wear a [[Sword\|sword]],<br>Who wears no honesty. Such smiling rogues as these,<br>Like rats, oft bite the holy cords a-twain<br>Which are too intrince t’unloose, smooth every<br>passion<br>That in the natures of their lords [[Rebel\|rebel]];<br>Being oil to [[Fire\|fire]], [[Snow\|snow]] to the colder moods,<br>Renege, affirm, and turn their halcyon beaks<br>With every gall and vary of their masters,<br>Knowing naught, like [[dogs]], but following. |
| *(didascalia)* | To Oswald A plague upon your epileptic visage! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Smile you my speeches as I were a [[Fool\|fool]]?<br>Goose, an I had you upon Sarum Plain<br>I’d drive ye cackling [[Home\|home]] to Camelot. |
| CORNWALL | What, art thou mad, old fellow? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] to [[Kent]] How fell you out? Say that. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No contraries hold more antipathy Than I and such a knave. |
| CORNWALL | Why dost thou call him knave?<br>What is his fault? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | His countenance likes me not. |
| CORNWALL | No more perchance does mine, nor his, nor hers. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, ’tis my occupation to be plain:<br>I have seen better faces in my [[Time\|time]]<br>Than stands on any shoulder that I see<br>Before me at this instant. |
| CORNWALL | This is some fellow<br>Who, having been praised for bluntness, doth affect<br>A saucy roughness, and constrains the garb<br>Quite from his [[Nature\|nature]]. He cannot flatter, he;<br>An honest mind and plain, he must speak [[Truth\|truth]].<br>An they will take’t, so; if not, he’s plain.<br>These kind of knaves I know, which in this plainness<br>Harbour more craft and more corrupter ends<br>Than twenty silly-ducking observants<br>That stretch their duties nicely. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, in good [[Faith\|faith]], in sincere verity,<br>Under th‘allowance of your great aspect,<br>Whose influence, like the wreath of radiant [[Fire\|fire]]<br>On flick’ring Phoebus’ front— |
| CORNWALL | What mean’st by this? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | To go out of my dialect, which you discommend so much. I know, sir, I am no flatterer. He that beguiled you in a plain accent was a plain knave, which for my part I will not be, though I should win your displeasure to entreat me to’t. |
| CORNWALL | (to Oswald)<br>What was th’offence you gave him? |
| OSWALD | I never gave him any.<br>It pleased the [[King]] his master very late<br>To strike at me upon his misconstruction,<br>When he, compact, and flattering his displeasure,<br>Tripped me behind; being down, insulted, railed,<br>And put upon him such a deal of man<br>That worthied him, got praises of the [[King]]<br>For him attempting who was [[Self\|self]]-subdued,<br>And in the fleshment of this dread exploit<br>Drew on me here again. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None of these rogues and cowards<br>But [[Ajax]] is their [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| CORNWALL | Fetch forth the stocks! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt some servants |
| CORNWALL | You stubborn, ancient knave, you reverend braggart,<br>We’ll teach you. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sir, I am too old to learn.<br>Call not your stocks for me. I serve the [[King]],<br>On whose employment I was sent to you.<br>You shall do small respect, show too bold malice<br>Against the grace and person of my master,<br>Stocking his messenger. |
| *(didascalia)* | CORNWALL calling |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Fetch forth the stocks!—<br>As I have life and [[Honour\|honour]], there shall he sit till [[Noon\|noon]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Till [[Noon\|noon]]?—till [[Night\|night]], my lord, and all [[Night\|night]] too. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, madam, if I were your father’s dog<br>You should not use me so. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Sir, being his knave, I will.<br>Stocks brought out |
| CORNWALL | This is a fellow of the selfsame colour<br>Our sister speaks of.—Come, bring away the stocks. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Let me beseech your grace not to do so.<br>The [[King]] his master needs must take it ill<br>That he, so slightly valued in his messenger,<br>Should have him thus restrained. |
| CORNWALL | I’ll answer that. |
| *(didascalia)* | Theg put [[Kent]] in the stocks |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | My sister may receive it much more worse<br>To have her gentlemen abused, assaulted. |
| CORNWALL | Come, my good lord, away! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt all but [[Gloucester]] and [[Kent]] |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I am sorry for thee, friend. ’Tis the Duke’s pleasure,<br>Whose disposition, all the world [[Well\|well]] knows,<br>Will not be rubbed nor stopped. I’ll entreat for thee. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Pray do not, sir. I have watched and travelled hard.<br>Some [[Time\|time]] I shall [[Sleep\|sleep]] out; the rest I’ll whistle.<br>A good man’s fortune may grow out at heels.<br>Give you good morrow. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The Duke’s to blame in this; ’twill be ill taken. Exit |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Good [[King]], that must approve the common say:<br>Thou out of heaven’s benediction com’st<br>To the warm sun. |
| *(didascalia)* | He takes out a letter |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Approach, thou beacon to this under globe,<br>That by thy comfortable beams I may<br>Peruse this letter. Nothing almost sees miracles<br>But misery. I know ‘tis from [[Cordelia]],<br>Who hath now fortunately been informed<br>Of my obscured course, and shall find [[Time\|time]]<br>For this enormous state, seeking to give<br>Losses their remedies. All weary and o’erwatched,<br>Take vantage, heavy eyes, not to behold<br>This shameful lodging. Fortune, good [[Night\|night]];<br>Smile once more; turn thy [[Wheel\|wheel]].<br>He sleeps |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Edgar]] |
| [[Edgar\|EDGAR]] | I heard myself proclaimed,<br>And by the happy hollow of a tree<br>Escaped the hunt. No port is free, no place<br>That guard and most unusual vigilance<br>Does not attend my taking. Whiles I may scape<br>I will preserve myself, and am bethought<br>To take the basest and most poorest shape<br>That ever penury in contempt of man<br>Brought near to beast. My face I’ll grime with filth,<br>Blanket my loins, elf all my hairs in knots,<br>And with presented [[Nakedness\|nakedness]] outface<br>The winds and persecutions of the sky.<br>The country gives me proof and precedent<br>Of Bedlam beggars who with roaring voices<br>Strike in their numbed and mortified arms<br>Pins, wooden pricks, nails, sprigs of rosemary,<br>And with this horrible object from low farms,<br>Poor pelting villages, sheep-cotes and mills<br>Sometime with lunatic bans, sometime with prayers<br>Enforce their [[Charity\|charity]]. ‘Poor Tuelygod, Poor Tom.’<br>That’s something yet. [[Edgar]] I nothing am. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]], his [[Fool]], and the First Gentleman |
| LEAR | ’Tis strange that they should so depart from [[Home\|home]]<br>And not send back my messenger. |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN |
| LEAR | As I learned,<br>The [[Night\|night]] before there was no purpose in them<br>Of this remove. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | (waking)<br>Hail to thee, noble master. |
| LEAR | Ha! Mak’st thou this shame thy pastime? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | No, my lord. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Ha, ha, he wears cruel garters! Horses are tied by the heads, [[dogs]] and bears by th’ neck, monkeys by th’ loins, and men by th’ legs. When a man’s overlusty at legs, then he wears wooden nether-stocks. |
| LEAR | (to [[Kent]])<br>What’s he that hath so much thy place mistook To set thee here? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | It is both he and she:<br>Your son and daughter. |
| LEAR | By Jupiter, I swear no. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | By Juno, I swear ay. |
| LEAR | They durst not do’t,<br>They could not, would not do’t. ’Tis worse than<br>murder,<br>To do upon respect such violent outrage.<br>Resolve me with all modest haste which way<br>Thou mightst deserve or they impose this usage,<br>Coming from us. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | My lord, when at their [[Home\|home]]<br>I did commend your highness’ letters to them,<br>Ere I was risen from the place that showed<br>My duty kneeling, came there a reeking post<br>Stewed in his haste, half breathless, painting forth<br>From [[Goneril]], his mistress, salutations,<br>Delivered letters spite of intermission,<br>Which presently they read, on whose contents 210<br>They summoned up their meiny, straight took [[Horse\|horse]],<br>Commanded me to follow and attend<br>The leisure of their answer, gave me cold looks;<br>And meeting here the other messenger,<br>Whose welcome I perceived had poisoned mine—<br>Being the very fellow which of late<br>Displayed so saucily against your highness—<br>Having more man than wit about me, drew.<br>He raised the [[house]] with loud and coward cries.<br>Your son and daughter found this trespass worth<br>The shame which here it suffers. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | [[Winter]]’s not gone yet if the wild geese fly that way. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings Fathers that wear rags |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Do make their children blind,<br>But fathers that bear bags<br>Shall see their children kind.<br>Fortune, that arrant whore,<br>Ne’er turns the key to th’ poor.<br>But for all this thou shalt have as many dolours for<br>thy daughters as thou canst tell in a year. |
| LEAR | O, how this mother swells up toward my heart!<br>Histerica passio down, thou climbing sorrow;<br>Thy element’s below.—Where is this daughter? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | With the Earl, sir, here within. |
| LEAR | Follow me not; stay here. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| *(didascalia)* | FIRST GENTLEMAN (to [[Kent]]) |
| LEAR | Made you no more offence but what you speak of? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | None.<br>How chance the [[King]] comes with so small a number? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | An thou hadst been set i’th’ stocks for that question, thou’dst [[Well\|well]] deserved it. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Why, [[Fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | We’ll set thee to school to an ant, to teach thee there’s no labouring i’th’ [[Winter\|winter]]. All that follow their noses are led by their eyes but blind men, and there’s not a nose among twenty but can smell him that’s stinking. Let go thy hold when a great [[Wheel\|wheel]] runs down a [[Hill\|hill]], lest it break thy neck with following; but the great one that goes upward, let him draw thee after. When a wise man gives thee better counsel, give me mine again. I would have none but knaves follow it, since a [[Fool\|fool]] gives it. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sings |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | That sir which serves and seeks for gain<br>And follows but for form,<br>Will pack when it begin to rain,<br>And leave thee in the [[Storm\|storm]].<br>But I will tarry, the [[Fool\|fool]] will stay,<br>And let the wise man fly.<br>The knave turns [[Fool\|fool]] that runs away,<br>The [[Fool\|fool]] no knave, pardie. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Where learned you this, [[Fool]]? |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Not i’th’ stocks, [[Fool\|fool]]. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[King Lear]] and the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| LEAR | Deny to speak with me? They are sick, they are weary,<br>They have travelled all the [[Night\|night]]?—mere fetches,<br>The images of revolt and flying off.<br>Fetch me a better answer. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | My dear lord,<br>You know the fiery quality of the Duke,<br>How unremovable and fixed he is<br>In his own course. |
| LEAR | Vengeance, plague, [[Death\|death]], confusion!<br>‘Fiery’? What ‘quality’? Why, [[Gloucester]], [[Gloucester]],<br>I’d speak with the Duke of Cornwall and his wife. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | [[Well]], my good lord, I have informed them so. |
| LEAR | ‘Informed them’? Dost thou understand me, man? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Ay, my good lord. |
| LEAR | The [[King]] would speak with Cornwall; the dear father<br>Would with his daughter speak, commands, tends<br>service.<br>Are they ‘informed’ of this? My breath and [[Blood\|blood]]—<br>‘Fiery’? The ‘fiery’ Duke—tell the hot Duke that—<br>No, but not yet. Maybe he is not [[Well\|well]].<br>Infirmity doth still neglect all office<br>Whereto our health is bound. We are not ourselves<br>When [[Nature\|nature]], being oppressed, commands the mind<br>To suffer with the [[Body\|body]]. I’ll forbear,<br>And am fallen out with my more headier will,<br>To take the indisposed and sickly fit<br>For the sound man.—[[Death]] on my state, wherefore<br>Should he sit here? This act persuades me<br>That this remotion of the Duke and her<br>Is practice only. Give me my servant forth.<br>Go tell the Duke and’s wife I’d speak with them,<br>Now, presently. Bid them come forth and hear me,<br>Or at their chamber door I’ll beat the drum<br>Till it cry [[Sleep\|sleep]] to [[Death\|death]]. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | I would have all [[Well\|well]] betwixt you. |
| *(didascalia)* | Exit |
| LEAR | O me, my heart! My rising heart! But down. |
| [[Fool\|FOOL]] | Cry to it, nuncle, as the cockney did to the eels when she put ‘em i’th’ paste alive. She knapped ‘em o’th’ coxcombs with a stick, and cried ‘Down, wantons, down!’ ’Twas her brother that, in pure kindness to his [[Horse\|horse]], buttered his hay. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the Duke of Cornwall, [[Regan]], the [[Duke of Gloucester]], and servants |
| LEAR | Good morrow to you both. |
| CORNWALL | Hail to your grace.<br>[[Kent]] here set at liberty |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I am glad to see your highness. |
| LEAR | [[Regan]], I think you are. I know what reason<br>I have to think so. If thou shouldst not be glad<br>I would [[divorce]] me from thy mother’s shrine,<br>Sepulchring an adultress. (To [[Kent]]) O, are you free? |
| *(didascalia)* | Some other [[Time\|time]] for that. Exit [[Kent]] |
| LEAR | Beloved [[Regan]],<br>Thy sister’s naught. O, [[Regan]], she hath tied<br>Sharp-toothed unkindness like a vulture here.<br>I can scarce speak to thee. Thou’lt not believe<br>With how depraved a quality-O, [[Regan]]! |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, sir, take patience. I have [[Hope\|hope]]<br>You less know how to value her [[Desert\|desert]]<br>Than she to scant her duty. |
| LEAR | Say, how is that? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I cannot think my sister in the least<br>Would fail her obligation. If, sir, perchance<br>She have restrained the riots of your followers,<br>’Tis on such ground and to such wholesome end<br>As clears her from all blame. |
| LEAR | My curses on her. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, you are old.<br>[[Nature]] in you stands on the very verge<br>Of his confine. You should be ruled and led<br>By some discretion that discerns your state<br>Better than you yourself. Therefore I pray you<br>That to our sister you do make return;<br>Say you have wronged her. |
| LEAR | Ask her [[Forgiveness\|forgiveness]]?<br>Do you but mark how this becomes the [[house]]? |
| *(didascalia)* | Kneehng ‘Dear daughter, I confess that I am old. |
| LEAR | Age is unnecessary. On my knees I beg<br>That you’ll vouchsafe me raiment, bed, and food.’ |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, no more. These are unsightly tricks.<br>Return you to my sister. |
| *(didascalia)* | LEAR rising |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Never, [[Regan]].<br>She hath abated me of half my train,<br>Looked black upon me, struck me with her tongue<br>Most serpent-like upon the very heart.<br>All the stored vengeances of heaven fall<br>On her ingrateful top! Strike her young bones,<br>You taking airs, with lameness! |
| CORNWALL | Fie, sir, fie. |
| LEAR | You nimble lightnings, dart your blinding flames<br>Into her scornful eyes. Infect her [[Beauty\|beauty]],<br>You fen-sucked fogs drawn by the pow’rful sun<br>To fall and blister. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O, the blest gods!<br>So will you wish on me when the rash mood is on. |
| LEAR | No, [[Regan]]. Thou shalt never have my curse.<br>Thy tender-hafted [[Nature\|nature]] shall not give<br>Thee o‘er to harshness. Her eyes are fierce, but thine<br>Do comfort and not burn. ’Tis not in thee<br>To grudge my pleasures, to cut off my train,<br>To bandy hasty words, to scant my sizes,<br>And, in conclusion, to oppose the bolt<br>Against my coming in. Thou better know‘st<br>The offices of [[Nature\|nature]], bond of [[Childhood\|childhood]],<br>Effects of courtesy, dues of gratitude.<br>Thy half o’th’ kingdom hast thou not forgot,<br>Wherein I thee endowed. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Good sir, to th’ purpose. |
| LEAR | Who put my man i’th’ stocks? |
| *(didascalia)* | Tucket within |
| CORNWALL | What trumpet’s that? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter Oswald the steward |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I know’t, my sister’s. This approves her letter<br>That she would soon be here. (To Oswald) Is your lady<br>come? |
| LEAR | This is a slave whose easy-borrowed [[Pride\|pride]]<br>Dwells in the sickly grace of her a follows.<br>(To Oswald) Out, varlet, from my sight! |
| CORNWALL | What means your grace? |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter [[Goneril]] |
| LEAR | Who stocked my servant? [[Regan]], I have good [[Hope\|hope]]<br>Thou didst not know on’t. Who comes here? O heavens,<br>If you do [[Love\|love]] old men, if your sweet sway<br>Allow obedience, if you yourselves are old,<br>Make it your cause! Send down and take my part.<br>(To [[Goneril]]) Art not ashamed to look upon this beard?<br>O [[Regan]], will you take her by the hand? |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why not by th’ hand, sir? How have I offended?<br>All’s not offence that indiscretion finds<br>And dotage terms so. |
| LEAR | O sides, you are too tough!<br>Will you yet hold?—How came my man i’th’ stocks? |
| CORNWALL | I set him there, sir; but his own disorders<br>Deserved much less advancement. |
| LEAR | You? Did you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I pray you, father, being weak, seem so.<br>If till the expiration of your month<br>You will return and sojourn with my sister,<br>Dismissing half your train, come then to me.<br>I am now from [[Home\|home]], and out of that provision<br>Which shall be needful for your entertainment. |
| LEAR | Return to her, and fifty men dismissed?<br>No, rather I abjure all roofs, and choose<br>To be a comrade with the wolf and owl,<br>To wage against the enmity o’th’ air<br>Necessity’s sharp pinch. Return with her?<br>Why, the hot-blooded France, that dowerless took<br>Our youngest born—I could as [[Well\|well]] be brought<br>To knee his throne and, squire-like, pension beg<br>To keep base life afoot. Return with her?<br>Persuade me rather to be slave and sumpter<br>To this detested groom. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | At your choice, sir. |
| LEAR | I prithee, daughter, do not make me mad.<br>I will not trouble thee, my [[Child\|child]]. Farewell.<br>We’ll no more meet, no more see one another.<br>But yet thou art my flesh, my [[Blood\|blood]], my daughter—<br>Or rather a disease that’s in my flesh,<br>Which I must needs call mine. Thou art a boil,<br>A plague-sore or embossed carbuncle<br>In my corrupted [[Blood\|blood]]. But I’ll not chide thee.<br>Let shame come when it will, I do not call it.<br>I do not bid the thunder-bearer shoot,<br>Nor tell tales of thee to high-judging Jove.<br>Mend when thou canst; be better at thy leisure.<br>I can be patient, I can stay with [[Regan]],<br>I and my hundred knights. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Not altogether so.<br>I looked not for you yet, nor am provided<br>For your fit welcome. Give ear, sir, to my sister;<br>For those that mingle reason with your passion<br>Must be content to think you old, and so—<br>But she knows what she does. |
| LEAR | Is this [[Well\|well]] spoken? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I dare avouch it, sir. What, fifty followers?<br>Is it not [[Well\|well]]? What should you need of more,<br>Yea, or so many, sith that both charge and danger<br>Speak ‘gainst so great a number? How in one [[house]]<br>Should many people under two commands<br>Hold amity? ’Tis hard, almost impossible. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Why might not you, my lord, receive attendance<br>From those that she calls servants, or from mine? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Why not, my lord? If then they chanced to slack ye,<br>We could control them. If you will come to me—<br>For now I spy a danger—I entreat you<br>To bring but five-and-twenty; to no more<br>Will I give place or notice. |
| LEAR | I gave you all. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And in good [[Time\|time]] you gave it. |
| LEAR | Made you my guardians, my depositaries,<br>But kept a reservation to be followed<br>With such a number. What, must I come to you<br>With five-and-twenty? [[Regan]], said you so? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | And speak’t again, my lord. No more with me. |
| LEAR | Those wicked creatures yet do look [[Well\|well]] favoured<br>When others are more wicked. Not being the worst<br>Stands in some rank of praise. (To [[Goneril]]) I’ll go with<br>thee.<br>Thy fifty yet doth [[Double\|double]] five-and-twenty,<br>And thou art twice her [[Love\|love]]. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | Hear me, my lord.<br>What need you five-and-twenty, ten, or five,<br>To follow in a [[house]] where twice so many<br>Have a command to tend you? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | What need one? |
| LEAR | O, reason not the need! Our basest beggars<br>Are in the poorest thing superfluous.<br>Allow not [[Nature\|nature]] more than [[Nature\|nature]] needs,<br>Man’s life is cheap as beast’s. Thou art a lady.<br>If only to go warm were gorgeous,<br>Why, [[Nature\|nature]] needs not what thou, gorgeous, wear’st,<br>Which scarcely keeps thee warm. But for true need—<br>You heavens, give me that patience, patience I need.<br>You see me here, you gods, a poor old man,<br>As full of grief as age, wretched in both.<br>If it be you that stirs these daughters’ hearts<br>Against their father, [[Fool\|fool]] me not so much<br>To bear it tamely. Touch me with noble anger,<br>And let not women’s weapons, [[Water\|water]]-drops,<br>Stain my man’s cheeks. No, you unnatural hags,<br>I will have such revenges on you both<br>That all the world shall—I will do such things—<br>What they are, yet I know not; but they shall be<br>The terrors of the earth. You think I’ll weep.<br>No, I’ll not weep. I have full cause of weeping, |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Storm]] and tempest |
| LEAR | But this heart shall break into a hundred thousand<br>flaws<br>Or ere I’ll weep.—O [[Fool]], I shall go mad! |
| *(didascalia)* | Exeunt Lear, [[Fool]], Gentleman, and [[Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | Let us withdraw. ’Twill be a [[Storm\|storm]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | This [[house]] is little. The old man and ’s people<br>Cannot be [[Well\|well]] bestowed. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | ’Tis his own blame;<br>Hath put himself from rest, and must needs taste his folly. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | For his particular I’ll receive him gladly,<br>But not one follower. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | So am I purposed.<br>Where is my lord of [[Gloucester]]? |
| CORNWALL | Followed the old man forth. |
| *(didascalia)* | Enter the [[Duke of Gloucester]] |
| CORNWALL | He is returned. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | The [[King]] is in high rage. |
| CORNWALL | Whither is he going? |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | He calls to [[Horse\|horse]], but will I know not whither. |
| CORNWALL | ’Tis best to give him way. He leads himself. |
| [[Goneril\|GONERIL]] | (to [[Gloucester]])<br>My lord, entreat him by no means to stay. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Alack, the [[Night\|night]] comes on, and the high winds<br>Do sorely ruffle. For many miles about<br>There’s scarce a bush. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | O sir, to wilful men<br>The injuries that they themselves procure<br>Must be their schoolmasters. Shut up your doors.<br>He is attended with a desperate train,<br>And what they may incense him to, being apt<br>To have his ear abused, wisdom bids [[Fear\|fear]]. |
| CORNWALL | Shut up your doors, my lord. ‘Tis a wild [[Night\|night]].<br>My [[Regan]] counsels [[Well\|well]]. Come out o’th’ [[Storm\|storm]]. Exeunt |


<span class="qlang-split" data-lang="it"></span>

| Chi parla | Battuta |
|---|---|
| *(didascalia)* | Entrano il conte di [[Kent]], travestito, e Oswald il maggiordomo, separatamente |
| OSWALD | Buon'alba a te, amico. Sei di questa [[house\|casa]]? [[Kent\|KENT]] Sì. |
| OSWALD | Dove possiamo mettere i nostri cavalli? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Nel fango. |
| OSWALD | Ti prego, se mi vuoi bene, dimmelo. 5 [[Kent\|KENT]] Non ti [[Love\|amo]]. |
| OSWALD | E allora non m'importa nulla di te. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Se t'avessi nel recinto di Lipsbury ti farei importare di me. |
| OSWALD | Perché mi tratti così? Non ti conosco. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Amico, io ti conosco. |
| OSWALD | Per che cosa mi conosci? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Per un furfante, un mascalzone, un mangiatore d'avanzi; un vile, superbo, vacuo, pezzente furfante da tre vestiti, da cento sterline, dalle sudicie calze di lana; un ribaldo dal fegato di giglio, attaccalite, figlio di puttana, specchiettoso, servizievole all'eccesso, schizzinoso; uno schiavo che eredita un solo baule; uno che si farebbe ruffiano per far buon servizio, e non è altro che il composto di furfante, pezzente, codardo, mezzano, e il figlio ed erede d'una cagna bastarda; uno che bastonerò fino a farlo guaire clamorosamente, se negherà una sola sillaba dei suoi titoli. |
| OSWALD | Ma che mostruoso individuo sei tu, per inveire così contro uno che non ti conosce né è conosciuto da te! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Che sfacciato ribaldo sei tu, a negare di conoscermi! Son forse due giorni che t'ho fatto lo sgambetto e t'ho bastonato davanti al [[King\|Re]]? Sfodera, canaglia; ché, benché sia [[Night\|notte]], pure la [[Moon\|luna]] splende. |
| *(didascalia)* | Sguaina la [[Sword\|spada]] |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Farò di te una zuppa nel chiaro di luna, figlio di puttana, sozzo frequentatore di barbieri, sfodera! |
| OSWALD | Va' via. Non ho nulla a che fare con te. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sfodera, mascalzone. Vieni con lettere contro il [[King\|Re]], e prendi le parti della marionetta [[Vanity\|Vanità]] contro la maestà di suo padre. Sfodera, canaglia, o ti carbonizzerò talmente gli stinchi—sfodera, mascalzone, fatti avanti! |
| OSWALD | Aiuto, olà, all'assassino, aiuto! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Colpisci, servo! Fèrmati, canaglia! Fèrmati, elegante servo, colpisci! 40 |
| OSWALD | Aiuto, olà, all'assassino, all'assassino! |
| *(didascalia)* | Entra Edmond il bastardo, poi il duca di |
| OSWALD | Cornwall, [[Regan]], il [[Duke of Gloucester\|duca di Gloucester]], e<br><br>servi |
| EDMOND | Ebbene, che succede? Separatevi. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Con voi, bel ragazzo. Se vi piace, venite, vi sbudello. Avanti, giovane padrone. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Armi? Spade? Che succede qui? |
| CORNWALL | Mantenete la pace, sulla vostra vita. Muore chi colpisce ancora.<br>Che succede? |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | I messaggeri di nostra sorella e del [[King\|Re]]. CORNWALL (a [[Kent]] e Oswald) Qual è la vostra lite?<br>Parlate. |
| OSWALD | Ho appena fiato, mio signore. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Nessuna meraviglia, avete tanto scomodato il vostro valore, mascalzone codardo. La [[Nature\|natura]] ti rinnega; un sarto t'ha fatto. |
| CORNWALL | Sei uno strano individuo—un sarto fare un uomo? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Un sarto, signore. Uno scalpellino o un pittore non avrebbero potuto farlo così male, foss'anche stato solo due anni nel mestiere. |
| CORNWALL | Parla dunque; come nacque la vostra lite? |
| OSWALD | Questo vecchio ribaldo, signore, di cui ho risparmiato la vita per riguardo alla sua barba grigia— |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Tu, figlio di puttana d'una Z, lettera inutile—(a Cornwall) mio signore, se mi date licenza pesterò questo [[Villain\|scellerato]] non stacciato fino a farne malta e ne intonacherò il muro d'una latrina. (A Oswald) Risparmiare la mia barba grigia, tu, cutrettola? |
| CORNWALL | Pace, messere.<br>Bestiale furfante, non conoscete riverenza? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sì, signore, ma la collera ha un suo privilegio. |
| CORNWALL | Perché sei in collera? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Che un servo come questo porti una [[Sword\|spada]],<br>lui che non porta onestà. Simili sorridenti canaglie,<br>come topi, spesso mordono in due le sacre corde<br>troppo intricate da sciogliere, lisciano ogni<br>passione<br>che nelle nature dei loro signori si [[Rebel\|ribella]];<br>essendo olio al [[Fire\|fuoco]], [[Snow\|neve]] agli umori più freddi,<br>negano, affermano, e volgono i loro becchi d'alcione<br>a ogni bile e mutamento dei loro padroni,<br>nulla sapendo, come [[dogs\|cani]], se non seguire. |
| *(didascalia)* | A Oswald Un accidente al tuo epilettico muso! |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Sorridete ai miei discorsi come s'io fossi un [[Fool\|buffone]]?<br>Oca, se t'avessi sulla piana di Sarum<br>ti caccerei schiamazzando a [[Home\|casa]], fino a Camelot. |
| CORNWALL | Come, sei pazzo, vecchio? |
| *(didascalia)* | [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] a [[Kent]] Come veniste ai ferri? Dite questo. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Nessun contrario ha più antipatia di me e d'un simile furfante. |
| CORNWALL | Perché lo chiami furfante?<br>Qual è la sua colpa? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Il suo volto non mi piace. |
| CORNWALL | Forse non ti piace nemmeno il mio, né il suo, né quello di lei. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Signore, è mio mestiere essere schietto:<br>ho visto facce migliori al mio [[Time\|tempo]]<br>di quante ne stiano su qualunque spalla ch'io veda<br>davanti a me in questo istante. |
| CORNWALL | Costui è uno di quelli<br>che, essendo stati lodati per la rudezza, affettano<br>una sfacciata ruvidezza, e forzano il proprio contegno<br>del tutto fuori dalla loro [[Nature\|natura]]. Lui non sa adulare, lui;<br>mente onesta e schietta, deve dire la [[Truth\|verità]].<br>Se l'accettano, bene; se no, lui è schietto.<br>Conosco questa razza di furfanti, che in questa schiettezza<br>nascondono più astuzia e fini più corrotti<br>di venti sciocchi inchinatori ossequiosi<br>che tendono meticolosamente i loro doveri. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Signore, in buona [[Faith\|fede]], in sincera verità,<br>sotto il permesso del vostro grande aspetto,<br>il cui influsso, come la corona di raggiante [[Fire\|fuoco]]<br>sulla fronte del tremulo Febo— |
| CORNWALL | Che intendi con questo? |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Uscire dal mio dialetto, che voi tanto disapprovate. So, signore, di non essere un adulatore. Colui che v'ingannò con accento schietto era uno schietto furfante, il che, per parte mia, non voglio essere, quand'anche dovessi guadagnarmi il vostro dispiacere per esserne pregato. |
| CORNWALL | (a Oswald)<br>Qual è l'offesa che gli faceste? |
| OSWALD | Non gliene feci mai alcuna.<br>Piacque al [[King\|Re]] suo padrone, non è molto,<br>di colpirmi per un suo fraintendimento,<br>quando costui, d'accordo con lui e lusingandone il dispetto,<br>mi sgambettò alle spalle; e caduto io, m'insultò, inveì,<br>e si diede tanta aria d'uomo<br>che se ne fece merito, ottenne le lodi del [[King\|Re]]<br>per aver assalito chi da [[Self\|sé]] s'era arreso,<br>e nell'ebbrezza di questa tremenda impresa<br>di nuovo qui ha sfoderato contro di me. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Non c'è canaglia o codardo tra costoro<br>di cui [[Ajax]] non sia lo [[Fool\|zimbello]]. |
| CORNWALL | Portate qua i ceppi! |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono alcuni servi |
| CORNWALL | Voi, ostinato vecchio furfante, venerando spaccone,<br>v'insegneremo noi. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Signore, son troppo vecchio per imparare.<br>Non chiamate i vostri ceppi per me. Io servo il [[King\|Re]],<br>per incarico del quale fui mandato a voi.<br>Mostrerete poco rispetto, e troppo ardita malizia<br>contro la grazia e la persona del mio padrone,<br>mettendo ai ceppi il suo messaggero. |
| *(didascalia)* | CORNWALL chiamando |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Portate qua i ceppi!—<br>Com'è vero che ho vita e [[Honour\|onore]], là siederà fino a [[Noon\|mezzogiorno]]. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Fino a [[Noon\|mezzogiorno]]?—fino a [[Night\|notte]], mio signore, e per tutta la [[Night\|notte]]. |
| [[Kent\|KENT]] | Ma, madama, se fossi il cane di vostro padre<br>non mi trattereste così. |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Signore, essendo il suo furfante, lo farò.<br>Vengono portati i ceppi |
| CORNWALL | Costui è un tale dello stesso identico colore<br>di cui parla nostra sorella.—Su, portate qua i ceppi. |
| [[Gloucester\|GLOUCESTER]] | Lasciate ch'io supplichi vostra grazia di non far così.<br>Il [[King\|Re]] suo padrone non potrà che prenderla male<br>che egli, così poco stimato nel suo messaggero,<br>lo veda a questo modo costretto. |
| CORNWALL | Ne risponderò io. |
| *(didascalia)* | Theg mettono [[Kent]] nei ceppi |
| [[Regan\|REGAN]] | Mia sorella potrebbe prenderla assai peggio<br>a vedere i suoi gentiluomini oltraggiati, assaliti. |
| CORNWALL | Venite, mio buon signore, via! |
| *(didascalia)* | Escono tutti tranne [[Gloucester]] e [[Kent]] |
